In the supplied text this head is merged into the Pada block after a stray signature line ("[5] Vol. 12"), and no page marker was supplied; the page break is inserted in kn.md and pages.json.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಾಯುಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sat inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> उरुव्यचसा प्रभूतव्यजनवत्यावतिनिस्तीर्णे महिनी अत एव महत्या असश्चशासज्यमाने । परस्परवियुक्ते इत्यर्थः । पिता पालयित्री द्यौः माता निर्मात्रि
> पृथिवी चेत्युभे भुवनानि भूतजातानि रक्षतः । पालयतः । द्यौष्टितः पृथिवी मातः ।
> ऋ. ६-५१-५ । इत्यादिश्रुतेर्मातापितृरूपत्वं । किंच सुधृष्टमे अतिशयेन दृष्टे ॥
> छांदसस्तकारलोपः ॥ प्रगल्वे रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ वपुष्ये न वपुषो हिते इव ।
> प्राणिनां पितराविव शरीररक्षके इत्यर्थः । तदेवोपपादयति । यद्यस्मात्सीं सर्वतः
> पिता पितृस्थानीया द्यौ रूपैर्निरूपणसाधनैः प्रशस्यॆः प्रकाशैर्निरूप्यमाणैर्वृष्टाःदिभिर्वाभ्यवासयत् अभिवासयति अधितिष्ठति तिस्मात्पिता माता च भुवनानि रक्षतः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): vast, mighty, unattached Heaven and Earth, as father and mother, protect the worlds; the father-sky covers all with radiant forms and rain.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರಸಿದ್ಧಾರ್ಥ") and the Pratipadārtha sat inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted. Likewise the Bhāvārtha label ("ಭಾಷಾರ್ಧ") and text.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उरुव्यचसा (*uruvyacasā*)—those spread out exceedingly widely; महिनी (*mahinī*)—therefore great; आसश्चता (*āsaścatā*) *[so printed]*—those that do not cling to one another; पिता (*pitā*)—the protecting presiding deity of the heaven-world; माता च (*mātā ca*)—and the Earth-goddess, the maker; भुवनानि (*bhuvanāni*)—all the worlds; रक्षतः (*rakṣataḥ*)—protect well. सुदृष्टमे (*sudṛṣṭame*) *[so printed]*—of firm resolve; रोदसी (*rodasī*)—Heaven and Earth; वपुष्ये न (*vapuṣye na*)—are as if solely for the welfare of all embodied beings. (Of the two,) the atmosphere, which is in the place of the father; सीं (*sīṃ*)—all around; रूपैः (*rūpaiḥ*)—by (its excellent) illuminations; अभ्यवासयत् (*abhyavāsayat*)—has covered (has dwelt all around).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The protecting presiding deity of the heaven-world and the Earth-goddess, the maker, who are spread out exceedingly widely, therefore great, and do not cling to one another, protect all the worlds well. Heaven and Earth, of firm resolve, are as if solely for the welfare of all embodied beings. Of the two, the atmosphere, which is in the place of the father, has covered, that is, pervaded, all around with its excellent illuminations.

**Printed English translation**

Wide-spreading, vast, unconnected, the father and mother ( of all beings), they two preserve the worlds. Resolute, as if ( for the good ) of embodied (beings), are Heaven and Earth, and the father has invested every thing with (visible) forms.

### Printed p. 67

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೩.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 67 (as supplied).*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

उरुव्यचसा (*uruvyacasā*)—उरु विस्तीर्णं व्यचः व्यापनं ययोः (*uru vistīrṇaṃ vyacaḥ vyāpanaṃ yayoḥ*) "those whose pervasion is exceedingly wide", or "those whose extent is enough to pervade everything". This word is used mostly as an epithet of Heaven and Earth and of Aditi. Since the identity of Aditi with the Earth is stated in many places,

भगो विभक्ता शवसावसा गमदुरुव्यचा अदितिः श्रोतु मे हवं । (*bhago vibhaktā śavasāvasā gamaduruvyacā aditiḥ śrotu me havaṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-46-6)

in such ṛks the breadth of Aditi, full of greatness, is itself a description of the Earth. Like उरुव्यचसा (*uruvyacasā*), the epithet वरीमता (*varīmatā*) also indicates the extent of Heaven and Earth. The sentence in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa that nothing in the universe is spread out more widely than Heaven and Earth makes this truth clear. Since the original of the Kāṇva recension is not available at present, its English translation is quoted here:

"The great Heaven and Earth" for additional superfluous (अतिरिक्त (*atirikta*)) is that (गर्भ (*garbha*)), beyond these two, Heaven and Earth, nothing whatever remains (or nothing surpasses them, अतिरिच्यते (*atiricyate*)). (S. B. E. Vol. XXVI. P. 336, f. n.)

It is stated that nothing in the world has an extension beyond Earth and Atmosphere. Likewise the sentence पित माता च भुवनानि रक्षतः (*pita mātā ca bhuvanāni rakṣataḥ*) *[so printed]* tells that the whole universe too relies on Earth and Atmosphere for its maintenance and support.

स्वाहोरोरंतरिक्षादारभते स्वाहा द्यावापृथिवीभ्यामिति तदाभ्यां द्यावापृथि- (*svāhororaṃtarikṣādārabhate svāhā dyāvāpṛthivībhyāmiti tadābhyāṃ dyāvāpṛthi-*)
वीभ्यामारभते ययोरिदं सर्वमधि ॥ (*vībhyāmārabhate yayoridaṃ sarvamadhi ‖*)

(Śa. Brā. 3-1-3-26)

नमो द्यावापृथिवीभ्यामिति तदाभ्यां द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां निह्नुवते ययो- (*namo dyāvāpṛthivībhyāmiti tadābhyāṃ dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ nihnuvate yayo-*)
रिदं सर्वमधि । (*ridaṃ sarvamadhi |*)

(Śa. Brā. 3-4-3-21)

द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां त्वा परिददामीति तदेनमाभ्यां द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां परिददाति (*dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ tvā paridadāmīti tadenamābhyāṃ dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ paridadāti*)
ययोरिदं सर्वमधि । (*yayoridaṃ sarvamadhi |*)

(Śa. Brā. 11-5-4-4)

### Printed p. 68

*Running head: 68 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160 (printed "ಸೂ- ೧೬೦.").*

In many such places it is shown, by repeating it again and again, that the existence of all the worlds, their maintenance and all the activity of protection rest on Heaven and Earth.

पिता माता च (*pitā mātā ca*)—The relation of mother and father that Heaven and Earth, of whom it is said द्याः पितः पृथिवी मातः (*dyāḥ pitaḥ pṛthivī mātaḥ*) *[so printed]*, have with all other beings was explained earlier.

सुधृष्ट्वमे (*sudhṛṣṭvame*) *[so printed]*—अतिशयेन दृष्टे । प्रगल्भे । (*atiśayena dṛṣṭe | pragalbhe |*) It means "those who are firm and of firm resolve".

रूपैरवासयत् (*rūpairavāsayat*)—Since the planets such as Āditya are produced from the atmosphere, (it is explained as) "through them it has spread its light"; or वृष्ट्यादिभिर्वा अभ्यवासयत् (*vṛṣṭyādibhirvā abhyavāsayat*), "it has filled with rain and the like".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*[The heading is printed "ವ್ಯಾಕರಣಕ್ರಿಯಾ" in the supplied text; the standard heading is inserted and the printed one kept in kn.md as a note.]*

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses उरुव्यचसा (*uruvyacasā*) (root व्यच (*vyaca*), Uṇādi suffix *asi*; the rule cited as "ಕಾ. ೧-೨-೧-೧", as printed; the accent by Pā. Sū. 6-2-199; सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) Pā. Sū. 7-1-39), महिनी (*mahinī*) (root मह (*maha*), suffix *inan*), असश्चता (*asaścatā*) (root षस्थ (*ṣastha*) *[so printed]*; नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) Pā. Sū. 6-2-172), रक्षतः (*rakṣataḥ*), सुधृष्टमे (*sudhṛṣṭame*), वपुष्ये (*vapuṣye*) and आवासयत् (*āvāsayat*) (root वस (*vasa*), *ṇic*, initial vṛddhi, imperfect in the Vedic language), with the accents; the analysis may continue on the next page.

#### Ṛk 3

*[The Saṃhitā and Pada of this Ṛk are printed as ordinary lines, not as blocks, in the supplied text; their labels are garbled ("। ಸಂಹಶಾಪಾದಿ ।" and "೧ ಪದಪಾಠ ೧"). The standard labels are inserted, the garbled lines kept in kn.md as notes, and the lines made blocks.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स वह्निः॑ पु॒त्रः पि॒त्रोः प॒वित्र॑वान्पु॒नाति॒ धीरो॒ भुव॑नानि॒॑
> मायया ।
> धे॒नुं च॒ पृश्निं॑ वृष॒भं सु॒रेत॑सं वि॒श्वाहा॑ शु॒क्रं पयो॑
> अस्य दुक्षत ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । वह्निः । पुत्रः । पित्रोः । पवित्रवान् । पुनाति । धीरः । भुवनानि । मायया ।
> धेनुं । च । पृश्निं । वृषभं । सुरेतसं । विश्वाहा । शुक्रं । पयः । अस्य । धुक्षत ॥३॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> पित्रोर्मातापित्रोर्द्यावापृथिव्योः ॥ पिता मात्रेति पिता शिष्यते । उदात्तयण इति पितुर्विभक्तिरुदात्ता ॥ पुत्रः पुरुत्राता पुत्रस्थानीय आदित्यः पवित्रवान्
> पावनरश्मियुक्तो धीरो धीमान् स वह्निः फलस्य वोढा धारकः सन्मयया
> प्रज्ञया स्वकीयया भुवनानि भूतजातानि पुनाति । पावयति । प्रकाशयतीत्यर्थः ।
> किंच स एव पुत्रः पृश्चिं शुक्लवर्णां धेनुं प्रीणयित्रीं भूमिं सुरेतसं शोभनसामर्थ्यं शोभनोदकं वा वृषभं सेक्तारं द्युलोकं च मायया पुनाति ।
> प्रकाशयतीत्यर्थः । कदा । विश्वाहा सर्वाण्यप्यहानि । सर्वकालमित्यर्थः ॥ किंचास्य
> म्युलोकस्य । यद्वा ॥ कर्मणि षष्ठी ॥ इमं द्युलोकं । शुक्रं पयो दीप्तं
> पयःसदृशमुदकं धुक्षत । दोग्धि ॥ दुहेश्छांदसे । लुजः शल इगुपधादनिटः क्ष इति
> च्छेः क्सादेशः ॥ ईदृशो महानुभाव आदित्यस्तयोः पुत्र इति द्यावापृथिव्योः स्तुतिः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Āditya, son of Heaven and Earth, wise, bearer of fruit, purifies the worlds by his wisdom and daily milks bright, milk-like water from the sky.]*

### Printed p. 70

*Running head: 70 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*)—of the parents (of the whole world), Heaven and Earth; पुत्रः (*putraḥ*)—who is in the position of a son; वह्निः (*vahniḥ*)—the bearer of fruit; ववित्रवान् (*vavitravān*) *[so printed for ಪವಿತ್ರವಾನ್]*—who has pure rays; धीरः सः (*dhīraḥ saḥ*)—the wise Āditya; मायया (*māyayā*)—by his wisdom; भुवनानि (*bhuvanāni*)—all the worlds; पुनाति (*punāti*)—illuminates and makes pure. (स एव (*sa eva*)—he himself) प्रश्निं (*praśniṃ*) *[so printed for ಪೃಶ್ನಿಂ]*—the white-coloured; धेनुं (*dhenuṃ*)—the delighting one, the Earth; सुरेतसं (*suretasaṃ*)—of excellent capacity; वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)—the heaven-world, which pours rain and the like (he makes pure); विश्वाहा (*viśvāhā*)—every day; अस्य (*asya*)—of this heaven-world (from the heaven-world); शुक्रं (*śukraṃ*)—shining; पयः (*payaḥ*)—water that resembles milk; धुक्षतः (*dhukṣataḥ*) *[so printed]*—milks.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Āditya, who is in the position of a son of Heaven and Earth, the parents of the whole world, who bears the fruit, has pure rays and is wise, illuminates all the worlds by his wisdom and makes them pure. He also purifies the white-coloured, delighting Earth, and the heaven-world, of excellent capacity, which pours rain and the like. Every day he milks from this heaven-world water that is shining and resembles milk.

*[Printed heading "English Tranlation" [sic].]*

**Printed English translation**

The pure and the resolute son of (these) parents, the bearer (of rewards sanctifies the worlds by his intelligence; as well as the milch-cow (the earth), and the vigorous bull (the heaven), and daily milks the pellucid milk (of the sky).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

पित्रोः पुत्रः (*pitroḥ putraḥ*)—In this ṛk, describing the great qualities of Āditya, it is indicated that the greatness of Heaven and Earth, his parents, must be greater still. पिता मात्रेति पिता शिष्यते (*pitā mātreti pitā śiṣyate*): it is said that if the two words *pitṛ* and *mātṛ* are combined, the single compound word *pitā* remains. It is said in many places that Heaven and Earth are the parents of Āditya, or that Āditya is the son of heaven: दिवस्पुत्राय सूर्याय शंसत (*divasputrāya sūryāya śaṃsata*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-37-1); and not only is it said of Sūrya alone that they are his parents, but also

### Printed p. 71

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨ ಅ. ೩. ನ. ೩. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 71 (printed "ನ. ೩." for "ವ. ೩."; as supplied).*

य इमे द्यावापृथिवी जनित्री रूपैरपिंशद्भवनानि विश्वा । (*ya ime dyāvāpṛthivī janitrī rūpairapiṃśadbhavanāni viśvā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-9)

in many such ṛks it is said that Heaven and Earth are the producers of the whole world; this is indicated here by the word पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*). Their son Āditya, मायया—भुवनानि पुनाति— (*māyayā—bhuvanāni punāti—*) "purifies the worlds by wisdom". Here the word माया (*māyā*) must be taken to mean wisdom. मायेति प्रज्ञानाम । (*māyeti prajñānāma |*) माया (*māyā*) वयुनं इति तन्नामसु पाठात् । (*vayunaṃ iti tannāmasu pāṭhāt |*) (Ni. 3-13). The authors of the Nirukta, quoting the ṛk माया मू तु यज्ञियानां (*māyā mū tu yajñiyānāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-88-6) and explaining स एव प्रज्ञां तैतां मन्यंते (*sa eva prajñāṃ taitāṃ manyaṃte*) *[so printed]*, have shown that the word माया (*māyā*) has the meaning "wisdom" (Ni. 7-27). Moreover, quoting the ṛk विश्वा हि माया (*viśvā hi māyā*) अवसि स्वधावः (*avasi svadhāvaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-58-1) they explained it as "wisdom" there too (Ni. 12-17). But in ṛks such as नि मायिनो दानवस्य (*ni māyino dānavasya*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-11-10), with मायाविनः दानवस्य वंचनानि (*māyāvinaḥ dānavasya vaṃcanāni*), the meanings "trickery" and "deceit" occur as well. In the present ṛk the purport is that Āditya purified the worlds by his wisdom.

धीरः (*dhīraḥ*)—धीमान् । (*dhīmān |*) Because he is wise, this epithet too is there. Ordinarily the meaning "courageous" is established for the word धीर (*dhīra*); but in the Vedas and the Upaniṣads too it is given the meaning धीमान् (*dhīmān*) (धीरो धीमान् (*dhīro dhīmān*), Ni. 3-12); this word is read among the names of the wise (Ni. 3-9). Even if धीर (*dhīra*) is explained as "courage", the purport is "one who has courage", since the wise man, having conquered fear, is fearless, and calm in mind even in times of calamity, and has other such qualities.

युवा सवासाः परिवीत आगात्स उ श्रेयान्भवति जायमानः । (*yuvā savāsāḥ parivīta āgātsa u śreyānbhavati jāyamānaḥ |*)
तं धीरासः कवय उन्नयंति स्वाध्यो ३ मनसा देवयंतः ॥ (*taṃ dhīrāsaḥ kavaya unnayaṃti svādhyo 3 manasā devayaṃtaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-8-4)

and in such ṛks, and in

श्रेयश्च प्रेयश्च मनुष्यमेतस्ता संपरीत्य विविनक्ति धीरः । (*śreyaśca preyaśca manuṣyametastā saṃparītya vivinakti dhīraḥ |*)
श्रेयो हि धीरोऽभिप्रेयसो वृणीते प्रेयो मंदो योगक्षेमा- (*śreyo hi dhīro 'bhipreyaso vṛṇīte preyo maṃdo yogakṣemā-*)
द्वृणीते ॥ (*dvṛṇīte ‖*)

(ಕರ. 2-2) *[abbreviation "ಕರ." as printed, not expanded]*

महांतं विभुमात्मानं मत्वा धीरो न शोचति । (*mahāṃtaṃ vibhumātmānaṃ matvā dhīro na śocati |*)

(ಕರ. 5-4) *[as printed]*

*[The text of p. 71 ends here with this quotation and its reference; the remarks that follow are in the next window.]*

### Printed p. 72

*Running head: 72 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160 (printed "ಮಂ ೧.").* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 3 continue.)

As in such Upaniṣad sentences धीरः (*dhīraḥ*) means "wise", "learned" (धीमान् (*dhīmān*), प्राज्ञः (*prājñaḥ*)), so here too it means "learned".

धेनुं (*dhenuṃ*)—The word धेनु (*dhenu*) is a synonym of "earth". धेनुर्धयतेर्वा, धिनोतेर्वा । (*dhenurdhayatervā, dhinotervā |*) धयतेऽसौ वत्सेनेति । तर्पयत्यसौ पयसा (*dhayate 'sau vatseneti | tarpayatyasau payasā*) (Ni. 11-42): since the milk is drunk by the calf, or since it gives milk and satisfies, cows are called धेनु (*dhenu*). The Earth too satisfies the world with the wealth of plants and the like; so this identity is stated.

पृश्निं (*pṛśniṃ*)—*Pṛśni* has the well-known meaning "the mother of deities such as the Maruts"; it is also a word that indicates colour; in some places it indicates a collection of mixed colours. In the present ṛk it is explained as "white colour".

सुरेतसं वृषभं (*suretasaṃ vṛṣabhaṃ*)—These epithets show the generative power of the presiding deity of the heaven-world. It was explained earlier that these two words indicate virility. The quality of the father, in द्यौः पिता (*dyauḥ pitā*), is described.

शुक्रं (*śukraṃ*)—शुक्रं शोचतेज्वलतिकर्मणः । (*śukraṃ śocatejvalatikarmaṇaḥ |*) Formed from the root शुच् (*śuc*), which gives the sense "to shine"; it means "exceedingly radiant".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*[The heading is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪೃಕನಪ್ರಕ್ರಿಯಾ"); the standard heading is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*) (the residue by माता च पिता च पितरौ तयोः पित्रोः पितामात्रा (*mātā ca pitā ca pitarau tayoḥ pitroḥ pitāmātrā*), Pā. Sū. 1-2-70), पुनाति (*punāti*) (root पूञ् (*pūñ*), *śnā*), धीरः (*dhīraḥ*) (root डुधाञ् (*ḍudhāñ*), suffix *kran* by सुसूधागृधिभ्यः क्रन् (*susūdhāgṛdhibhyaḥ kran*); Pā. Sū. 6-1-169), पृश्निम् (*pṛśnim*) (root स्पृश (*spṛśa*), Uṇ. Sū. 492), सुरेतसम् (*suretasam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-199, number broken across lines), विश्वाहा (*viśvāhā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70) and, on the next page, धुक्षत (*dhukṣata*) (root दुह (*duha*); Pā. Sū. 3-4-6, 3-1-45, 8-2-37 and 8-3-59, as printed), with the accents.

> वि । यः । ममे । रजसी इति । सुक्रतुयया । अजरेभिः । स्कंभनेभिः । सं । आनृचे ।
> ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> पूर्वं पुत्रमाहात्म्येनैते प्रशस्येदानीं स्वोत्पादकस्तुत्या प्रशंसति । अयं
> देवानां मध्ये देवतमोऽपसामहस्तमः । अप इति कर्मनाम । तेन तद्वान्
> लक्ष्यते । कर्मवतां मध्ये प्रकृष्टकर्मा । अयमित्युक्तं क इत्याह । यो देवो
> विश्वशंभुवा सर्वप्रकारेण भूतानां सुखस्य भावयित्र्यौ रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ
> महानुभावे जजान उत्पादितवान् । न केवलमुत्पादनमात्रं अपि तु यो देवो
> रजसी रंजनात्मिके द्यावापृथिव्यौ । रजसी इति द्यावापृथिव्योर्नाम रजसी सदसी इति

> *pūrvaṃ putramāhātmyenaite praśasyedānīṃ svotpādakastutyā praśaṃsati | ayaṃ*

> *rajasī raṃjanātmike dyāvāpṛthivyau | rajasī iti dyāvāpṛthivyornāma rajasī sadasī iti*

### Printed p. 74

*Running head: 74 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22 (printed with "ಆ."), Sū. 160.* (The bhāṣya block continues from p. 73.)

> तन्नामसु पाठात् । उक्तरूपे वि ममे विशेषेण परिच्छनत्ति । किं स्तोपभोगाय
> नेत्याह । सुक्रतूयया शोभनकर्मच्छया । येन कर्मणा प्राणिनां सुखं
> संभवति तादृक्कर्मेच्छया । अथवैतदुत्तरत्र संबंध्यते । सुक्रतूययोक्तेन निमित्तेनेमे द्यावापृथिव्यावजरेभिरजीर्णैर्दृढतरैः स्यंभनेभिर्गतिप्रतिबंधसाधनैः
> शंकुभिः समानृचे । सम्यक् सर्वतः पूजितवान् । स्थापितवानित्यर्थः ॥ ऋच स्तुता ।
> लिटि रेफसामान्याद्विहल्त्वमस्तीति तस्मान्नुड्द्विहल इत्यभ्यासस्य नुट् ॥ यस्मादेवं
> तस्मादयमेव देवोऽयमेवापस्तम इत्येवं महानुभावेन परमेश्वरेणोत्पन्ने
> इति स्तुतिः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): praising the maker: this Āditya, foremost of gods and doers, created, measured and, to support rites, propped Heaven and Earth with undecaying pillars.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("॥ ಪ್ರತಿವರ್ಧ ॥") and the Pratipadārtha sat inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted. Likewise the Bhāvārtha label ("ಬಾವಾರ್ಥ") and text.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यः (*yaḥ*)—the Āditya who; विश्वशंभुवा (*viśvaśaṃbhuvā*)—who gives happiness to the whole world; रोदसी (*rodasī*)—Heaven and Earth; जजान (*jajāna*)—caused to be born; and यः (*yaḥ*)—who; रजसी (*rajasī*)—both Heaven and Earth; वि ममे (*vi mame*)—measured; (moreover) सुक्रतूयया (*sukratūyayā*)—with the wish to help the performance of excellent rites; अजरेभिः (*ajarebhiḥ*)—by unwasting; स्कंभनेभिः (*skaṃbhanebhiḥ*)—supporting pillars; समानृचे (*samānṛce*)—gave support and established: such; अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Āditya; देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—among the gods (the most divine); अपसां (*apasāṃ*)—among those who perform works; अपस्तमः (*apastamaḥ*)—one who performs exceedingly excellent works (is).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Āditya who created Heaven and Earth, which give happiness to the whole world, who measured Heaven and Earth, and who, with the wish to help the performance of excellent rites, gave support with unwasting supporting pillars and established Heaven and Earth: such an Āditya is the most divine among the gods and, among those who perform works, the one who performs the most excellent works.

**Printed English translation**

He it is, amongst gods (the most divine), amongst (pious) works the most pious, who gave birth to the all-delighting heaven and earth : who measurd [sic] them both, and, for the sake of holy rites, propped them up with undecaying pillars.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

रोदसी जजान (*rodasī jajāna*)—In the sentence "he created Heaven and Earth", a contradiction appears when looked at superficially.

### Printed p. 75

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨ ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೩.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 75 (as supplied).* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 4 continue.)

नमो मित्रस्य वरुणस्य चक्षसे महो देवाय तदृतं सपर्यत । (*namo mitrasya varuṇasya cakṣase maho devāya tadṛtaṃ saparyata |*)
दूरेदृशे देवजाताय केतवे दिवस्सुताय सूराय शंसत ॥ (*dūredṛśe devajātāya ketave divassutāya sūrāya śaṃsata ‖*)

*[Stray fragment "( ಋ." as supplied, before the reference.]* (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-37-1)

In such sentences Āditya is called the son of heaven; in still other places he is also said to be the son of Heaven and Earth. But, supporting the view stated in the present ṛk,

यत्रा समुद्रः स्कभितो व्यौनदपां नपात्सविता तस्य वेद । (*yatrā samudraḥ skabhito vyaunadapāṃ napātsavitā tasya veda |*)
अतो भारत आ उत्थितं रजोऽतो द्यावापृथिवी अप्रथेतां !! (*ato bhārata ā utthitaṃ rajo 'to dyāvāpṛthivī aprathetāṃ !!*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-149-2) *[the closing double danda is printed "!!", as supplied]*

in such ṛks it is also said, अस्मादेव सवितुः भूमिरासीत् । रजः अंतरिक्षं च अजायत । (*asmādeva savituḥ bhūmirāsīt | rajaḥ aṃtarikṣaṃ ca ajāyata |*), that the earth and the atmosphere were born from this very Savitṛ. There is no contradiction in saying that there is a mutual relation of producer and produced; for, since Savitṛ rises in the atmosphere itself and the atmosphere is his support and refuge, he is called the son of heaven. And

दिवो धर्ता भवनस्य प्रजापतिः पिशंगं द्रापिं प्रति मुंचते कविः । (*divo dhartā bhavanasya prajāpatiḥ piśaṃgaṃ drāpiṃ prati muṃcate kaviḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-53-2)

आप्रा रजांसि दिव्यानि पार्थिवा श्लोकं देवः कृणुते धर्मणे (*āprā rajāṃsi divyāni pārthivā ślokaṃ devaḥ kṛṇute dharmaṇe*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-53-2) *[the same reference is printed again, as supplied]*

नि नाकमुख्यत्सविता वरेण्योऽनु प्रयाणमुषसो वि राजति । (*ni nākamukhyatsavitā vareṇyo 'nu prayāṇamuṣaso vi rājati |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-81-2)

in all such ṛks, because he made the earth and the atmosphere, which were hidden by darkness, bright and visible to all, because he is well known as the supporter of these worlds, and because he helps the two worlds to move according to the rule, he is said to be the producer of Heaven and Earth. Since there is truth in both statements, there is no contradiction between them.

विश्वशंभुवा रोदसी (*viśvaśaṃbhuvā rodasī*)—सर्वप्रकारेण भूतानां सुखस्य भावयित्र्यौ । (*sarvaprakāreṇa bhūtānāṃ sukhasya bhāvayitryau |*) "Givers of happiness to the beings in every way." Because the atmosphere *[printed "ಆಂತರಿಕ್ಷವೂ"]* makes the whole world luminous and is the support of rain and the like, and because the Earth, besides satisfying the gods through sacrifices and the like, becomes again the cause of rain and the like and provides wealth of plants and so on to all beings, it is said that both give happiness to the whole universe. This was explained earlier with examples.

### Printed p. 76

*Running head: 76 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160.*

स्कंभनेभिः समानृचे (*skaṃbhanebhiḥ samānṛce*)—"He stood (them) up, holding them up by giving a prop with supporting pillars."

न प्रमिये सवितुद्वैव्यस्य तद्यथा विश्वं भुवनं धारयिष्यति । (*na pramiye savitudvaivyasya tadyathā viśvaṃ bhuvanaṃ dhārayiṣyati |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-54-4)

सविता यंत्रैः पृथिवीमरम्णादस्कंभने सविता द्यामदृंहत् । (*savitā yaṃtraiḥ pṛthivīmaramṇādaskaṃbhane savitā dyāmadṛṃhat |*)
अश्वमिवाधुक्षद्दु निमंतरिक्ष मतूर्ते बद्धंसविता समुद्रं ॥ (*aśvamivādhukṣaddu nimaṃtarikṣa matūrte baddhaṃsavitā samudraṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-149-1)

Savitṛ has established the heaven-world, which was without support, by giving it support and a prop. By means of regulating instruments, such as the giving of rain, he has upheld the Earth. With bonds in the form of wind and the like *[printed "ವಾಯ್ತಾದಿ"]* he has made the atmosphere bound. Thus, because he has given to the three worlds, the earth and the rest, which were without support and without refuge, a support according to the need of each, that point is described by स्कंभनेभिः समानृचे (*skaṃbhanebhiḥ samānṛce*). The support and the sustenance found in the earth and the rest have a limit and a rule of time; but, to show that the support and refuge which Āditya gives to the earth and the atmosphere are eternal, the epithet अजरेभिः (*ajarebhiḥ*), "not wasting away", to स्कंभनेभिः (*skaṃbhanebhiḥ*) shows the nature and the power of the support.

(अयं देवानां मध्यॆ देवतमः (*ayaṃ devānāṃ madhyè devatamaḥ*))—This Āditya is the most excellent deity among the gods. Therefore among the gods he obtained the position of Brahman; he is one who took the place of Brahman in the earthly sacrifices and the like. Whatever excellent worthiness is to be had, among all the gods Āditya alone has this excellent worthiness; so "I was in the form of Bṛhaspati for all of them" is said in the "ವಾ. ಸಂಹಿತೆ" *[abbreviation as printed, not expanded]*. In the present ṛk the quality of Āditya is described only by the words अयं देवानां (*ayaṃ devānāṃ*). देवानां मध्यॆ देवतमः (*devānāṃ madhyè devatamaḥ*) is the sentence of Sāyaṇa's bhāṣya. This excellent quality is found in Savitṛ in many forms. For example, because Āditya gives vitality to all the gods,

सवितारं प्रसवायोपाधावत्सविता वै देवानां प्रसविता । (*savitāraṃ prasavāyopādhāvatsavitā vai devānāṃ prasavitā |*)

(Śa. Brā. 5-1-1-15)

सविता प्रसनानामधिपतिः । (*savitā prasanānāmadhipatiḥ |*)

(Tai. Saṃ. 3-3-5)

in sentences such as these he is praised as the impeller of all the gods. And not only this:

### Printed p. 77

*Running head: [೮.೨.೬. ೩ ವ. ೩. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 77 (printed "೮.೨.೬. ೩" for the Aṣṭaka and Adhyāya; as supplied).*

वनानि विभ्यो नकिरस्य तानि व्रता देवस्य सवितुर्मिनंति । (*vanāni vibhyo nakirasya tāni vratā devasya saviturminaṃti |*)
न यस्येंद्रो वरुणो न मित्रो व्रतमर्यमा न मिनंति रुद्रः ॥ (*na yasyeṃdro varuṇo na mitro vratamaryamā na minaṃti rudraḥ ‖*)
नारातयस्तमिदं स्वस्ति हुवे देवं सवितारं नमोभिः ॥ (*nārātayastamidaṃ svasti huve devaṃ savitāraṃ namobhiḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-38-7 to 9)

in such ṛks it is made clear that none of the gods, such as Indra, can transgress the rule of Savitṛ, and that all must remain in accordance with that rule. Thus, from many points of view, since the glory of Āditya in the form of Savitṛ is the greatest among the gods, and since it shows the nature of a leader, the greatness of Āditya is described by देवानां (*devānāṃ*) (मध्ये देवतमः (*madhye devatamaḥ*)) *[the supplied text has "ಸವಿತೃರೂಪನಾಡ" for "ಸವಿತೃರೂಪನಾದ"]*.

रजसी (*rajasī*)—रजो रजतेर्ज्योती रज उच्यते । (*rajo rajaterjyotī raja ucyate |*) उदकं रज उच्यते । (*udakaṃ raja ucyate |*) लोका (*lokā*) रजांस्युच्यंते (*rajāṃsyucyaṃte*) (Ni. 4-19). Formed from the root रंज् (*raṃj*), which gives the sense "to shine", the word रजस् (*rajas*) means "light", "water" and "worlds".

रजांसि चित्रा वि चरंति तन्यवो दिवः सम्राजा पयसा न उक्षतं । (*rajāṃsi citrā vi caraṃti tanyavo divaḥ samrājā payasā na ukṣataṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5 63-5) *[printed "೫ ೬೩-೫"]*

Quoting this ṛk, the authors of the Nirukta have shown that the word रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*) means "worlds" (in the 44th section of the 10th chapter of the Nirukta, as printed). While explaining the ṛk

अज्जामुक्षैरहिं गृणीषे बुद्धे नदीनां रजस्सु षीदन् । (*ajjāmukṣairahiṃ gṛṇīṣe buddhe nadīnāṃ rajassu ṣīdan |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-34-16)

they explain that the word रजस्सु (*rajassu*) means उदकेषु (*udakeṣu*), "in the waters"; and in the 7th section of the 12th chapter, while explaining the ṛk

एता उ त्या उषसः केतुमुक्त पूर्वे अर्धे रजसो भानुमंजते । (*etā u tyā uṣasaḥ ketumukta pūrve ardhe rajaso bhānumaṃjate |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-1)

they have also explained रजसः (*rajasaḥ*) as "atmosphere".

श्रीणि भांति रजांस्यस्य यत्पदानि तु तेजसा । (*śrīṇi bhāṃti rajāṃsyasya yatpadāni tu tejasā |*)

*[the reference is not printed]* The authors of the Bṛhaddevatā, describing the striding of Viṣṇu over the three worlds, have used the word रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*) as a synonym of the three worlds. *[printed "ಶ್ರೀಣಿ" in the Sanskrit line, for ತ್ರೀಣಿ, as supplied]* The words रजः (*rajaḥ*), रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*) are generally used as synonyms of "worlds". But, *[the text of p. 77 ends here, with "ಆದರೆ,"; the passage continues on p. 78, in the next window.]*

### Printed p. 78

*Running head: 78 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160 (printed "ಸೂ.೧೬೦").* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 4 continue; the passage broken off at the end of p. 77 with "ಆದರೆ," continues.)

यॆन्मा सोमासो ममदन्यदुक्थोभे भयेते रजसी अपारे । (*yènmā somāso mamadanyadukthobhe bhayete rajasī apāre |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-42-6)

In such ṛks this word is in the dual and has the technical meaning "Heaven and Earth". Only in some places, that is,

अहश्च कृष्णमहरर्जुनं च वि वर्तेते रजसी वेद्याभिः ॥ (*ahaśca kṛṣṇamahararjunaṃ ca vi vartete rajasī vedyābhiḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-9-1)

in one or two ṛks such as this, the basic meaning of the root is the main one: रजसी जगद्रंजयंतौ (*rajasī jagadraṃjayaṃtau*), "the two that delight the world". In the present ṛk too it is explained as रंजनात्मके द्यावापृथिव्यौ (*raṃjanātmake dyāvāpṛthivyau*).

स्कंभनेभि (*skaṃbhanebhi*) *[printed with a colon, "ಸ್ಕಂಭನೇಭಿ:"]*—गतिबंधनैः शंकुभिः । (*gatibaṃdhanaiḥ śaṃkubhiḥ |*) It is explained as "pegs that hold the motion"; that is, it means an instrument that regulates the movements of the worlds. It can be an instrument of measuring, and it can also be a supporting pillar. The extent of its meaning is great. In the Atharvaveda (10-7) the deity named Skambha (the deity in the form of the support of the universe) is well known.

क्व प्रेप्संती युवती विरूपे अहोरात्रे द्रवतः संविदाने । यत्र प्रेप्संती (*kva prepsaṃtī yuvatī virūpe ahorātre dravataḥ saṃvidāne | yatra prepsaṃtī*)
रभियंति आपः स्कंभं । यस्मिन् स्तब्ध्वा प्रजापतिर्लोकान्सर्वानधारयत् (*rabhiyaṃti āpaḥ skaṃbhaṃ | yasmin stabdhvā prajāpatirlokānsarvānadhārayat*)
स्कंभं ॥ (*skaṃbhaṃ ‖*)

(Atharvaveda 10-7-6 to 7; printed "ಅ. ವೇ.")

"Where the day and the night, both going for union, are eager to meet, into whom all the river-flows run, and on whose support Prajāpati created and regulated all these worlds: what is the nature of that Skambha?": thus it asks. The whole nature of Skambha described in this chapter is like the description of the nature of Hiraṇyagarbha described in sūkta 10-121 of the Ṛk-saṃhitā: as is said in sentences such as स दाधार पृथिवीमुत द्यां (*sa dādhāra pṛthivīmuta dyāṃ*) (10-121-1), he is said to be the support of all the universes. But in the Ṛk-saṃhitā too the meaning "support" is well known for the word स्कंभ (*skaṃbha*).

अयं विदच्चित्रदृशीकमर्णः शुक्रसद्मनामुषसामनीके । (*ayaṃ vidaccitradṛśīkamarṇaḥ śukrasadmanāmuṣasāmanīke |*)
अयं महान्महता स्कंभनेनॊद्वामस्तभ्नाद्वषभो मरुत्वान् ॥ (*ayaṃ mahānmahatā skaṃbhanenòdvāmastabhnādvaṣabho marutvān ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-47-5)

Here for स्कंभन (*skaṃbhana*) the meaning स्कंभसाधनेन मध्यवर्तिनांतरिक्षेण (*skaṃbhasādhanena madhyavartināṃtarikṣeṇa*), "by the atmosphere in the middle, in the form of a pillar", is given. Even so, this support is not all-pervading; the idea is of a regional support.

### Printed p. 79

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೩.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 79 (as supplied).*

In the present ṛk, as in the Atharvaveda sentence quoted above, the word has a meaning of extent that shows the pervasion of the universe. But although, according to Sāyaṇa's explanation, the sense "instrument that regulates the motion of the worlds" is suggested, since later the word समानृचे (*samānṛce*) is explained as स्थापितवान् (*sthāpitavān*), "established", the meaning "support" must be intended here too.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses अपसाम् (*apasām*) (suffix *asun*, Uṇ. Sū. 4-647; the genitive of "those who have works", by transference), अपस्तम (*apastama*) (suffix *tamap*), जजान (*jajāna*) (root जनी (*janī*), causal sense; accent by लिति (*liti*), Pā. Sū. 6-1-193, printed ".೬-೧-೧೯೩"), विश्वशंभुवा (*viśvaśaṃbhuvā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-106, as printed here; सुसां सुलुक् (*susāṃ suluk*) *[so printed for ಸುಪಾಂ ಸುಲುಕ್]*, Pā. Sū. 7-1-39), ममे (*mame*), सुक्रतूयया (*sukratūyayā*) (suffix *kyac*; Pā. Sū. 3-3-101; the loss of the *a* by Pā. Sū. 6-4-48), अजरेभिः (*ajarebhiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-116) and आनृचे (*ānṛce*) (root ऋच (*ṛca*); the augment *nuṭ* by Pā. Sū. 7-4-71), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೪ ॥.

### Printed p. 80

*Running head: 80 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. "೧೬೮" (printed so, for 160).*

#### Ṛk 5

*[The label printed before the Saṃhitā is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಂಸಾರ") and the Saṃhitā lines are not a block; the standard label is inserted, the garbled word kept in kn.md as a note, and the lines made a block; a hyphenated line break "ಸಮಿ-ನ್ವತಂ" is joined.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ते नो॑ गृणा॒ने म॑हिनी॒ महि॒ श्रवः॑ क्ष॒त्रं द्या॑वापृथिवी॒
> धासथो बृ॒हत् ।
> येना॒भि कृ॒ष्टीस्त॒तना॑म वि॒श्वहा॑ प॒नाय्य॒मोजो॑ अ॒स्मे समि॑न्वतं ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The Pada is kept as supplied, e.g. "ದಾ ಸ ಥ ಃ", "ಕೃಷ್ಣೀಃ", "ಪನಾಯ್ಕಂ", "ಆಸ್ಮೇ".]*

> ते इति । नः । गृणाने इति । महिनी इति । महि । श्रवः । क्षत्रं । द्यावापृथिवी इति ।
> दा स थ ः । बृहत् ।
> येन । अभि । कृष्णीः । ततनाम । विश्वहा । पनाय्कं । ओजः । आस्मे इति । सं । इन्वतं ।
> ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಾಯುಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sat inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The block closes with "ii", the double danda, as supplied.]*

> ते प्रसिद्धे हे द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ गृणाने अस्माभिः स्तूयमाने
> सत्यौ ॥ कर्मणि कर्तृप्रत्ययः ॥ महि महदतिप्रभूतं श्रवः सर्वत्र प्रसिद्धमन्नं सर्वत्र
> शूयमाणां कीर्तिं वा नोऽस्मभ्यं धासथः । धत्तं ॥ दधातेर्लॆट्यडागमः । सिब्ब
> हलमिति सिप् ॥ तथा बृहदतिप्रभूतं क्षत्रं बलं धासथः । तदेव विशेष्यते । येनान्नबलेन विश्वहा सर्वॆष्टप्यहस्सु कृष्टीः पुत्रादिरापाः प्रजा अभि ततनाम अभितो
> विस्तारयाम ॥ तनोतेर्लोटि छांदसो विकरणस्य श्लुः । आडुत्तमस्येत्याडागमः ।
> तस्य छंदस्युभयथेत्यार्थधातुकत्वादभ्यस्ताद्युदात्तत्वाभावे धातुस्वरः ॥ कृष्णय इति
> मनुष्यनाम कृष्णयश्चर्षणय इति तन्नामसु पाठात् । किंच पनाय्कं स्तुत्यमोजः
> शरीरबलमस्मे अस्मासु सम्यगिन्वतं । व्याप्नुतं । प्रवर्धयतमित्यर्थः । इन्वतिर्व्याप्तिवचनः इन्वति ननक्षेति तन्नामसु पाठात् ii

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): O praised Heaven and Earth, give us great food and vast strength, by which we daily extend our progeny, and increase our praiseworthy vigour.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ते (*te*)—well-known; महिनी (*mahinī*)—great; द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—O Heaven and Earth; गृणाने (*gṛṇāne*)—being praised (by us); येन (*yena*)—by which; विश्वाहा (*viśvāhā*)—every day; कृष्टी: (*kṛṣṭī:*) *[so printed]*—the people in the form of sons and the rest; अभि ततनाम (*abhi tatanāma*)—we extend (such); महि (*mahi*)—great; श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—food (or fame); बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant; क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)—strength… *[the Pratipadārtha is cut off here at the end of the supplied text of p. 80; it continues on p. 81, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 81

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೩.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 81 (as supplied).* (The Pratipadārtha of Ṛk 5 continues after "ಕ್ಷತ್ರಂ—strength".)

नः (*naḥ*)—to us; धासथः (*dhāsathaḥ*)—give; (and) अस्मे (*asme*)—to us; पनाय्क्यं (*panāykyaṃ*) *[so printed]*—worthy of praise; ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—vigour; समिन्वतं (*saminvataṃ*)—increase.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O well-known and great Heaven and Earth! Praised by us, give us abundant food and strength. By means of it, grant that we may daily increase our progeny. Increase also our vigour, which is worthy of praise.

**Printed English translation**

Glorified by us, grant to us, Heaven and Earth, abundant food and great strength ; whereby we may daily multiply mankind ; bestow upon us commendable vigour.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk a reward is expected from Heaven and Earth, whom (the seer) has worshipped. श्रव (*śrava*), क्षत्र (*kṣatra*) and ओजस् (*ojas*) are the rewards that are expected. Of these, श्रव (*śrava*) and क्षत्र (*kṣatra*) are the means of the increase of progeny.

श्रवः—सर्वत्र प्रसिद्धमन्नं, सर्वत्र श्रूयमाणां कीर्तिं वा । (*śravaḥ—sarvatra prasiddhamannaṃ, sarvatra śrūyamāṇāṃ kīrtiṃ vā |*) It is explained as well-known food or well-known fame. Both these meanings are possible for this word. The authors of the Nirukta, explaining the ṛk अभि श्रव ऋज्यंतः (*abhi śrava ṛjyaṃtaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-37-3), (say) for the word श्रव (*śrava*): श्रव इत्यन्ननाम, श्रूयत इति सतः । (*śrava ityannanāma, śrūyata iti sataḥ |*) — because it is well known everywhere (is heard by all), the word श्रव (*śrava*), formed from the root श्रु (*śru*), means "food" (Ni. 10-3); and

इंद्रस्य यस्य सुमखं सहो महि श्रवो नृण्णं च रोदसी सपर्यतः ॥ (*iṃdrasya yasya sumakhaṃ saho mahi śravo nṛṇṇaṃ ca rodasī saparyataḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-50-1)

here श्रवणीयं यशः । (*śravaṇīyaṃ yaśaḥ |*) is the explanation: "the fame that is heard by all", that is, well-known glory (Ni. 11-9). Therefore in the present ṛk the meaning "food" or "glory", the one synonymous with the other, is stated; or, since food too gives a share of glory, "food as the means of glory" is also understood.

बृहत्या श्रीकामो यशस्वामः कुर्वीत । श्रीवैर्यशश्चंदसां बृहती । श्रिय (*bṛhatyā śrīkāmo yaśasvāmaḥ kurvīta | śrīvairyaśaścaṃdasāṃ bṛhatī | śriya*)
मेव यश आत्मनत्ते । (*meva yaśa ātmanatte |*)

(Ai. Brā. 1-5)

(Ai. Brā. 1-5) *[the reference is printed twice, as supplied]*

*[Stray signature line "[6] Vol. 12" at the end of p. 81, kept in kn.md as a note.]*

### Printed p. 82

*Running head: 82 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22 (printed "ಅ ೨೨."), Sū. 160.*

Here the identity of Śrī, in the form of wealth such as grain, and of the glory obtained from it is stated. If one has wealth, a man becomes famous through gifts and the like; so wealth of food and the like is the basis of glory, and if wealth such as food exists, glory is obtained of itself; hence two meanings can be given to the word श्रव (*śrava*). It is prayed: "give us such श्रव (*śrava*)".

क्षेत्र (*kṣetra*) *[so printed for ಕ್ಷತ್ರ]*—The word क्षत्र (*kṣatra*) means "strength". This is not ordinary strength; it is the power that protects food, glory and the whole of a man's possessions. If this *kṣatra* is not there, a man can have no kind of protection. Because it is so necessary, (the seer), not content with the satisfaction of food and glory, prays for *kṣatra* also. Therefore in other places too the greatness of *kṣatra* is praised at length. The means of wealth of food and the like is the sacrifice; the protector of such a sacrifice is *kṣatra*; if there is no support of *kṣatra*, there is no protection of the sacrifice. Hence its greatness and its necessity.

अथातो देवयजनस्यैव याज्ञस्तदाहुर्यद्ब्राह्मणो राजन्यो वैश्यो (*athāto devayajanasyaiva yājñastadāhuryadbrāhmaṇo rājanyo vaiśyo*)
दीक्षिष्यमाणः क्षत्रियं देवयजनं याचति कं क्षत्रियो याचेदिति । दैवं (*dīkṣiṣyamāṇaḥ kṣatriyaṃ devayajanaṃ yācati kaṃ kṣatriyo yācediti | daivaṃ*)
क्षत्रं याचेदित्याहुरादितोवै दैवं क्षत्रमादित्य एषां भूतानामधि- (*kṣatraṃ yācedityāhurāditovai daivaṃ kṣatramāditya eṣāṃ bhūtānāmadhi-*)
पतिः । .... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... तस्य ह न काचन (*patiḥ | .... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... .... tasya ha na kācana*)
रिष्टिर्भवति देवेन सवित्रा प्रसूतस्कोत्तरोत्तरिणीं ह श्रियमश्नुतेऽश्नुते (*riṣṭirbhavati devena savitrā prasūtaskottarottariṇīṃ ha śriyamaśnute 'śnute*)
ह प्रजानामैश्वर्यमाधिपत्यं य एवमुपस्थाय याचित्वा देवयजनमध्यव- (*ha prajānāmaiśvaryamādhipatyaṃ ya evamupasthāya yācitvā devayajanamadhyava-*)
साय दीक्षिते क्षत्रियः सन् । (*sāya dīkṣite kṣatriyaḥ san |*)

(ಇ ಬ್ರ. 7-20) *[printed so; abbreviation not expanded]*

If Brahmins and others of the other classes wish to perform a sacrifice, they must obtain the support of a *kṣatriya*; if a *kṣatriya* himself wishes to perform a sacrifice, he must obtain the support of the divine *kṣatra*. Āditya, the lord of the whole world, is the essence of this *kṣatra*. Every sacrificer, completing the sacrifice with the protection of this Āditya in the form of *kṣatra*, obtains lordship himself, obtains all wealth, and obtains unobstructed victory.

At sacrifices, or at other times of praising the deities, in order that the work may be accomplished without obstacle and that all wealth may be obtained, just as the protection of deities such as Āditya, in the form of *kṣatra*, must be obtained, so the protection of mantras such as the *trivṛt-stoma*, in the form of *kṣatra*, must be obtained: thus their power is described.

सैतस्मिन्नधूळ्हा स यजमानस्तद्वैवं क्षत्रं सहो बलं । अश्नुते ह वै दैवं (*saitasminnadhūḷhā sa yajamānastadvaivaṃ kṣatraṃ saho balaṃ | aśnute ha vai daivaṃ*)
क्षत्रं सहो बलं । (*kṣatraṃ saho balaṃ |*)

(ಇತರೀಯ ಬ್ರಾಹ್ಮಣಂ 3-41) *[printed so, "ಇತರೀಯ" for Aitareya]*

### Printed p. 83

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೩.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 83 (as supplied).*

The *trivṛt-stoma*, the means of brilliance, vigour, standing, victory and greatness, is itself the essence of this *kṣatra*. This power can stop any kind of obstacle. One who knows the secret of this mantra obtains the power to stop all kinds of obstacles: thus the power of mantras such as the *trivṛt-stoma* is described; and besides,

त्रिष्टुप्रैष्टुभो वै राजन्य ओजो ना इंद्रियं वीर्यं त्रिष्टुबोजः क्षत्रं (*triṣṭupraiṣṭubho vai rājanya ojo nā iṃdriyaṃ vīryaṃ triṣṭubojaḥ kṣatraṃ*)
वीर्यं राजन्यस्तदेनमोजसा क्षत्रेण वीर्येण समर्थयति ॥ (*vīryaṃ rājanyastadenamojasā kṣatreṇa vīryeṇa samarthayati ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 8-2)

in such sentences the metres, such as *triṣṭubh*, which are in the form of *kṣatra*, and the point that, by worshipping them, one obtains strength in the form of *kṣatra* together with vigour and *ojas* and makes it a means of the wealth of progeny: thus the greatness of *kṣatra* is praised. To show that this is a supernatural power, दैवं क्षत्रं (*daivaṃ kṣatraṃ*) is said everywhere.

क्षत्रं तदपि विज्ञेयं ब्रह्म यत्पाति शाश्वतं । (*kṣatraṃ tadapi vijñeyaṃ brahma yatpāti śāśvataṃ |*)

(Bṛ. De. 5-126) *[printed "ಬೃ. ದೇ."]*

That which has the ability to protect the eternal Brahman should be recognised as *kṣatra*. By this power all the other powers are protected.

तस्मादु सर्वेषु लोकेषु क्षत्रस्यैव प्रशासनमभूत् । (*tasmādu sarveṣu lokeṣu kṣatrasyaiva praśāsanamabhūt |*)

(Chā. U. 5-3-7) *[printed "ಛಾ. ಉ."]*

Therefore in all the worlds lordship belongs to the *kṣatriya*.

तस्मात् क्षत्रात्परं नास्ति तस्माद्ब्राह्मणः क्षत्रियमधस्तादुपास्ते । (*tasmāt kṣatrātparaṃ nāsti tasmādbrāhmaṇaḥ kṣatriyamadhastādupāste |*)

(Bṛ. U. 1-4-11)

Therefore, at the Rājasūya and similar sacrifices, even a Brahmin sits at a lower place than the *kṣatriya*. But this *kṣatra* is not merely a physical power.

तच्छ्रेयोरूपमत्यसृजत धर्मं तदेतत् क्षत्रस्य क्षत्रं यद्धर्मस्तस्मा- (*tacchreyorūpamatyasṛjata dharmaṃ tadetat kṣatrasya kṣatraṃ yaddharmastasmā-*)
द्वर्मात्परं । (*dvarmātparaṃ |*)

(Bṛ. U. 1-4-14)

This power is a *dharma* better than all *dharmas*; apart from it there is no other *dharma*. Because there is such a supernatural power, in the present ṛk, in order to obtain wealth in the form of food and in the form of glory, along with it the power in the form of *kṣatra*, which protects it, *[the supplied text of p. 83 ends here, in the middle of the sentence ("…ಕ್ಷತ್ರರೂಪದ ಶಕ್ತಿ"); it continues on p. 84, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 84

*Running head: 84 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 160.* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 5 continue.)

*[The sentence broken off at the end of p. 83 continues with "…ಶಕ್ತಿ|ಯನ್ನೂ ಪ್ರಾರ್ಥಿಸಿದೆ.": "…is also prayed for."]* It is also stated clearly that, in येन कृष्णी अभि ततनाम (*yena kṛṣṇī abhi tatanāma*) *[so printed, "ಕೃಷ್ಣೀ"]*, even wealth in the form of sons grows only by resorting to this power.

कृष्णी (*kṛṣṇī*) *[so printed for ಕೃಷ್ಟೀ]*—The word has the ordinary meaning "human beings". The authors of the Nirukta,

आ दधिक्राः शवसा पंच कृस्टीः सूर‍्य इव ज्योतिषापस्ततान । (*ā dadhikrāḥ śavasā paṃca kṛsṭīḥ sūrya iva jyotiṣāpastatāna |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-38-10)

for the words पंच कृष्टीः (*paṃca kṛṣṭīḥ*) in this ṛk, explained पंच जनान् (*paṃca janān*) (Ni. 10-31), including all classes of human beings. In the present ṛk it is explained as पुत्रादिरूपाः प्रजाः (*putrādirūpāḥ prajāḥ*), "people in the form of sons and others"; the purport is wealth of progeny.

पनाय्कं (*panāykaṃ*) *[printed "ಪನಾಯ್ಕಂ-"]*—स्तुत्यं । (*stutyaṃ |*) It means "worthy of praise". It is an epithet of the word ओजः (*ojaḥ*). The authors of the Nirukta have included पनस्यति (*panasyati*) and पनायते (*panāyate*) among the names of worship (Ni. 3-19), and

रथे तिष्ठन्नयति वाजिनः पुरो यत्रयत्र कामयते सुषारथिः । (*rathe tiṣṭhannayati vājinaḥ puro yatrayatra kāmayate suṣārathiḥ |*)
अभीशूनां महिमानं पनायत मनः पश्चादनु यच्छंति रश्मयः ॥ (*abhīśūnāṃ mahimānaṃ panāyata manaḥ paścādanu yacchaṃti raśmayaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-25-6)

for the word पनायत (*panāyata*) in this ṛk they explain पूजयत (*pūjayata*). In the same way here too the sense of worship is there, and the purport is "worthy of praise". Since it is an epithet of *ojas*, one should understand what the nature of that *ojas* is.

ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—The authors of the Nirukta have included this word among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9) and then, with ओज (*oja*)तेर्वोब्ब तेर्वा (*tervobba tervā*) (Ni. 6-8, the line broken in print), have shown that it is formed either from the root ओज (*oja*), meaning "to be able", or from the root उब्ज् (*ubj*), meaning "to subdue". The ability to acquire power oneself and to subdue other obstacles is indicated by this word.

मंद्र ओजिष्ठ इत्योजस्वत्तत् क्षत्रस्य रूपं । (*maṃdra ojiṣṭha ityojasvattat kṣatrasya rūpaṃ |*)

(Ai. Brā. 8-2)

In such sentences the identity of the power in the form of *kṣatra* and the power in the form of *ojas* is stated. It is described not merely as an external power but as an inner power that conquers the six enemies (*ariṣaḍvarga*).

इन्वतं (*invataṃ*)—इन्वति, नक्षति (*invati, nakṣati*) and others are read among the names of pervasion (Ni. 3-10). Here it means "increase"; it is prayed: "increase our power".

### Printed p. 85

*Running head: [ಅ ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೩. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 85 (as supplied).*

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses गृणाने (*gṛṇāne*) (root ग्य (*gya*) *[so printed]*, used in the sense of praise; *śānac* in the passive sense by the Vedic rule, *śnā*; Pā. Sū. 7-3-80 and 6-1-193), धासथः (*dhāsathaḥ*) (root डुधाञ् (*ḍudhāñ*), *let*; Pā. Sū. 3-4-94 and 3-1-34), कृष्टीः (*kṛṣṭīḥ*) (root कृष (*kṛṣa*), suffix *ktic*, Pā. Sū. 3-3-174; accent Pā. Sū. 6-1-163), ततनाम (*tatanāma*) (root तनु (*tanu*); Pā. Sū. 3-4-99, 3-4-92, 8-1-66 and 3-4-117), विश्वहा (*viśvahā*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) and इन्वतम् (*invatam*) (root इवि (*ivi*)), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೫ ॥.

*Here the 160th sūkta is ended: "ಇಲ್ಲಿಗೆ ೧೬೦ನೇ ಸೂಕ್ತವು ಮುಗಿದುದು." A line of ornament follows: "————:0:————".*

### Printed p. 86

*Running head: 86 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.*

## Sūkta 161 (Maṇḍala 1)

*"ನೂರ ಅರವತ್ತೊಂದನೆಯ ಸೂಕ್ತವು" — "the hundred and sixty-first sūkta".*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; the sense is in the Anuvāda below):

> किमु श्रेष्ठ इति चतुर्दशर्चं पंचमं सूक्तं दैर्घतमसमार्भवं । अंत्या
> त्रिष्टुप् शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुबंत परिभाषया जगत्यः ! अनुक्रांतं च । किमु श्रेष्ठः षळूनार्भवं त्रिष्टुबंतमिति ॥ व्यूळ्छे दशरात्रे षष्ठेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्र आर्भवं निविद्वानं
> द्विप्रतीकॆं । तत्र किमु श्रेष्ठ इत्येतदंत्यवर्जं प्रतीकं । व्यूळ्छेदिति खंडे सूत्रितं।
> किमु श्रेष्ठ उप नो वाजा इति त्रयोदशार्ध्वं चतस्रश्च । अ. ८-८ । इति ॥

> *kimu śreṣṭha iti caturdaśarcaṃ paṃcamaṃ sūktaṃ dairghatamasamārbhavaṃ | aṃtyā*
> *triṣṭup śiṣṭāstriṣṭubaṃta paribhāṣayā jagatyaḥ ! anukrāṃtaṃ ca | kimu śreṣṭhaḥ ṣaḷūnārbhavaṃ triṣṭubaṃtamiti ‖ vyūḷche daśarātre ṣaṣṭhe 'hani vaiśvadevaśastra ārbhavaṃ nividvānaṃ*
> *dvipratīkèṃ | tatra kimu śreṣṭha ityetadaṃtyavarjaṃ pratīkaṃ | vyūḷchediti khaṃḍe sūtritaṃ|*
> *kimu śreṣṭha upa no vājā iti trayodaśārdhvaṃ catasraśca | a. 8-8 | iti ‖*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta beginning किमु श्रेष्ठ (*kimu śreṣṭha*) is the fifth sūkta in the twenty-second anuvāka. It has fourteen ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Dīrghatamas, son of Ucathya (printed "ಉಚಥ್ಯ", as supplied). The Ṛbhus are the deity *[printed "ಋಭಗಳೇ"]*. Only the last ṛk of the sūkta is in *triṣṭubh* metre; the other ṛks are in *jagatī* metre *[printed "ಜಗತೀಳಂದಸ್ಸಿನವು"]*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: किमु श्रेष्ठ षळूनार्भवं त्रिष्टुबंतमिति (*kimu śreṣṭha ṣaḷūnārbhavaṃ triṣṭubaṃtamiti*). On the sixth day of the sacrifice of ten days called *vyūḍha* (or the sacrifice called *daśarātra*), when the mantras of the Vaiśvadeva-śastra are recited, of the two *pratīkas* (parts) provided for the *nivid-dhāna* mantras related to the deity Ṛbhu, the ṛks other than the last are to be used as the mantras of one *pratīka*, as explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra in the section व्यूढश्चेत् (*vyūḍhaścet*): किमुश्रेष्ठ (*kimuśreṣṭha*) उपनो वाजा इति त्रयोदशार्भवं चतस्रश्च (*upano vājā iti trayodaśārbhavaṃ catasraśca*) (Ā. 8-8).

**Sūkta—161**

*[Om sign ("॥ ಓಂ ॥") printed above the sūkta number, left out here and in the Kannada-script file as a sign only.]*

Maṇḍala 1 ‖ Anuvāka 22 ‖ Sūkta 161 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 3 ‖ Varga 4, 5, 6 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 14 ‖
Ṛṣi: Dīrghatamas Aucathya · Devatā: the Ṛbhus (ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)) · Chandas: Ṛks 1–13 *jagatī*; Ṛk 14 *triṣṭubh*.

### Printed p. 87

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೪. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 87 (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 1

*[The Saṃhitā of this Ṛk is garbled in the supplied text (it reads, e.g., "ಯವಿಷ್ಟೋ", "ಕದದ್ಯೂಚಿಮು", "ಚಮಸಂ", and carries a numeral "೧" at the end of the first line); no picture was supplied for it. It is kept as supplied.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> किमु श्रेष्ठः किं यविष्टो न आजगन्निमायते दूत्यं १
> कदद्यूचिमु ।
> न निंदिम चमसं यो महाकुलोऽग्ने भ्रातर्दुर्ण
> इद्भूतिमूदिनु ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[The label line is garbled in the supplied text ("ಚಡಚಾಡು") and sits inside the Saṃhitā block; the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

> किं । ऊं इति । श्रेष्ठः । किं । यविष्ठः । नः । आ । अ ज ग न् । किं । ईयते । दूत्यं ।
> कत् । यत् । ऊचिम ।
> न । निंदिम । चमसं । यः । महाकुलः । अग्ने । भ्रातः । द्रुणः । इत् । भूतिं ।
> ऊदिम ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಸಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯ c") and sits inside the Pada block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined; a break "ಸಮಾನ-ರೂಪ…" across the page boundary is kept.]*

> ऋभवो नाम सुधन्वनस्त्रयः पुत्रा ऋभुर्विभ्वा वाज इति । ते च मनुष्याः
> संतः सुकर्मणा देवत्वं प्राप्य कदाचित्कर्मकाले सोमपानाय प्रवृत्ताः । तान्प्रति
> देवैः प्रेरितोऽग्निः परस्परसमानरूपान्दृष्टा स्वयमपि तदाकारं धृत्वा तेषु
> मध्यॆ स्वयं चतुर्थः सन् पातुं प्रवृत्तः । ते च ऋभव आगतं तं समानरूपमवलोक्य विवेकुमसमर्थाः परस्परमेवं संदिहते । अयं किमु श्रेष्ठः किं नु
> खल्पस्मत्तोऽयं प्रशस्यतमो वयसा श्रेष्ठः सोऽस्मानाजगन् । आगमत् । प्राप्तः ॥
> गमर्लजि छांदसः शपः श्लुः । हल्बादिलोपे मो नो धातोरिति नत्वं । अजगन्निति
> गतिकर्मा अगन्न जगन्निति तन्नामसु पाठात् ॥ किं यविष्ठः किं नास्माकं युवतमोऽस्मत्तः कनीयानाजगन् ! प्राप्तः । किं वा दूत्यं दूतकर्म देवसंबंधीयते ।
> गच्छति । देवैः प्रेरितो दूतोऽस्मानागतो वा ॥ दूतस्य भागकर्मणी इति यत् !!
> यदूचिम यदेतद्दूमः तत्कत् । कथं निश्चेतव्यमित्यर्थः । वयं तावत्रय एनेदानीं चत्वारः समानरूपा वर्तामहे तस्मादयमधिकः किमु श्रेष्ठ इति विचिकित्सा ।
> एवं संदिह्य कथंचित्सतोऽन्यं निश्चित्य तं प्रत्यापरोक्षेण ब्रुवते । हे अग्ने
> भ्रातर्भ्रातृवद्भागार्ह । भ्राता यथा बलात्स्वकीयभागं स्वीकरोति तद्वत् समान-

> *ṛbhavo nāma sudhanvanastrayaḥ putrā ṛbhurvibhvā vāja iti | te ca manuṣyāḥ*
> *saṃtaḥ sukarmaṇā devatvaṃ prāpya kadācitkarmakāle somapānāya pravṛttāḥ | tānprati*
> *devaiḥ prerito 'gniḥ parasparasamānarūpāndṛṣṭā svayamapi tadākāraṃ dhṛtvā teṣu*
> *madhyè svayaṃ caturthaḥ san pātuṃ pravṛttaḥ | te ca ṛbhava āgataṃ taṃ samānarūpamavalokya vivekumasamarthāḥ parasparamevaṃ saṃdihate | ayaṃ kimu śreṣṭhaḥ kiṃ nu*
> *khalpasmatto 'yaṃ praśasyatamo vayasā śreṣṭhaḥ so 'smānājagan | āgamat | prāptaḥ ‖*
> *gamarlaji chāṃdasaḥ śapaḥ śluḥ | halbādilope mo no dhātoriti natvaṃ | ajaganniti*
> *gatikarmā aganna jaganniti tannāmasu pāṭhāt ‖ kiṃ yaviṣṭhaḥ kiṃ nāsmākaṃ yuvatamo 'smattaḥ kanīyānājagan ! prāptaḥ | kiṃ vā dūtyaṃ dūtakarma devasaṃbaṃdhīyate |*
> *gacchati | devaiḥ prerito dūto 'smānāgato vā ‖ dūtasya bhāgakarmaṇī iti yat !!*
> *yadūcima yadetaddūmaḥ tatkat | kathaṃ niścetavyamityarthaḥ | vayaṃ tāvatraya enedānīṃ catvāraḥ samānarūpā vartāmahe tasmādayamadhikaḥ kimu śreṣṭha iti vicikitsā |*
> *evaṃ saṃdihya kathaṃcitsato 'nyaṃ niścitya taṃ pratyāparokṣeṇa bruvate | he agne*
> *bhrātarbhrātṛvadbhāgārha | bhrātā yathā balātsvakīyabhāgaṃ svīkaroti tadvat samāna-*

### Printed p. 88

*Running head: 88 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.* (The bhāṣya block continues from p. 87, after "ಸಮಾನ-".)

> रूपमाश्रित्य बलाच्चमसपानाय प्रवृत्त इति भ्रातरित्युक्तं । हे तादृशाग्ने चमसं न
> निंदिम । अधिकः समागत इति पानमकृत्वा चमसं न दूषयामः ॥ णुदि कुत्सायां ।
> छंदसि वेति वक्तव्यमिति द्विर्वचनाभावः ॥ अदूष्यत्वे कारणमाह । यश्चमसो महाकुलः महाकुलोत्पन्नः त्वष्टा निर्मितत्वात् । वक्ष्यति च त्वष्टा ग्नास्वंतर्यानज इति ।
> अतः कारणात् द्रुणः । विकारे प्रकृतिशब्दः । ततश्च तत्स्थे तच्छब्दः । इच्छब्बोऽवधारणे ।
> दारुविकारचमसस्य भूतिं प्राप्तिमूदिम । ब्रूमः ॥ विदेश्यांदसे लिटि वचिस्वपीति
> संप्रसारणं । क्रादिनियमादिट् ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): the Ṛbhus ask whether the newcomer, Agni in their likeness, is elder, younger or a messenger, and they honour the ladle.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕ್ರಿಪಾಪಾತ್ರ್ಯ") and the Pratipadārtha sat inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted. Likewise the Bhāvārtha label ("॥ ಭವಾರ್ಥ ॥") and text.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

नः (*naḥ*)—to us; अजगन् (*ajagan*)—this one who has come; किमु श्रेष्ठः (*kimu śreṣṭhaḥ*)—(in age or in worth) is he greater than we? किं यविष्ठः (*kiṃ yaviṣṭhaḥ*)—or is he younger? (or) मूत्यं (*mūtyaṃ*) *[so printed for ದೂತ್ಯಂ]*—having taken up the office of messenger (of the gods); किं ईयते (*kiṃ īyate*)—has he come? यत् (*yat*) ऊचिम (*ūcima*)—if there is anything we have to say; कत् (*kat*)—what is it? भ्रातः अग्ने (*bhrātaḥ agne*)—O Agni, brother; यः महाकुलः (*yaḥ mahākulaḥ*)—which (this ladle) is born of a noble family; (तं (*taṃ*)) चमसं (*camasaṃ*)—that ladle; न निंदिम (*na niṃdima*)—(in anger that a newcomer has come to drink, without drinking ourselves) we do not revile; द्रुण इत् (*druṇa it*)—of the ladle made of wood; भूतिमित् (*bhūtimit*) *[so printed]*—the greatness; ऊदिम (*ūdima*)—we declare, making it known.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Who is this one who has newly come to us? Is he older than we, in age or in worth, or younger? Or has he come having taken up the office of messenger of the gods? If there is anything that we have to say to him, what is it? O Agni, brother! Out of anger that a newcomer has come to drink with us, we do not revile, without drinking, this ladle, which is born of a noble family. We shall tell you, so that you too may know it, the greatness of this vessel made of wood.

**Printed English translation**

Is this our senior or our junior who has come (to us); has he come upon a message (from the gods); what is it we should say? Agni, brother, we revile not the ladle which is of exalted race; verily we assert the dignity of the wooden (Implement).

### Printed p. 89

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೪ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 89 (as supplied).*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the introduction to the bhāṣya of this ṛk, Sāyaṇa has told the earlier story connected with it. The three sons of Sudhanvan, named Ṛbhu (printed "ಯಭು"), Vibhvan and Vāja, though human, having obtained divinity by their good works, once, at the time of a sacrifice, set out with the gods to drink Soma. Agni, impelled by the gods, saw them, who were of identical form, took on the same form himself and began to drink with them. Not knowing who the fourth was who had come with the same form as theirs, they doubt among themselves. This inquiry is in the first half of this ṛk; that, after some time, having ascertained who he was, they will converse with him and tell him the greatness of the ladle, is said in the second half. The noble birth of these brothers, the greatness of their good works, how the gods, out of jealousy, caused them trouble when they joined the gods, and how Prajāpati and Savitṛ then helped them, are told in some places. For example:

ऋभवो वै देवेषु तपसा सोमपीथमभ्यजयंस्तेभ्यः प्रातस्सवने वाचि (*ṛbhavo vai deveṣu tapasā somapīthamabhyajayaṃstebhyaḥ prātassavane vāci*)
कल्पयिषंस्तानग्निर्वसुभिः प्रातःसवनादनुदत तेभ्यो माध्यंदिने सवने (*kalpayiṣaṃstānagnirvasubhiḥ prātaḥsavanādanudata tebhyo mādhyaṃdine savane*)
वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तानिंद्रो रुद्रॆर्माध्यंदिनात्सवनादनुदत तेभ्यस्तृतीय (*vāci kalpayiṣaṃstāniṃdro rudrèrmādhyaṃdinātsavanādanudata tebhyastṛtīya*)
सवने वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तान्विश्वे देवा अनोनुद्यंत नेह पास्यंति नेहेति (*savane vāci kalpayiṣaṃstānviśve devā anonudyaṃta neha pāsyaṃti neheti*)
स प्रजापतिरब्रवीत्सवितारं तव वा इमेऽंतेवासास्त्वमेवैभिः संपिबस्वेति (*sa prajāpatirabravītsavitāraṃ tava vā ime 'ṃtevāsāstvamevaibhiḥ saṃpibasveti*)
स तथेत्यब्रवीत्सविता तान्नै त्वमुभयतः परिपिबेति तांप्रजापतिरुभयतः (*sa tathetyabravītsavitā tānnai tvamubhayataḥ paripibeti tāṃprajāpatirubhayataḥ*)
परपिबत् ॥ (*parapibat ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 3-3-30)

The Ṛbhus obtained a share of Soma among the gods by their austerity and intense wish. First, when they wished to obtain it at the morning pressing, Agni, joining the Vasus, rejected them and drove them away. Then, when they wished to obtain it at the midday pressing, Indra, joining the Rudras, drove them away. Again, when they wished to obtain it at the third pressing, the Viśvedevas rejected them there too with harsh words: "You are certainly not fit to drink Soma here; you must not." Then Prajāpati said to Savitṛ: "These Ṛbhus are your pupils; drink Soma with them." Savitṛ agreed. Prajāpati too, staying beside them, drank the Soma juice: thus this story is told in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa.

### Printed p. 90

*Running head: 90 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 1 continue.)

Having obtained such a qualification for drinking Soma, they were drinking from the ladle when Agni, impelled by the gods, entered in the middle; at first they did not know about him and doubted, and then, after discovering who he was, they speak as is said in the ṛk.

किमु श्रेष्ठः किं यविष्ठः (*kimu śreṣṭhaḥ kiṃ yaviṣṭhaḥ*)—ते च ऋभवः आगतं तं समानरूपमवलोक्य (*te ca ṛbhavaḥ āgataṃ taṃ samānarūpamavalokya*) विवेकुमसमर्थाः परस्परमेवं संदिहते । (*vivekumasamarthāḥ parasparamevaṃ saṃdihate |*) When Agni, having taken on their very form, came and sat among them, they could not tell any difference between themselves and Agni; then, in doubt, they express their doubt: "Is the newcomer older than we, or younger? What is the purpose of his coming?" and so on.

महाकुलः (*mahākulaḥ*)—महाकुलोत्पन्न स्वष्टा निर्मितत्वात् । (*mahākulotpanna svaṣṭā nirmitatvāt |*) *[so printed, "ಸ್ವಷ್ಟಾ"]* Since it was made by Tvaṣṭṛ, it is said to be born of a great family.

उत त्यं चमसं नवं त्वष्टुर्देवस्य निष्कृतं । अकर्त चतुरः पुनः ॥ (*uta tyaṃ camasaṃ navaṃ tvaṣṭurdevasya niṣkṛtaṃ | akarta caturaḥ punaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-20-6)

In such places the excellent origin of this vessel is described: "Tvaṣṭṛ first made the ladle." That is stated here by the epithet महाकुलः (*mahākulaḥ*).

द्रुणः (*druṇaḥ*)—दारुविकारचमसः । (*dāruvikāracamasaḥ |*) The ladle made of wood.

भ्रातः (*bhrātaḥ*)—भ्रातृवद्भागार्ह । (*bhrātṛvadbhāgārha |*) Just as a brother insistently expects his share, so Agni, who came as a fourth to take a share with the three of them, is addressed as "brother".

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*[The heading is printed "ವ್ಯಾಕರಣಕ್ರಿಯಾ" in the supplied text; the standard heading is inserted and the printed one kept in kn.md as a note.]*

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses श्रेष्ठः (*śreṣṭhaḥ*) (suffix *iṣṭhan*, Pā. Sū. 5-3-55; the substitution *śra* by Pā. Sū. 5-3-60; Pā. Sū. 6-4-163), यविष्ठः (*yaviṣṭhaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-156) and, on the next page, अजगन् (*ajagan*) (root गमल् (*gamal*); Pā. Sū. 7-4-62 and 8-2-64), ईयते (*īyate*), दूत्यम् (*dūtyam*) (suffix *yat*, Pā. Sū. 4-4-120; Pā. Sū. 6-4-148; Pā. Sū. 6-1-185), ऊचिम (*ūcima*) (root वच (*vaca*), perfect in a present sense; Pā. Sū. 6-1-15), निंदिम (*niṃdima*) (root णिदि (*ṇidi*); Pā. Sū. 6-1-65 and 7-2-35), चमसम् (*camasam*) (root चमु (*camu*), suffix *asac*; Pā. Sū. 6-1-163) and ऊदिम (*ūdima*) (root नद (*nada*) *[so printed]*), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೧ ॥ and a stray ornament line ":-0:-", kept as supplied.

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Two garbled labels ("ಶಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ", "ಕಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") stand inside the Pada block in the supplied text; moved out as notes and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the bhāṣya are joined.]*

> एवं पृष्टोग्निस्तान्प्रत्युवाच । हे सौधन्वनाः सुधन्वनः पुत्राः ययमेकं
> चमसं त्वष्टा संपादितं पूर्वमेकमेव संतं चमसं चतुरः कृणोतन । चतुः
> संख्याकान्कुरुत ॥ चतुरः शसीत्यंतोदात्तत्वं । पूर्ववत्तनप् ॥ यथोपरितने
> ज्येष्ठ आह चमसा द्वा करेतीत्युपक्रम्य कनिष्ठ आह चतुरस्करेतीति चतुष्कं निर्धारितं । ऋ. ४-३३-५ । न केवलमहमेव ब्रवीमि किंतु वो युष्मान्प्रति तदुक्तं
> चतुर्धाकरणरूपं कर्मोत्तरत्र वक्ष्यमाणमश्वरथादिकरणरूपं च कर्म देवा
> इंद्रादयोऽब्रुवन् । देवत्वप्राप्तुपायत्वेन तत्तादृशं कर्म वो युष्माकं
> वक्तुमहमागमं । प्राप्तोऽस्मि । यस्मादेवं तस्माद्यूयं यद्येवा करिष्यथ
> यद्येवं मदुक्तप्रकारेण कर्तुं शक्नुथ तथा चेद्देवैः साकं सह यज्जियासो
> भविष्यथ । इति तैरुक्तमित्यर्थः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Agni answers: O sons of Sudhanvan, make the one ladle four; the gods told you; if you do this you will share the sacrifice with the gods.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सौधन्वनाः (*saudhanvanāḥ*)—O sons of Sudhanvan; एकं चमसं (*ekaṃ camasaṃ*)—this ladle, made (by Tvaṣṭṛ at first) as a single vessel; चतुरस्कृणोतन (*caturaskṛṇotana*)—make it into four vessels (thus); तत् (*tat*)—that work to be done by you; वः (*vaḥ*)—to you; देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the gods; अब्रुवन् (*abruvan*)—said; तत् (*tat*)—therefore; वः (*vaḥ*)—to you; आगमं (*āgamaṃ*)—I have come… *[the Pratipadārtha is cut off here at the end of the supplied text of p. 92; it continues on p. 93, in the next window]*

### Printed p. 93

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ಪ. ೪] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 93 (printed "ಪ. ೪" for the Varga; as supplied).* (The Pratipadārtha of Ṛk 2 continues after "ಆಗಮಂ—I have come".)

यदि (*yadi*)—if; एव (*eva*)—in this manner; करिष्यथ (*kariṣyatha*)—if you do; देवैः साकं (*devaiḥ sākaṃ*)—with the gods; यज्ञियासः (*yajñiyāsaḥ*)—entitled to a share of the sacrifice; भविष्यथ (*bhaviṣyatha*)—you will become.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O sons of Sudhanvan! This ladle, which was first made by Tvaṣṭṛ as a single one, you are to make into four vessels: this is the message of the gods to you. I have come here to make that message known to you. If you carry it out, you too will then become entitled to receive a share of the sacrifice together with the gods.

**Printed English translation**

Make fourfold the single ladle; so the gods comand [sic] you; and for that purpose am I come, sons of Sudhanwan: if you accomplish this, you will be entitled to sacrifices along with the gods,

*[The label of the Special Topics is garbled in the supplied text ("ವಿಶೇಷವಿವರಗಳು"); the standard label is inserted and the garbled line kept in kn.md as a note.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

एवं चमसं चतुरः कृणोतन (*evaṃ camasaṃ caturaḥ kṛṇotana*) *[so printed, "ಏವಂ"]*—Agni conveys the message of the gods: make the ladle, which was first made as one, into four. It is well known that the Ṛbhus, honouring this message, made by their skill the single vessel into four. In this poem too it is well known that this power came to them through the teaching of Tvaṣṭṛ and that they were his favourite pupils. In the same way it is said that in some other ways they showed their extraordinary power and obtained the qualification to drink Soma together with the gods.

सुधन्वन अंगिरसस्यासन्नुत्रास्त्रयः पुरा । (*sudhanvana aṃgirasasyāsannutrāstrayaḥ purā |*)
ऋभुर्विभ्वा च वाजश्च शिष्यास्त्वष्टुश्च तेभवन् ॥ (*ṛbhurvibhvā ca vājaśca śiṣyāstvaṣṭuśca tebhavan ‖*)
शिक्षयामास तांस्वष्टा त्वाष्टं यत्कर्म किंचन । (*śikṣayāmāsa tāṃsvaṣṭā tvāṣṭaṃ yatkarma kiṃcana |*)
परिनिष्ठितकर्माणो विश्वेदेवा उपाह्वयन् ॥ (*pariniṣṭhitakarmāṇo viśvedevā upāhvayan ‖*)
विश्लेषां ते ततश्च कुर्वाहनान्यायुधानि तु । (*viśleṣāṃ te tataśca kurvāhanānyāyudhāni tu |*)
धेनुं सबर्दुघां चक्रुरमृतं सबरुच्यते ॥ (*dhenuṃ sabardughāṃ cakruramṛtaṃ sabarucyate ‖*)
बृहस्पतेरथाश्विभ्यां रथं दिव्यं त्रिवंधुरं । (*bṛhaspaterathāśvibhyāṃ rathaṃ divyaṃ trivaṃdhuraṃ |*)
इंद्राय च हरी देवप्रहितेनाग्निनापि यत् ॥ (*iṃdrāya ca harī devaprahitenāgnināpi yat ‖*)
एकं चमसमित्युक्ते ज्येष्ठ आहेत्यथो दिवि । (*ekaṃ camasamityukte jyeṣṭha āhetyatho divi |*)
उक्त्वा ततक्षुश्चमसन् यथोक्तं तेन हर्षिताः ॥ (*uktvā tatakṣuścamasan yathoktaṃ tena harṣitāḥ ‖*)

### Printed p. 94

*Running head: 94 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. "೧.೬೧" (printed so, for 161).*

त्वष्टा च सविता चैव देवदेवः प्रजापतिः । (*tvaṣṭā ca savitā caiva devadevaḥ prajāpatiḥ |*)
सर्वान्देवान् समामंत्र्य अमृतत्वं ददुश्च ते ॥ (*sarvāndevān samāmaṃtrya amṛtatvaṃ daduśca te ‖*)
तेषामाद्यांत्ययोर्नाम्ना दृश्यते बहुवत् स्तवः । (*teṣāmādyāṃtyayornāmnā dṛśyate bahuvat stavaḥ |*)
तृतीयसवने तेषां तैस्तु भागः प्रकल्पितः ॥ (*tṛtīyasavane teṣāṃ taistu bhāgaḥ prakalpitaḥ ‖*)
अपिबत्तोममिंद्रश्च तैस्तत्र सवने सह । (*apibattomamiṃdraśca taistatra savane saha |*)

(Bṛ. De. 3-83 to 89) *[printed "ಬೃ. ದೇ. ೩-೮೩ ರಿಂದ ೮೯"]*

Before this, the three sons of Sudhanvan, Ṛbhu, Vibhvan and Vāja, became pupils of Tvaṣṭṛ. Tvaṣṭṛ, who was skilled in all the arts, taught them too all the arts. Then the Viśvedevas, who were skilled in the arts, confronted them with an attitude of opposition. Then they made weapons and vehicles for all the gods. Afterwards they created the cow that gives immortality. For Bṛhaspati and the Aśvin deities they made chariots with three seats, for Indra horses, and for Agni, who had been sent by the gods as a messenger, they made, as he had wished, the single ladle into four as well: thus it is explained.

चमसं (*camasaṃ*)—चमसः कस्मात् ? चमंत्यस्मिन्निति । (*camasaḥ kasmāt ? camaṃtyasminniti |*) After asking from which root the word चमस (*camasa*) is formed, the authors of the Nirukta have shown that it is formed from the root चम (*cama*), which means "to eat" (Ni. 11-12).

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses चतुरः (*caturaḥ*) (accent by Pā. Sū. 6-1-167, printed "ಪಾ-. ಸೂ."), कृणोतन (*kṛṇotana*) (root कृवि (*kṛvi*); Pā. Sū. 7-1-45; the *u* augment of धिन्विकृण्योरच (*dhinvikṛṇyoraca*), Pā. Sū. 3-1-80), अब्रुवन् (*abruvan*) (root ब्रूञ् (*brūñ*)), अगमम् (*agamam*) (aorist first person), and, on p. 95, सौधन्वनाः (*saudhanvanāḥ*) (suffix *aṇ*; Pā. Sū. 6-1-198), करिष्यथ (*kariṣyatha*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-33, 8-1-30 and 6-1-186), यज्ञियासः (*yajñiyāsaḥ*) (suffix *gha*; Pā. Sū. 6-4-198 and 7-1-50) and भविष्यथ (*bhaviṣyatha*), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೨ ॥.

### Printed p. 96

*Running head: 96 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The Ṛk 3 bhāṣya begins here. The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಕಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ") and sat inside the block; moved out and the standard label inserted. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> हे आगत देव अग्निमंगनादिगुणविशिष्टं दूतं दूतकर्म प्राप्तवंतं त्वां
> प्रति यत् यत्कार्यमब्रवीतन । अब्रुवन् ॥ व्यत्ययेन मध्यमः । पूर्ववत्तनप् ॥
> किमिति अश्वः कर्त्वः । एकस्मादश्वादश्वांतरं कर्तव्यमित्यर्थः । अश्वादश्वमतक्षतेति वक्ष्यमाणत्वात् ॥ कृत्यार्थे तवैकेनिति त्वन्प्रत्ययः ॥ उतापि च रथः कर्त्वः कर्तव्यः। धेनुः
> कर्त्वा । चर्मरहिता मृता गौर्नित्यदोग्री पुनर्नूतना कार्या । तदुत्तरत्र निश्चर्मणो गामरिणीत धीतिभिरिति वक्ष्यमाणत्वात् । तथा द्वा द्वौ पितरौ जीर्णौ संतौ
> पुनर्युवशा युवान् शयान् प्रकृष्टयौवनोपेत् कर्त्वा कर्तव्या । हे भ्रातरग्ने
> युष्माकं देवानां तैरुक्तानि कर्माणि कृत्वानु पश्चात्कृत्वी कृत्वा कर्मणैमसि ।
> एमः । अभिमुख्येन प्राप्पुमः ॥ इण् गत् । इदंतो मसिः ॥ यद्वा । ऋषेरिदं
> वाक्यं । हे ऋभवः यूयमागतमग्निं दूतं प्रति यदब्रवीतन यत्कार्यं
> वक्ष्यमाणरूपं कर्तव्येन वदथ अंगीकुरुथ तानि सर्वाणि संपाद्य हे भ्रातरग्ने
> कृत्वा कर्मणैमसीति ब्रूथेत्यृषिराह ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): the Ṛbhus ask Agni, the messenger, what is to be made — horse, chariot, cow, or aged parents made young — and say that, having done all, they are ready.]*

*[The Pratipadārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರಕಡಃರ್ಥ") and the Pratipadārtha sat inside the bhāṣya block; moved out, un-quoted, and the standard label inserted. Likewise the Bhāvārtha label ("ಧನಾರ್ಥ") and text.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

भ्रातः (*bhrātaḥ*)—O Agni, in the form of a brother; दूतं (*dūtaṃ*)—who has taken up the office of messenger; अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—and is endowed with qualities such as अंगन (*aṃgana*) *[so printed, "ಅಂಗನಾದಿ"]*—you, about …; यत् (*yat*)—in what work (printed "ಕಾವ್ಯ", as supplied); अब्रवीतन (*abravītana*)—they said (which of the following must be done?); अश्वः (*aśvaḥ*)—the horse; कर्त्वः (*kartvaḥ*)—is it to be made? उत (*uta*)—or; इह (*iha*)—in the present case; रथः (*rathaḥ*)—the chariot; कर्त्वः (*kartvaḥ*)—is it to be made? धेनुः (*dhenuḥ*)—the cow; कर्त्वा (*kartvā*)—is it to be made? द्वा (*dvā*)—both the parents too; युवशा (*yuvaśā*)—as young people; कर्त्वा (*kartvā*)—are they to be made? तानि (*tāni*)—having done all these tasks; अनु (*anu*)—afterwards; वः (*vaḥ*)—as the gods have said to you; कृत्वी (*kṛtvī*)—(in your) work (printed "ಕಾವ್ಯ"); एमसि (*emasi*)—we shall be ready.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, in the form of a brother, who have taken up the office of messenger and are endowed with qualities such as अंगन (*aṃgana*)! Which of the works stated below have the gods sent word to you that we must do? Must a horse be made? Or must a chariot be built in the present case? Must the dead cow be filled with life and made an everlasting milch cow? Or must the aged parents be made young? After doing all these works we shall make the ladle grow as you have said, that is, we shall make what is one into four.

### Printed p. 97

*Running head: [ಆ ೨.ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೪.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 97 (as supplied).*

**Printed English translation**

*[The Latin text breaks "What-ever" over a line, joined here.]*

Then said they, in answer to Agni, the messenger (of the gods)—Whatever is to be done, whether a horse is to be made, or a car is to be made, or a cow is to be made, or the two (old parents) are to be made young,—having done all these (acts), Brother Agni, we are then ready to do (what you desire) to be done.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Even Sāyaṇa has not been able to give the meaning of this ṛk exactly. He too has explained it in two ways: in the first, by construing it as said by the Ṛbhus to Agni; in the second, by construing it as said by the seer of the sūkta to the Ṛbhus themselves. According to the first construction the meaning was given in the Pratipadārtha. According to the second construction, हे ऋभवः यूयमागतमग्निं दूतं प्रति यदब्रवीतन यत्कार्यं वक्ष्यमाण- (*he ṛbhavaḥ yūyamāgatamagniṃ dūtaṃ prati yadabravītana yatkāryaṃ vakṣyamāṇa-*) रूपं कर्तव्यत्वेन वदथ तानि सर्वाणि संपाद्य हे भ्रातरन्ने कृत्वा कर्मणैमसीति (*rūpaṃ kartavyatvena vadatha tāni sarvāṇi saṃpādya he bhrātaranne kṛtvā karmaṇaimasīti*) ब्रूथेत्यृषिराह (*brūthetyṛṣirāha*): "O Ṛbhus, ask Agni, who has come to you as a messenger of the gods, which work you are to do; do those too, and then tell him: O brother Agni, we are ready in the duty that you have stated": this is explained as the sentence said by the seer to the Ṛbhus.

Here the skill in the arts of the Ṛbhus and their filial devotion are described. Since the Ṛbhus are very famous for these qualities, such descriptions occur in all the sūktas seen by seers such as Viśvāmitra and Vāmadeva.

ये अश्विना ये पितरा य ऊती धेनुं ततक्षुर्यभवो ये अश्वा । (*ye aśvinā ye pitarā ya ūtī dhenuṃ tatakṣuryabhavo ye aśvā |*)
ये अंसत्रा य ऋधग्रोदसी ये विभ्वो नरः स्वपत्यानि चक्रुः ॥ (*ye aṃsatrā ya ṛdhagrodasī ye vibhvo naraḥ svapatyāni cakruḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-34-9)

"The Aśvin deities were pleased by your chariot-making. Your parents were pleased because you removed their old age and made them young. You made the dead cow a giver of milk": thus the power of the Ṛbhus is praised. And,

अनवस्ते रथमश्वाय तक्षन् त्वष्टा वज्रं पुरुहूत द्युमंतं ॥ (*anavaste rathamaśvāya takṣan tvaṣṭā vajraṃ puruhūta dyumaṃtaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-31-4)

"O Indra, the Ṛbhus made your excellent chariot": thus their skill in making chariots is praised.

*[Stray signature line "[ 7 ] Vol. 12" at the end of p. 97, kept in kn.md as a note.]*

### Printed p. 98

*Running head: 98 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.*

अश्वः कर्त्वः (*aśvaḥ kartvaḥ*)—There is a special point when it says this: आश्वादश्वमतक्षत (*āśvādaśvamatakṣata*) *[so printed]*, "from one horse they created another horse", is explained later; this is a supernatural power.

धेनुः कर्त्वा (*dhenuḥ kartvā*)—"You made the dead cow, which had no skin, into a cow that gives milk": this is indicated here, as it is explained in the next mantra, निश्चर्मणो गामरिणीत (*niścarmaṇo gāmariṇīta*) (1-161-7).

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses अग्निम् (*agnim*) (root आगि (*āgi*) *[so printed]*; sūtra number printed "ಪಾ. ಸೂ. ೪೯೦"), अब्रवीतन (*abravītana*) (root ब्रूञ् (*brūñ*); Pā. Sū. 3-1-85; Pā. Sū. 7-1-45; Pā. Sū. 7-3-93), कर्त्वः (*kartvaḥ*) (root डुकृञ् (*ḍukṛñ*); Pā. Sū. 3-4-14; Pā. Sū. 6-1-199), युवशा (*yuvaśā*) (root शीज् (*śīj*) *[so printed]*; सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) Pā. Sū. 7-1-39), कृत्वी (*kṛtvī*) and इमसि (*imasi*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-46), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೩ ॥ and a stray ornament line ".:0:", kept as supplied.
