**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> च॒कृ॒वांस॑ ऋभव॒स्तद॑पृच्छत॒ क्वेद॑भू॒द्यः स्य दू॒तो न॒ आज॑गन् ।
> य॒दावाख्य॑च्चम॒साञ्च॒तुरः॑ कृ॒तानादित्त्वष्टा॒ ग्नास्वं॒तर्न्या॑नजे ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> चकृवांसः । ऋभवः । तत् । अपृच्छत । क्व । इत् । अभूत् । यः । स्यः । दूतः । नः ।
> आ । अजगन् ।
> यदा । अव्वअख्यत् । चमसान् । चतुरः । कृतान् । आत् । इत् । त्वष्टा । ग्नासु ।
> अंतः । नि । आनजे ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> हे ऋभवः तत्कार्यं यथा चकृवांसः कृतवंतो यूयमपृच्छत । प्रश्नं
> कुरुत ॥ करोतेर्लिटः क्वसुः ॥ अत्र यद्यप्यभुरिति त्रयाणां मध्य एकस्य नाम
> तथापि तस्य प्राथम्याच्छत्रिणो गच्छंतीतिवत्सर्वेऽप्यभव उच्यंते । किमिति । स्यः स
> दूतः क्वेदभूत् । कुत्र गतः । स इत्युक्तं क इत्याह । यः यो दूतो नोऽस्मानाजगन् आगमत् प्राप्तः । एवंभूते सति यदायं त्वष्टा चतुरः कृतांश्चमसानवाख्यत् अभिपश्यति ॥ ख्यातेर्लुङ्यस्यतिवक्तिख्यातीति चेरणादेशः ॥ आदिदनंतरमेव त्वष्टा
> तस्य चमसस्कोत्पादक एतन्नामको देवो ग्नासु स्त्रेषु । मेना ग्ना इति स्त्रीणामिति
> निरुक्तं । ३-२१ । अंतर्मध्यॆ न्यानजे । व्यक्तोऽभूत् । स्त्रियमात्मनममन्यतेत्यर्थः॥
> अंजेर्लिटि तस्मान्नुड्विहल इति नुट् । छांदसोऽनुनासिकलोपः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): the Ṛbhus asked where the messenger had gone; when Tvaṣṭṛ saw the four ladles, Agni vanished among the women.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus; तत् (*tat*)—the work that was prescribed; चकृवांसः (*cakṛvāṃsaḥ*)—having done (after doing it); यः (*yaḥ*)—the messenger who; नः (*naḥ*)—to us; आ अजगन् (*ā ajagan*)—had come; स्यः दूतः (*syaḥ dūtaḥ*)—that messenger, who had been visible before us; क्वेदभूत् (*kvedabhūt*)—"where has he gone?" (thus); अपृच्छत (*apṛcchata*)—you asked. यदा (*yadā*)—when; चतुरः कृतान् (*caturaḥ kṛtān*)—made into four; चमसान् (*camasān*)—the ladles *[the line is divided between pp. 99 and 100: "ಚಮಸ|ಪಾತ್ರೆಗಳನ್ನು"]*; अवाख्यत् (*avākhyat*)—he saw; आदित् (*ādit*)—immediately; त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ (your teacher); ग्नासु अंतः (*gnāsu aṃtaḥ*)—among the women; न्यानजे (*nyānaje*)—vanished.

### Printed p. 100

*Running head: 100 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus! After doing your prescribed work you asked where Agni, who had come to you as a messenger, had gone. As soon as you had made the single ladle into four, Tvaṣṭṛ, your teacher, vanished among the women.

**Printed English translation**

So doing, Ribhus, you inquired—Where, indeed, is he who came to us as a messenger ? when Twashtri observed the one ladle become four, he was immediately lost amongst the women.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ग्नास्वंतर्न्यानजे (*gnāsvaṃtarnyānaje*)—ग्नासु अंतः न्यानजे । (*gnāsu aṃtaḥ nyānaje |*) "He vanished among the women": Sāyaṇa explains it as स्त्रियमात्मानममन्यत (*striyamātmānamamanyata*), "he regarded himself too as one of the women". But nowhere is it described that Tvaṣṭṛ took a woman's form or a woman's quality; only his moving about in the company of women is described.

उत स्य देवो भुवनस्य सक्षणिस्तष्टा ग्नाभिः सजोषा जूजुवद्रथं । (*uta sya devo bhuvanasya sakṣaṇistaṣṭā gnābhiḥ sajoṣā jūjuvadrathaṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-31-4)

रुद्रो रुद्रेभिर्देवो मृळयाति नस्त्वष्टा नो ग्नाभिः सुविताय जिन्वतु । (*rudro rudrebhirdevo mṛḷayāti nastvaṣṭā no gnābhiḥ suvitāya jinvatu |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-66-3)

In all such ṛks it is understood that Tvaṣṭṛ always kept the company of the wives of the gods. In the same way this point is clear in the ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-22-9, 2-36-3, 6-50-13, 7-35-6, 10-64-10 and others. From this description the scholar Roth conjectures that all of Tvaṣṭṛ's work of creation was accomplished through the wombs of women.

"This, according to professor Roth, S.V., results from the fact that it is in the wombs of females that his creative action is principally manifested." [O. S. T. Vol. V. P. 229]. *[Latin text, as supplied.]*

This conjecture too finds support in some sentences.

### Printed p. 101

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೪.) Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 101 (the closing bracket is printed as a parenthesis, as supplied).*

विष्णुर्योनिं कल्पयतु त्वष्टा रूपाणि पिंशतु । (*viṣṇuryoniṃ kalpayatu tvaṣṭā rūpāṇi piṃśatu |*)
आ सिंचतु प्रजापतिर्धाता गर्भं दधातु ते ॥ (*ā siṃcatu prajāpatirdhātā garbhaṃ dadhātu te ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-184-1)

In such Ṛk-saṃhitā sentences, and in

अथ त्वष्टारं यजति । त्वष्टा वै सिक्त १ रेतो विकरोति । (*atha tvaṣṭāraṃ yajati | tvaṣṭā vai sikta 1 reto vikaroti |*)

(Śa. Brā. 1-9-2-10) *[the word "ಸಿಕ್ತ ೧" is printed so, with a numeral]*

Brāhmaṇa sentences such as this, it is stated that Tvaṣṭṛ makes all his creation by means of the semen sown in the womb, and that his work always needs the help of women. Moreover, since the beauty of women is manifest in all his creation, that point is described as ग्नास्वंतर्न्यानजे (*gnāsvaṃtarnyānaje*).

ग्नासु (*gnāsu*)—This word is read among the names of women, मेना ग्ना (*menā gnā*). ग्ना गच्छंत्येनाः । (*gnā gacchaṃtyenāḥ |*) "Men go to them; therefore women are called ग्ना (*gnā*)": thus the authors of the Nirukta have shown the derivation of this word (Ni. 3-21). It is also read among the names of speech, श्लोकः (*ślokaḥ*), धारा (*dhārā*) and so on (2-23); therefore the sentence ग्नास्वंतर्न्यानजे (*gnāsvaṃtarnyānaje*) can be explained in another way too.

त्वष्टारं यजति वाग्वै त्वष्टा वाग्भीदं सर्वं ताष्टीव वाचमेव तत्प्रीणाति (*tvaṣṭāraṃ yajati vāgvai tvaṣṭā vāgbhīdaṃ sarvaṃ tāṣṭīva vācameva tatprīṇāti*)
वाचं यजमाने दधाति ॥ (*vācaṃ yajamāne dadhāti ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 2-4)

In such sentences, because the identity of speech and Tvaṣṭṛ is stated, and because he is able to describe the small as spread out and the spread-out as small ("even great things such as mountains are taken by a small word, and so this world becomes as if carved", Sāyaṇa's bhāṣya, abbreviated "ಸಾ. ಭಾ."), and has the capacity to create as he likes, this name is well known: so it is said. If that meaning is taken, one may also say that he vanished into speech, which has the power of creation.

त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—तस्य चमसस्कोत्पादक एतन्नामको देवः । (*tasya camasaskotpādaka etannāmako devaḥ |*) The deity of this name, the maker of the ladle. The nature and the greatness of this deity are explained in the special topics of the next sūkta, that is, of the ṛk 1-162-3.

ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—अत्र यद्यपृभुरिति त्रयाणां मध्ये एकस्य नाम तथापि तस्य प्राथ (*atra yadyapṛbhuriti trayāṇāṃ madhye ekasya nāma tathāpi tasya prātha*)म्यात्सर्वेऽपि ऋभव उच्यंते. (*myātsarve 'pi ṛbhava ucyaṃte.*) Though the name Ṛbhu is the name of one of the three, still, by priority, all are called Ṛbhus: it is shown that, although Ṛbhu is only one of the three, by this word the names of all the three are indicated.

### Printed p. 102

*Running head: 102 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1, A. 22, Sū. 161.* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 4 continue.)

ऋभवः उरु भांतीति वा, ऋतेन भांतीति ना, ऋतेन भवंतीति वा (*ṛbhavaḥ uru bhāṃtīti vā, ṛtena bhāṃtīti nā, ṛtena bhavaṃtīti vā*) *[so printed, "ನಾ" for ವಾ]* (Ni. 11-15): "because they shine very widely, or shine with excellent rites such as sacrifice, or exist with such excellent rites, this name is well known": thus the authors of the Nirukta (explain), and

विष्ट्वी शमी तरणित्वेन वाघतो मर्तासः संतो अमृतत्वमानशुः । (*viṣṭvī śamī taraṇitvena vāghato martāsaḥ saṃto amṛtatvamānaśuḥ |*)
सौधन्वना ऋभवः सूरचक्षसः संवत्सरे समपृच्यंत धीतिभिः ॥ (*saudhanvanā ṛbhavaḥ sūracakṣasaḥ saṃvatsare samapṛcyaṃta dhītibhiḥ ‖*)

(ಯ. ಸಂ, 1-110.4) *[printed "ಯ. ಸಂ" with a comma and a stop; the abbreviation is not on the user's list and is kept as printed, presumably Ṛ. Saṃ.]*

quoting this ṛk they describe that the Ṛbhus, uniting with the priests, performed sacrifices and the like very quickly and, though human, obtained by their works divinity free from death. Then the Ṛbhus, sons of Sudhanvan, with a radiance equal to that of the Sun, united with the rites such as the Agniṣṭoma to be performed in the seasons beginning with spring, which are the parts of the year, and became fit to receive oblations: describing their greatness thus, they explain their history with ऋभुर्विभ्वावाज (*ṛbhurvibhvāvāja*) इति सुधन्वन अंगिरसस्य त्रयः पुत्रा बभूवुः । (*iti sudhanvana aṃgirasasya trayaḥ putrā babhūvuḥ |*) This explanation was given earlier.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed):* the Kannada analyses चकृवांसः (*cakṛvāṃsaḥ*) (root डुकृञ् (*ḍukṛñ*), *kvasu* by Pā. Sū. 3-2-107), अपृच्छत (*apṛcchata*) (root प्रच्छ (*praccha*)), स्यः (*syaḥ*) (the pronoun त्यद् (*tyad*)), अजगन् (*ajagan*), अवाख्यत् (*avākhyat*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-52 and 6-4-64), त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*) (root त्वक्षू (*tvakṣū*)) and, on p. 103, आनजे (*ānaje*) (root अंजू (*aṃjū*); Pā. Sū. 7-4-20 and 7-4-21), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೪ ॥.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> हना॑मैना॒ इति॒ त्वष्टा॒ यदब्र॑वीच्चम॒सं ये दे॑व॒पान॒मनिं॑दिषुः ।
> अ॒न्या नामा॑नि कृण्वते सु॒ते सचा॑ अ॒न्यैरे॑नान्क॒न्या॒३॒॑ ३ नाम॑भिः
> स्परत् ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> हनाम । एनान् । इति । त्वष्टा । यत् । अब्रवीत् । चमसं । ये । देवपानं ।
> अनिंदिषुः ।
> अन्या । नामानि । कृण्वते । सुते । सचा । अन्यैः । एनान् । कन्या । नामभिः । स्परत् ।
> ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> एनान्वक्ष्यमाणान् हनामेति यद्यदाब्रवीत्त्वष्टा । पूजार्थं बहुवचनं ।
> एतच्छब्दस्य प्रकृतपरामर्शकत्वादृभूणां प्रकृतत्वात् तेषां हननप्रसक्तिं दर्शयति

### Printed p. 104

*Running head: 104 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 1 (printed "ಮಂ. ೧,"), A. 22, Sū. 161.* (The bhāṣya block continues from p. 103.)

> यदाब्रवीदित्यर्थः । तदाप्रभृति मनुष्याः सुतेऽभिषुते सोमे पानाय
> प्रस्तुते सति सचा सह परस्परमन्या नामान्यन्यानि होत्रध्वर्यूद्गात्रादीनि कृण्वते ।
> कुर्वंति । पूर्वनाम प्रच्छाद्योपहवकालेध्वर्य उपहूयस्व होतरुपहूयस्वेत्येवमन्यैरेव नामभिरात्मानं त्वष्टवधभीत्या प्रख्यापयंतीत्यर्थः । पूर्वमन्यानि कथं प्रसक्तानीत्यत आह । एनान्नरान् कन्या स्वोत्पादयत्रि मातान्वैरेव
> नामभिः स्वरत् । प्रीणयति ॥ स्पृ प्रीतिबलयोः । लेट्यडागमः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Tvaṣṭṛ said: let us slay those who profaned the ladle; the Ṛbhus took other names (hotṛ, adhvaryu) and their mother pleased them by those names.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ये (*ye*)—who; देवपानं (*devapānaṃ*)—fit for the gods' drinking; चमसं (*camasaṃ*)—this ladle; अनिंदिषुः (*aniṃdiṣuḥ*)—(though human, touching the vessel of the gods and making it four) made defiled: such; एनान् (*enān*)—these Ṛbhus; हनाम (*hanāma*)—let us kill; इति (*iti*)—thus; त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Tvaṣṭṛ, who made the ladle; अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*)—said; (then these Ṛbhus) सचा (*sacā*)—mutually; सुते (*sute*)—at the time of the sacrifice at which Soma juice is pressed; अन्या नामानि (*anyā nāmāni*)—different names (the names hotṛ, adhvaryu and the like); कृण्वते (*kṛṇvate*)—take; कन्या (*kanyā*)—the mother who gave birth (to them); अन्नैः नामभिः (*annaiḥ nāmabhiḥ*) *[so printed, "ಅನ್ನೈಃ"]*—by (hotṛ and the like) different names; एनान् (*enān*)—these Ṛbhus; स्वरत् (*svarat*) *[so printed in the Pratipadārtha]*—satisfies.

*[The Bhāvārtha label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಭಾಷಾರ್ಥ"); the standard label is inserted and the garbled word kept in kn.md as a note.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

When the Ṛbhus, who were human, touched the ladle fit for the drinking of the gods and made it defiled, Tvaṣṭṛ, who had made this ladle, tried to kill them. Then the Ṛbhus changed their names and took part in the sacrifices under names such as *hotṛ*. Then the mother satisfied them, who appeared under other names.

**Printed English translation**

When Twashtri said—Let us slay those who have profaned the ladle, (designed) for drinking of the gods; then they made use of other names for one another, as the libatation [sic] was poured out: and the maiden (mother) propitiated them by different appellations.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

हनामैना इति त्वष्टा यदब्रवीत् (*hanāmainā iti tvaṣṭā yadabravīt*)—It is said that Tvaṣṭṛ, angered because they, though human, touched the vessel of the gods and, making what was one into four, defiled it, wished to kill them. For this sentence there are contradictory sentences in two places. First,

### Printed p. 105

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೪. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 105 (as supplied).* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 5 continue.)

न निंदिम चमसं यो महाकुलोग्ने ध्रातर्द्रुण इद्भूतिमूदिम ॥ (*na niṃdima camasaṃ yo mahākulogne dhrātardruṇa idbhūtimūdima ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-161-1): in the very first ṛk of this same sūkta the Ṛbhus have announced that they do not make the vessel of the gods defiled. And besides,

ज्येष्ठ आह चमसा द्वा करेति कनीयान् त्रीन्कृणवामेत्याह । (*jyeṣṭha āha camasā dvā kareti kanīyān trīnkṛṇavāmetyāha |*)
कनिष्ठ आह चतुरस्करेति त्वष्ट ऋभवस्तत्पनयद्वचो वः ॥ (*kaniṣṭha āha caturaskareti tvaṣṭa ṛbhavastatpanayadvaco vaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-33-5): in this ṛk, when Vāja, the youngest of them, said that this ladle would be made into four parts, their teacher Tvaṣṭṛ agreed to it; and

सत्यमूचुनर एवा हि चक्रुरनु स्वधामृभवो जग्मुरेतां । (*satyamūcunara evā hi cakruranu svadhāmṛbhavo jagmuretāṃ |*)
विभ्राजमानांश्च मसा अहेवावेनत्त्वष्टा चतुरो ददृश्वान् ॥ (*vibhrājamānāṃśca masā ahevāvenattvaṣṭā caturo dadṛśvān ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-33-6): here it is clear that Tvaṣṭṛ, seeing the brightness of the ladle made into four parts, was exceedingly delighted and accepted that arrangement, yet it is said that he wished to kill the Ṛbhus.

नामान्यन्यानि कृण्वते (*nāmānyanyāni kṛṇvate*)—होत्रध्वरूद्गात्रादीनि कृण्वते । (*hotradhvarūdgātrādīni kṛṇvate |*) Of the three, one took a name as Hotṛ, another as Adhvaryu and another as Udgātṛ, and, hiding their old names, they took part in the sacrificial rites, it is said.

कन्या (*kanyā*)—this word is explained as स्वोत्पादयित्रि माता । (*svotpādayitri mātā |*), the mother who herself gave birth. Whom is meant here is not clear.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses हनाम (*hanāma*) (root हन (*hana*), first person plural of the *loṭ*), अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*) (root ब्रूञ् (*brūñ*)), देवपानम् (*devapānam*) (root पा (*pā*); Pā. Sū. 6-1-166) and, on p. 106, अनिंदिषुः (*aniṃdiṣuḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-109), कृण्वते (*kṛṇvate*) and स्वरत् (*svarat*) (Pā. ### Printed p. 107

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 107 (as supplied).* (The bhāṣya block continues from p. 106.)

> गामुपाजत । उपागमत् । स्वीकृतवानित्यर्थः । यद्यप्यत्र गौरिति विशेषो न श्रुतः
> तथापि पूर्वं धेनुः कर्त्वेत्युक्तत्वात् पुरस्ताच्च ये धेनुं विश्वजुवं विश्वरूपां।
> ऋ. ४-३३-८ । इतिवक्षमाणत्वाच्च विश्वरूपा गौरीत्येवाध्यवसीयते । अत एव
> विश्वरूपामित्यस्याश्च पंक्तिरिति केचन व्याचक्षते तदसत् । युष्मत्संपादितानि रथावीनींद्रादयः स्वभागत्वेनाकल्पयन् । तस्मादृभुर्विभ्वा वाजश्च यूयं देवानिंद्रादीनगच्छत । गत्वाच हे स्वपसः शोभनाश्वरथादिकर्मवंतो यूयं यज्ञियं
> यज्ञार्हं यज्ञसंबंधिनं भागं सोमरूपं देवत्वं प्राप्नैः सेवनीयमैतन ।
> प्राप्नुत । कर्मसामर्थ्येन हविर्भुजो भवतेत्यर्थः । इत्येवमृषिराह ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Indra, the Aśvins and Bṛhaspati took horses, chariot and cow; so the Ṛbhus are told to go to the gods and take the sacrificial portion.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*[The first word is printed "ಯಭುಗಳ" (for ಋಭುಗಳ) and is kept as supplied.]* (By the skill of the Ṛbhus) इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra; हरी (*harī*)—his horses called Hari; युयुजे (*yuyuje*)—yoked to the chariot; अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—the Aśvin gods; रथं (*rathaṃ*)—(their) chariot (got ready); बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati; विश्वरूपां (*viśvarūpāṃ*)—the cow endowed with many forms; उपाजत (*upājata*)—accepted; (therefore) ऋभुः विभ्वा वाजश्च (*ṛbhuḥ vibhvā vājaśca*)—O Ṛbhu gods, famed by these three names; देवान् (*devān*)—to the gods; अगच्छत (*agacchata*)—go; स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*)—you who have excellent deeds (of making horses and chariots); यज्ञियं (*yajñiyaṃ*)—connected with the sacrifice; भागं (*bhāgaṃ*)—the portion of the Soma juice; ऐतन (*aitana*)—obtain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

By the skill of the Ṛbhus, Indra yoked his horses called Hari to his chariot. The Aśvin gods got their chariot ready. Bṛhaspati accepted the cow endowed with many forms. Therefore, O Ṛbhu gods, famed by these three names, go to the gods. You who have the skill of making excellent horses and chariots, obtain the portion of Soma juice that belongs to the sacrifice.

**Printed English translation**

Indra has caparisoned his horses: the Ashwins have harnessed their cars : Brihaspathi has accepted the omniform (cow): therefore, Ribhu, Vibhwa, and Vaja, go to the gods, doers of good deeds, enjoy your sacrificial portion.

### Printed p. 108

*Running head: 108 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಆ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧] (as supplied; "ಆ." printed for "ಅ."; the opening bracket is placed after "sahitā").*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

*(Ṛk 6, continued from the previous window.)* Described here are the supernatural skill of the Ṛbhus and their excellent works, the means by which they attained a rank equal to that of the gods and bear the share of the sacrifice together with them. It is told that with their help Indra obtained an excellent chariot and the horses fit for it.

इंद्रो हरी (*iṃdro harī*)—Indra yoked to his chariot his horses called Hari. हरी इंद्यस्य (*harī iṃdyasya*) *[so printed]* (Ni. 2-28): thus the name of Indra's horses is well known.

तक्षनथं सुवृतं विद्मनापसस्तक्षन्हरी इंद्रवाहा वृषण्वसू । (*takṣanathaṃ suvṛtaṃ vidmanāpasastakṣanharī iṃdravāhā vṛṣaṇvasū |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-111-1)

अनवस्ते रथमश्वाय तक्षन् त्वष्टा षज्रं पुरुहूत द्युमंतं । (*anavaste rathamaśvāya takṣan tvaṣṭā ṣajraṃ puruhūta dyumaṃtaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-31-4)

इंद्राय च हरी । (*iṃdrāya ca harī |*) (ಬೃ. ದೇ. 3-85) *[the abbreviation "ಬೃ. ದೇ." is not on the user's list and is kept as printed]*

thus it is well known that the Ṛbhus, by their excellent skill, made the horses called Hari, strong of body and the carriers of Indra. In the same way,

बृहस्पतेरथाश्विभ्यां रथं दिव्यं त्रिवंधुरं (*bṛhaspaterathāśvibhyāṃ rathaṃ divyaṃ trivaṃdhuraṃ*) (ಬೃ. ದೇ. 3-85)

says that a chariot was built and given to Bṛhaspati and to the Aśvin gods. Here it is said बृहस्पतिर्विश्वरूपां (*bṛhaspatirviśvarūpāṃ*) (the next word, "ಉಪಾಜತ", is split over two lines in the print). Sāyaṇa has explained विश्वरूपां (*viśvarūpāṃ*) as नानारूपोपेतां गां । (*nānārūpopetāṃ gāṃ |*), a cow of many colours.

यद्यप्यत्र गौरिति विशेषो न श्रुतः तथापि पूर्वं धेनुः कर्त्वा इत्युक्तत्वात् (*yadyapyatra gauriti viśeṣo na śrutaḥ tathāpi pūrvaṃ dhenuḥ kartvā ityuktatvāt*)

परस्ताच्च ये धेनुं विश्वजुवं विश्वरूपां इति वक्ष्यमाणत्वाच्च विश्वरूपा गौः इत्येवाध्यवसीयते ॥ (*parastācca ye dhenuṃ viśvajuvaṃ viśvarūpāṃ iti vakṣyamāṇatvācca viśvarūpā gauḥ ityevādhyavasīyate ‖*) *[the line-end hyphen "ಇತ್ಯೇ-ವಾಧ್ಯವಸೀಯತೇ" is joined]*

Here the word "cow" as the thing qualified is absent; still, because earlier the making of a cow has been stated (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-161-3) and later too the creation of a cow is told by "viśvarūpā", Sāyaṇa has settled the doubt by saying that here too the meaning must be understood as a cow.

वृषभं चर्षणीनां विश्वरूपमदाभ्यं । बृहस्पतिं वरेण्यं । (*vṛṣabhaṃ carṣaṇīnāṃ viśvarūpamadābhyaṃ | bṛhaspatiṃ vareṇyaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-62-6)

In ṛks of this kind the word "viśvarūpa" is an epithet of Bṛhaspati himself. According to the Nirukta sentence विश्वरूपा बृहस्पतेः (*viśvarūpā bṛhaspateḥ*) (Ni. 2-28) it is understood that "Viśvarūpa" is the name of Bṛhaspati's horse.

रथं ये चक्रुः सुवृतं नरेष्ठां ये धेनुं विश्वजुवं विश्वरूपां । (*rathaṃ ye cakruḥ suvṛtaṃ nareṣṭhāṃ ye dhenuṃ viśvajuvaṃ viśvarūpāṃ |*)
त आ तक्षन्त्वभवो रयिं नः स्ववसः स्वपसः सुहस्ताः ॥ (*ta ā takṣantvabhavo rayiṃ naḥ svavasaḥ svapasaḥ suhastāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-33-8)

### Printed p. 109

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 109 (as supplied).*

Here it is clear that the Ṛbhus created for Bṛhaspati also a cow called "viśvarūpā". That point is told here too.

From the description of the peculiarities of the chariot of the Aśvin gods, the skill in construction of the Ṛbhus who made it is known. Epithets such as त्रिचक्रः, मधुवाहनः, त्रिवंधुरः (*tricakraḥ, madhuvāhanaḥ, trivaṃdhuraḥ*) show the peculiarity of their chariot. The description of the chariot of the Aśvin gods, which shows the Ṛbhus' skill in construction, is given at great length in the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā.

निमिषश्चिज्जवीयसा रथेना यातमश्विना । अंति षद्भूतु नामवः ॥ (*nimiṣaścijjavīyasā rathenā yātamaśvinā | aṃti ṣadbhūtu nāmavaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-73-2)

In this the chariot of the Aśvin gods is said to be able to run faster than the blink of an eye, and in

प्र नां रथो मनोजवा इयर्ति तिरो रजांस्यश्विना शतोतिः । (*pra nāṃ ratho manojavā iyarti tiro rajāṃsyaśvinā śatotiḥ |*)
अस्मभ्यं सूर‍्यावसू इयानः । (*asmabhyaṃ sūryāvasū iyānaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-68-3)

and other places its greatness is praised as able to travel through all worlds and as protecting all worlds; and

आ तेन यातं मनसो जवीयसा रथं यं वामृभवश्च क्रुरश्विना । (*ā tena yātaṃ manaso javīyasā rathaṃ yaṃ vāmṛbhavaśca kruraśvinā |*)
यस्य योगे महिता जायते दिव उभे अहनी सुदिने विवस्वतः ॥ (*yasya yoge mahitā jāyate diva ubhe ahanī sudine vivasvataḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-39-12)

in this the chariot of the Aśvin gods made by the Ṛbhus is swifter than thought; as soon as it is yoked with horses, Uṣas, the daughter of heaven, appears and welfare comes to the world: thus it is described as a means not merely of the beauty of the chariot but also of the welfare of the world.

All this is the greatness of the Ṛbhus' skill in making. The Ṛbhus, who have such divine power and who display their influence by their excellent works throughout earth and atmosphere in a manner astonishing even to the gods, are prayed to join the gods in order to take part in the sacrifice.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* युयुजे (*yuyuje*) (root युजिर् (*yujir*), a *liṭ* form, with the rule Pā. Sū. 8-1-63 on the prohibition of the *nighāta* accent); on p. 110 follow आश्विना (*āśvinā*) *[so printed, with long ā]* (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39), बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-140), विश्वरूपां (*viśvarūpāṃ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-106), आजत (*ājata*) (root अज (*aja*)), अगच्छत (*agacchata*) (root गमल्य (*gamalya*) *[so printed]*), स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*) (a sūtra whose number is split over two lines) and ऐतन (*aitana*) (root इण् (*iṇ*); Pā. Sū. 7-1-45 and 6-4-22 as printed), with the accent rules; it ends with the mark ॥ ೬ ॥.

### Printed p. 111

*Running head: [ಆ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೫ ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 111 (as supplied: "ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಪೀತಾ" is printed for "ಋಗ್ವೇದಸಂಹಿತಾ"; the head had been merged into the Pada block).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> हे सौधन्वनाः यूयं निश्चर्मणो मृताया गोः सकाशादुत्कत्ताच्चर्मणो गां नूतनां निररिणीत । निरगमयत । उत्पादितवंत इत्यर्थः । तेनैव
> चर्मणा संवीतां तत्सदृशीमन्यां धेनुं कृतवंत इत्यर्थः । यद्यपि पूर्वमेवाश्वादीनां विनियोग उक्तः तथापि तेषामत्यंतदुःखसंपादत्वज्ञापनाय पुनराह ।
> न केवलं गोकरणमेव अपि च या जरंता यौ जीर्णौ पितरौ ता तौ युवशा
> युवानावकृषोतन । अकुरुत । किंचाश्वादश्वमेकेन विद्यमानेनाश्वेनाश्वांतरमतक्षत । प्रयत्नेन संपादितवंतः । यस्मादेवं कृतवंतस्तस्माद्रथं युष्मदीयं
> युक्ताश्वैर्योजयित्वा देवानिंद्रादीनुपायातन । देवसमीपं प्राप्तुत ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Sudhanvan's sons made a cow from a hide, rejuvenated their parents, made a horse from a horse, and must harness their chariot.]*

*[The label of the Pratipadārtha is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರಸಿಹವಾರ್ಥ") and the text stood inside the bhāṣya block; the garbled word is kept in kn.md as a note, the standard label is inserted and the text moved out of the block.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सौधन्वनाः (*saudhanvanāḥ*)—O sons of Sudhanvan; निश्चर्मणः (*niścarmaṇaḥ*)—from the hide of a dead cow; गां (*gāṃ*)—a (new) cow; अरिणीत (*ariṇīta*)—you made; धीतिभिः (*dhītibhiḥ*)—by your works full of power; या (*yā*)—the parents who; जरंता (*jaraṃtā*)—were aged; ता (*tā*)—them; युवशा (*yuvaśā*)—as young; अकृणोतन (*akṛṇotana*)—you made; अश्वात् (*aśvāt*)—from one horse (that existed before); अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*)—another horse; अतक्षत (*atakṣata*)—you made; रथं (*rathaṃ*)—your chariot (*the printed "!" after it is a misprint for the stop*); युक्ता (*yuktā*)—having got it ready with the horses; देवान् (*devān*)—to the gods, Indra and the others; आयातन (*āyātana*)—go and join.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, sons of Sudhanvan, you created a new cow from the hide of a dead cow. By your works full of power you made your aged parents young. From the one horse that existed before you made another horse. Having got your chariot ready with the horses, go and join Indra and the other gods.

**Printed English translation**

Sons of Sudhanwan, from a hideless (cow) you have formed a living one: by your marvellous acts you have made your aged parents young: from one horse you have fabricated another: harness now your chariot, and repair unto the gods.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Anticipating the doubt that explaining here the matters described in this ṛk, which have been told before, would be a repetition, Sāyaṇa says यद्यपि पूर्वमेवाश्वादीनां (*yadyapi pūrvamevāśvādīnāṃ*)

### Printed p. 112

*Running head: 112 ಕಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೮ (as supplied: "ಕಾಯಣ" for "ಸಾಯಣ" and "೧೬೮" for "೧೬೧"; the head stood in the middle of the sentence and its page mark was missing, so it is supplied).*

विनियोग उक्तस्तथापि तेषामत्यंतदुःखसंपादित्वज्ञापनाय पुनराह । (*viniyoga uktastathāpi teṣāmatyaṃtaduḥkhasaṃpāditvajñāpanāya punarāha |*) *[so printed, "ಸಂಪಾದಿತ್ವ"; the bhāṣya block has "ಸಂಪಾದತ್ವ"]* He has given the reply that, although these were indicated earlier, such works cannot be accomplished by ordinary power and extraordinary power is needed to carry out such toilsome work, and that for this reason they are explained again here.

Here one more point is to be noted. The root तक्ष् (*takṣ*), which is commonly used to denote the making-work of Tvaṣṭṛ, the teacher of the Ṛbhus, is used here too, in the verb of the third pāda ("from one horse another"; the verb is split over two lines in the print). Besides, in the same way in ṛks such as तक्षन्रथं सुवृतं (*takṣanrathaṃ suvṛtaṃ*) (1-111-1) and त आ तक्षन्त्वभवो रयिं (*ta ā takṣantvabhavo rayiṃ*) (4-33-8) also the creative work of the Ṛbhus is told with this root: this is another special feature.

धीतिभिः (*dhītibhiḥ*)—the word "dhīti" is included both among the names of works and among the names of wisdom, and since the distinction of the Ṛbhus is made manifest by both these qualities, either meaning can be given here.

### Printed p. 114

*Running head: 114 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).* (The bhāṣya block continues from p. 113.)

> वेदं वाक्यं । हे सौधन्वनाः यूयं देवत्वं प्राप्य देवयजनं प्रति सोमपानाय रथेनागत्य श्रांतानश्वान् प्रात्सवन इदमुदकं सोमलक्षणं पिबतेत्यब्रवीतन । तत्र यदि नेच्छथ इदं वा मुंजनेजनं मुंजेन दूषितं वाजिनं पिबतेत्यब्रवीतन पश्चात्तद्वयं यदि नेव हर्यथ हे अश्वाः यूयं तृतीये सवने
> माधयाध्यै । तत्रापि वाजिनप्रचारात् ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): the Ṛbhus are told to drink Soma at the morning pressing, or the midday one, or else rejoice at the third.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सौधन्वनाः (*saudhanvanāḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, sons of Sudhanvan; इदं उदकं (*idaṃ udakaṃ*)—this water in the form of Soma (of the morning pressing); पिबत (*pibata*)—drink; इति (*iti*)—thus; अब्रवीतन (*abravītana*)—(they) said; वा (*vā*) ((घ) (*(gha)*))—or; मुंजनेजनं (*muṃjanejanaṃ*)—pure, separated from the grass that grew together with Soma on Mount Muñja; इदं (*idaṃ*) ((घ) (*(gha)*))—at least this water in the form of juice; पिबत (*pibata*)—drink; यदि (*yadi*)—if perchance; तत् (*tat*)—that too; नेव हर्यथ (*neva haryatha*)—you do not desire; तृतीये घ सवने (*tṛtīye gha savane*)—at least at the third pressing; मादयाध्यै (*mādayādhyai*) *[so printed in the Pratipadārtha]*—drink and be satisfied.

*[The label of the Bhāvārtha is garbled in the supplied text ("ಭಾನುವಾರ"); the standard label is inserted and the garbled word kept in kn.md as a note.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, sons of Sudhanvan, drink this water in the form of Soma at the morning pressing. Or drink the juice of the midday pressing, which is separated from the Muñja grass and pure. Or, if that too does not please you, drink at least at the third pressing and be satisfied.

**Printed English translation**

They, (the gods), have said, Sons of Sudhanwan, drink of this water, (the Soma juice); or drink that which has been filtered through the Munja grass; or if you be pleased with neither of these, be exhilarated (by that which is drunk) at the third (daily) sacrifice-

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Two kinds of construction are given for this ṛk. First, the seer of the sūkta, addressing the gods, says to them that they gave the Ṛbhus an invitation to drink Soma: "you said thus to the Ṛbhus". Second, addressing the Ṛbhus themselves, he says that after they came in their chariot for the Soma-drinking at the divine sacrifice they urged their horses to drink: "you said thus to your horses".

The saying three times of इदमुदकं पिबत ; इदं वा पिबत ; तृतीये सवने मादयध्वै । (*idamudakaṃ pibata ; idaṃ vā pibata ; tṛtīye savane mādayadhvai |*) is shown, with एतत्प्रातस्सवनिकाभिप्रायं (*etatprātassavanikābhiprāyaṃ*) ; एतन्माध्यंदिन सवनाभिप्रायं (*etanmādhyaṃdina savanābhiprāyaṃ*) (so printed) and so on, as the context

### Printed p. 115

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೫.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 115 (as supplied).*

for the first two sentences. It is stated that the word मादयध्वै (*mādayadhvai*) is used because in the third pressing a place was obtained by them. The situation in which they first twice did not get an opportunity is described thus:

यभवो वै देवेषु तपसा सोमपीथमभ्यजयंस्तेभ्यः प्रातस्सवने वाचि (*yabhavo vai deveṣu tapasā somapīthamabhyajayaṃstebhyaḥ prātassavane vāci*)
कल्पयिषंस्तानग्निर्वसुभिःप्रातस्सवनादनुदत । (*kalpayiṣaṃstānagnirvasubhiḥprātassavanādanudata |*)
(Ai. Brā. 3-30) *[the first word is printed "ಯಭವೋ" for "ಋಭವೋ"; kept as supplied]*

The Ṛbhus attained, by their supernatural power of works, the place of the gods. Then, when the gods made ready to admit them also with themselves to the Soma-drinking, Agni, joining with the Vasus, drove them away without admitting them.

तेभ्यो माध्यंदिने सवने वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तानिंद्रो रुद्रॆर्माध्यंदिनात्सवनादनुदत । (*tebhyo mādhyaṃdine savane vāci kalpayiṣaṃstāniṃdro rudrèrmādhyaṃdinātsavanādanudata |*)
(Ai. Brā. 3-30) *[the hyphenated line break "ಮಾಧ್ಯಂದಿನಾ-ತ್ಸವನಾದನುದತ" is joined]*

Thereafter, when the gods made ready to make an opening for them at the midday pressing, Indra, joining with the Rudras, drove them away.

तेभ्यस्तृतीयसवने वाचि कल्पयिषंस्तान्विश्वेदेवाः अनोनुद्यंत नेह पास्यंति (*tebhyastṛtīyasavane vāci kalpayiṣaṃstānviśvedevāḥ anonudyaṃta neha pāsyaṃti*)
नेहेति स प्रजापतिरब्रवीत्सवितारं तव ना इमे०तेवासास्त्वमेवैभिः (*neheti sa prajāpatirabravītsavitāraṃ tava nā ime0tevāsāstvamevaibhiḥ*)
संपिबस्वेति स तथेत्यब्रवीत्सविता तान्वै त्वमुभयतः परिपिबेति तान्प्रजापतिरुभयतः पर्यपिबत् । (*saṃpibasveti sa tathetyabravītsavitā tānvai tvamubhayataḥ paripibeti tānprajāpatirubhayataḥ paryapibat |*)
(Ai. Brā. 3-30) *[the hyphenated line break "ತಾನ್ಪ್ರಜಾಪತಿ-ರುಭಯತಃ" is joined]*

Even then the Viśvedevas did not admit them, and Prajāpati, addressing Savitṛ, said: "These are your pupils; drink with them." Then Savitṛ, and with him Prajāpati, drank together with the Ṛbhus. That matter is told here.

घ-घेत्यनर्थकः, प्रसिद्ध्यर्थो वा । (*gha-ghetyanarthakaḥ, prasiddhyartho vā |*) The word "ಘ" is commonly used without meaning, only to fill out the pāda; in some places it indicates the sense "well known".

प्र घा नृस्य महतो महानि सत्या सत्यस्य करणानि वोचं । (*pra ghā nṛsya mahato mahāni satyā satyasya karaṇāni vocaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-15-1)

in this, घ इति प्रसिद्धौ (*gha iti prasiddhau*) is explained as the well-known great deeds of Indra.

आ घा गमद्यदि श्रवत्सहस्रिणीभिरूतिभिः । वाजेभिरुप नो हवं ॥ (*ā ghā gamadyadi śravatsahasriṇībhirūtibhiḥ | vājebhirupa no havaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-30-8)

### Printed p. 116

*Running head: 116 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧. ೬೧] (as supplied: the sūkta number is printed "೧. ೬೧").* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 8 continue.)

In the ṛk quoted at the end of the previous window the meaning "certainly" is given for the word "ಘ": if Indra hears our prayer he will certainly come. In the present ṛk it may be understood as indicating a limit: "at least drink this".

मुंजनेजनं (*muṃjanejanaṃ*)—मुंजन्नाम पर्वतः । सोमोत्पत्तिस्थानं । तत आहृतत्वात्तेन (*muṃjannāma parvataḥ | somotpattisthānaṃ | tata āhṛtatvāttena*) मुंजतृणेन शोधितं । अपगततृणमित्यर्थः । (*muṃjatṛṇena śodhitaṃ | apagatatṛṇamityarthaḥ |*) The Kannada explains: brought from the mountain called Muñjavān, where it was mixed with grass and the like, it was, after being brought from there, cleared of those impurities and purified; that is the meaning. The Nirukta authors, having quoted the ṛk

प्रावेपा मा बृहतो मादयंति प्रवातेजा इरिणे वर्वृतानाः । (*prāvepā mā bṛhato mādayaṃti pravātejā iriṇe varvṛtānāḥ |*)
सोमस्येव मौजवतस्य भक्षो विभीवको जागृविर्मह्यमच्छान् ॥ (*somasyeva maujavatasya bhakṣo vibhīvako jāgṛvirmahyamacchān ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-34-1)

(Ni. 9-8), have explained this mountain and the nature of the Soma produced on it, where they compare the taste of the fruit of the vibhīvaka tree with the sweetness of the juice of the Soma plant produced on the Mauja mountain.

मौजवतो मूजवति जातः । मूजवान् पर्वतो मुंजवान् । मुंजो (*maujavato mūjavati jātaḥ | mūjavān parvato muṃjavān | muṃjo*)
विमुच्यत इषीकया (*vimucyata iṣīkayā*) (Ni. 9-8). मौजवतः (सोमस्य) (*maujavataḥ (somasya)*) means produced on the Muñjavat mountain. Because it is filled with the grass called muñja, that mountain is well known by the name Mūjavat. It is explained that the Soma plant grows on that mountain and has a juice of the greatest sweetness. The present ṛk says that it is pure, being filtered through that grass.

मादयाध्वै (*mādayādhvai*)—for the drinking at both times, the morning pressing and the midday pressing, the expression "pibata" (drink) is used, while the use of "mādayādhvai" for the drinking of the third pressing shows the special character of the third pressing. This intoxicating satisfaction is possible only through the drinking of the third pressing. Because this word was once used also for the drinking of the midday pressing, an objection arose, and an answer to it has been given:

असावि देवं गोऋजीकमंध इति मध्यंदिन उन्नीयमानेभ्योऽन्वाह वृषण्वतीः पीतवतीः सुतवतीर्मद्वती रूपसमृद्धाः । ऐंद्रीरन्वाहैंद्रो वै (*asāvi devaṃ goṛjīkamaṃdha iti madhyaṃdina unnīyamānebhyo 'nvāha vṛṣaṇvatīḥ pītavatīḥ sutavatīrmadvatī rūpasamṛddhāḥ | aiṃdrīranvāhaiṃdro vai*)
यज्ञस्त्रिष्टुभोऽन्वाह त्रैष्टुभं वै माध्यंदिनं सवनं । तदाहुर्यत्तृतीय (*yajñastriṣṭubho 'nvāha traiṣṭubhaṃ vai mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ | tadāhuryattṛtīya*)
सवनस्यॆव रूपं मद्वदथ कस्मान्माध्यंदिने मद्वतीरनु चाह यजंति (*savanasyèva rūpaṃ madvadatha kasmānmādhyaṃdine madvatīranu cāha yajaṃti*)
चाभिरिति । माद्यंतीव वै मध्यंदिने देवताः समेव तृतीयसवने मादयंते तस्मान्मध्यंदिने मद्वतीरनु चाह यजंति चाभिः ॥ (*cābhiriti | mādyaṃtīva vai madhyaṃdine devatāḥ sameva tṛtīyasavane mādayaṃte tasmānmadhyaṃdine madvatīranu cāha yajaṃti cābhiḥ ‖*)
(ಇ. ಬ್ರ. 6-10) *[the abbreviation "ಇ. ಬ್ರ." is printed so, without the long ā; presumably the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, as the Kannada on p. 117 says; kept as printed. The hyphenated line breaks "ವೃಷ-ಣ್ವತೀಃ" and "ಮಾದ-ಯಂತೇ" are joined.]*

### Printed p. 117

*Running head: [ಅ ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 117 (as supplied).*

At the midday pressing the Adhvaryu

असावि देवं गोयजीकमंधो न्यस्मिन्निंद्रो जनुषेमुवोच । (*asāvi devaṃ goyajīkamaṃdho nyasminniṃdro januṣemuvoca |*)
बोधामसि त्वा हर्यश्व यज्ञैर्बोधा नः स्तोममंधसो मदेषु ॥ (*bodhāmasi tvā haryaśva yajñairbodhā naḥ stomamaṃdhaso madeṣu ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-21-1)

should recite the sūkta that begins with this ṛk. In this first mantra the nature of intoxication is stated by अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*) मदेषु (*madeṣu*). But since all the gods obtain joy at the time of the Soma-drinking of the third pressing, the question arises why a ṛk with this word is recited at the time of the midday pressing. However, since the gods drink Soma at the midday pressing too, intoxication comes to them without fail; but at the third pressing, संमादयंते (*saṃmādayaṃte*) they are exceedingly and greatly delighted by the abundance of the Soma-drinking. That is the only difference, so there is no objection: thus it is explained in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa. In the present ṛk the word मादयाध्यॆ (*mādayādhyè*) *[so printed]* is used to indicate the greater joy of the third pressing. What is the nature of this joy?

अपादिंद्रो अपादग्निर्विश्वे देवा अमत्सत । (*apādiṃdro apādagnirviśve devā amatsata |*)
वरुण इदिह क्षयत्तमापो अभ्यनूषत वत्सं संशिश्वरीरिव ॥ (*varuṇa idiha kṣayattamāpo abhyanūṣata vatsaṃ saṃśiśvarīriva ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-69-11)

Here the joy accompanied by the intoxication produced by the Soma-drinking of Indra, Agni, Varuṇa and the other gods, and expressed in sound, is compared with the joyful expression of a cow that, seeing her calf, runs towards it making sound. The Ṛbhus are prayed to become sharers in such intoxicating joy.

हरथ (*haratha*) *[so printed, "(ನೇವ) ಹರಥ", for "ಹರ್ಯಥ"]*—हरतिः कांतिकर्मा (*haratiḥ kāṃtikarmā*) (Ni. 3-6), हरतिः प्रेप्साकर्मा विहरतीति (*haratiḥ prepsākarmā viharatīti*) (Ni. 7-17). Because it is very attractive, it gives the meaning "desire that thing". When showing the derivation of the word "hiraṇya" the Nirukta authors say हरतेर्वा स्यात् प्रेप्साकर्मणः (*haratervā syāt prepsākarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 2-10), and have shown that the name "hiraṇya" is well known because of the above meaning. In the present ṛk its negative use occurs. If you do not desire the Soma-drinking of the morning and midday pressings, you are prayed to obtain the drinking of the third pressing, which gives more joy than they, and to gain satisfaction in it.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses पिबत (*pibata*) (root पा (*pā*), with the rule Pā. Sū. 7-3-78 on p. 118), अब्रवीतन (*abravītana*) (root ब्रूञ् (*brūñ*); Pā. Sū. 7-3-93), घा (*ghā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-133), हर्यथ (*haryatha*) (root हर्य (*harya*); with the remark of Yāska हर्यति उशिक् (*haryati uśik*), printed reference "ನಿರು. 2-6-10") and मादयाध्वै (*mādayādhvai*) (root मद (*mada*); Pā. Sū. 3-4-79, 3-4-94, 3-4-96 and 3-1-25), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೮ ॥.

#### Ṛk 9

> आपो॒ भूयि॑ष्ठा॒ इत्येको॑ अब्रवीद॒ग्निर्भूयि॑ष्ठ॒ इत्य॒न्नो॑
> अ॑ब्रवीत् ।
> व॒ध॒र्यंतीं॑ ब॒हुभ्यः॒ प्रैको॑ अब्रवीदृ॒ता वदं॑तश्चम॒सा॑
> अपिंशत ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> आपः । भूयिष्ठाः । इति । एकः । अब्रवीत् । अग्निः । भूयिष्ठः । इति । अन्यः ।
> अब्रवीत् ।
> वधःयंतीं । बहुभ्यः । प्र । एकः । अब्रवीत् । ऋता । वदंतः । चमसान् ।

> *vadhaḥyaṃtīṃ | bahubhyaḥ | pra | ekaḥ | abravīt | ṛtā | vadaṃtaḥ | camasān |*

### Printed p. 119

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 119 (as supplied).* (The Ṛk 9 Saṃhitā and Pada are at the end of the previous window.)

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[The label is garbled in the supplied text ("ಶಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಂ", inside the block); the standard label is inserted and the garbled word kept in kn.md as a note. Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined, and the Latin letter "S" in "ವಧೋS-ರ್ಕ" is read as the avagraha (ಽ).]*

> चमसचतुर्धाकरणकाले किमिति सत्यं वदंतो व्यभजन्निति तदाह । एकस्त्रयाणामन्यतम आपो भूयिष्ठा इत्यब्रवीत् । न ह्युदकात्पशस्त्रं लोकोपकारकं
> तत्त्वांतर मस्ति । आपो भूयिष्ठा इत्यृतमवादीत् । अपामेव श्रेष्ठत्वमप एव
> ससर्जादौ । मनु. १-८ । इत्यादिशास्त्रात् । तथान्योऽग्निर्भूयिष्ठ इत्यब्रवीत् ।
> आंतरं बाह्यं च दाहपाकभुक्तजरणादिव्यापारेणाग्नॆरेव जगन्निर्वाहकत्वादग्नेरेव
> भूयिष्ठत्वमित्येव मन्यते। तथा वर्धयंतीं! वधरिति रेफांतोऽपि वज्रनाम वधोऽर्क इति तन्नामसु पाठात् । तदिच्छति वृष्टुदकायेति वर्धयंती मेघपंक्तिरुच्यते।
> यद्यपीयं स्ववधार्थं वज्रं स्वयमेव नापेक्षते तथापि वृष्ट्यर्थमिंद्रवज्रेण
> हन्यमानत्वात् तदिच्छंतीत्युपचर्यते । यद्वा । बहुभ्यस्तेषामर्थाय वर्धयंती
> वधमात्मन इच्छंतीं भूमिं ॥ छांदसो रेफोपजनः ॥ तामेवैको बहुभ्यः
> संवादिभ्यः श्रेष्ठतमामब्रवीत् । उदकस्यापि मेघकारणत्वात् । एवमृता ऋतान्युक्तरूपाणि यथार्थानि पाक्यानि वदंतः परस्परं ब्रुवंतश्चमसानपिंशत । अवयविनोऽकुरुत । चतुर्धा व्यभजतेत्यर्थः ॥ पिश अवयवे । शे मुचादीनामिति नुम् ॥
> इदमेवर्तवदनमपेक्ष्यभुशब्दं व्याचक्षणो यास्कः । ऋभव उरु भांतीति
> वर्तेन भांतीति वर्तेन भवंतीति वा । नि. ११-१५ । इत्युक्तत्वात् ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): one Ṛbhu called water the best, another fire, another the earth (the rain-cloud), each giving reasons; speaking true things they divided the ladle in four.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

आपः (*āpaḥ*)—water; भूयिष्ठा (*bhūyiṣṭhā*)—most praiseworthy (the most excellent of all); इति (*iti*)—thus; एकः (*ekaḥ*)—one of the Ṛbhus; अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*)—said; अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—fire; भूयिष्ठः (*bhūyiṣṭhaḥ*)—the most excellent; इति (*iti*)—thus; अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—another; अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*)—said; एकः (*ekaḥ*)—the third; वर्धयंतीं (*vardhayaṃtīṃ*) *[printed with a stray stop before it]*—the cloud or the earth; प्र (*pra*)—as more excellent; बहुभ्यः (*bahubhyaḥ*)—to all who were in dialogue; अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*) *[the hyphenated line break "ಅಬ್ರ-ವೀತ್" is joined]*—said; (एवं (*evaṃ*)—in this way) ऋता (*ṛtā*)—true matters; वदंतः (*vadaṃtaḥ*)—making clear and explaining; चमसान् (*camasān*)—(the one ladle); अपिशंत (*apiśaṃta*) *[so printed in the Pratipadārtha]*—divided so that it became four parts (four).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

One of the Ṛbhus said that water is the most excellent principle. Another said that fire is the best of all. The third said, to all the others who were in the dialogue there, that the earth is the most excellent of all. Thus, all of them, explaining true matters to one another, divided the single ladle so that it became four parts.

### Printed p. 120

*Running head: 120 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧] (as supplied).*

**Printed English translation**

Waters are the most excellent, said one (of them): Agni is the most excellent, said another : the third declared to many the Earth (to be the most excellent), and thus speaking true things the Ribhus divided the ladle.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The Ṛbhus, famed for their excellent works (स्वपसः (*svapasaḥ*)), have in this ṛk also revealed their excellent philosophical knowledge. One has told his view that water is the excellent principle, another that it is fire, the third that it is the earth. The subject of this dialogue is not what is the original principle of the whole world, but what is the most excellent principle in the whole world. But Sāyaṇa, by अपामेव श्रेष्ठत्वमप एव ससर्जादावित्यादिशास्त्रात् । (*apāmeva śreṣṭhatvamapa eva sasarjādāvityādiśāstrāt |*) *[so printed, "ಸಸರ್ಜಾದಾವಿ"; the bhāṣya block has "ಸಸರ್ಜಾದೌ"]* has said that, water being the first in all creation, it is the most excellent: this is one reason for its excellence. Or, by नह्युदकात्पशस्यं लोकोपकारकं तत्त्वांतरमस्ति (*nahyudakātpaśasyaṃ lokopakārakaṃ tattvāṃtaramasti*) *[the hyphenated line break "ನಹ್ಯುದ-ಕಾತ್ಪ" is joined]*—there is no other principle more beneficial to the world than water—he has shown the second reason for its excellence. In the present case water, fire and earth, all three, are well known as excellent principles. Whether one takes the point of being the first among all creations or the point of being beneficial to the world, all obtain a praiseworthy place by their distinction. Therefore these three views all reflect the truth. It should not be understood from this that they are opposed to one another. Since, in examining the original principle of the world, it is said at times that fire was created from water, at times that water was created from fire in the form of radiance, and that fire is the food of water, there is truth in the view of all three.

आपो भूयिष्ठाः (*āpo bhūyiṣṭhāḥ*)—water is the origin of everything. From water comes food, from water purity, from water the origin of the other principles. Therefore, in support of the view that water is the most excellent, there is praise of water in many sentences.

ओमानमापो मानुषीरमृक्तं धात तोकाय तनयाय शंयोः । (*omānamāpo mānuṣīramṛktaṃ dhāta tokāya tanayāya śaṃyoḥ |*)
यूयं हि ष्ठा भिषजो मातृतमा विश्वस्य स्थातुर्जगतो जनित्रीः ॥ (*yūyaṃ hi ṣṭhā bhiṣajo mātṛtamā viśvasya sthāturjagato janitrīḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-50-7)

The waters are the producers of the whole world, movable and immovable. They are what gives all protection. They are nourishing like a mother to the whole universe. Moreover,

आपो ह यद्बृहतीर्विश्वमायन् गर्भं दधाना जनयंतीरग्निं । (*āpo ha yadbṛhatīrviśvamāyan garbhaṃ dadhānā janayaṃtīragniṃ |*)
ततो देवानुं समवर्ततासुरेकः कस्मै देवाय हविषा विधेम । (*tato devānuṃ samavartatāsurekaḥ kasmai devāya haviṣā vidhema |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-121-7)

*[In the supplied text the reference line is printed twice; the second is kept in kn.md as an italic note.]*

### Printed p. 121

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 121 (as supplied).*

The waters, the origin of the whole world, holding Hiraṇyagarbha within themselves for the creation of all beings marked by fire, pervaded the whole world. Prajāpati, the creator, was also born from there.

आपो ह वा इदमग्रे सलिलमेवास । ता अकामयंत कथं नु प्रजायेमहीति ता आश्राम्यंस्तास्तपोऽतप्यंत तासु तपस्तप्यमानासु हिरण्मय (*āpo ha vā idamagre salilamevāsa | tā akāmayaṃta kathaṃ nu prajāyemahīti tā āśrāmyaṃstāstapo 'tapyaṃta tāsu tapastapyamānāsu hiraṇmaya*)
मांडग्ं संबभूव । (*māṃḍagṃ saṃbabhūva |*)
(ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 11-1-6-1) *[abbreviation "ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ." as printed, presumably the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa, as the Kannada says; the hyphenated line break "ಪ್ರಜಾಯೇ-ಮಹೀತಿ" is joined; the word "ಹಿರಣ್ಮಯ-ಮಾಂಡಗ್ಂ" is split over two lines in the print and is quoted in two parts]*

Before creation the waters alone covered the whole world. Wishing to increase in the form of progeny, they practised austerity, and from them a golden egg arose: thus the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa states the meaning of the ṛk 10-121-7 of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā. This same principle

आपो अग्रे विश्वमावन् गर्भं दधाना अमृता ऋतज्ञाः । (*āpo agre viśvamāvan garbhaṃ dadhānā amṛtā ṛtajñāḥ |*)
यासु देवीष्वधि देव आसीत् .... .... .... .... । (*yāsu devīṣvadhi deva āsīt .... .... .... .... |*) (ಅ. ವೇ. 4-2-6) *[the line ends in printed dots]*
आपो वत्सं जनयंतीर्गर्भमग्रे समैरयन् । (*āpo vatsaṃ janayaṃtīrgarbhamagre samairayan |*)
तस्योत जायमानस्य उल्ब आसीद्धिरण्ययः । (*tasyota jāyamānasya ulba āsīddhiraṇyayaḥ |*) (ಅ. ವೇ. 4-2-8) *[the reference line is printed twice; the second is kept in kn.md as an italic note; the abbreviation "ಅ. ವೇ." is as printed, presumably the Atharvaveda, as the Kannada says]*

and so on is set forth also in the Atharvaveda. In the same way,

इमा आपः सर्वेषां भूतानां मध्वासामपां सर्वाणि भूतानि मधु यश्चायमास्वप्सु तेजोमयोऽमृतमयः पुरुषोऽयमेव स योऽयमात्मेदममृतमिदं ब्रह्मदं सर्वं (*imā āpaḥ sarveṣāṃ bhūtānāṃ madhvāsāmapāṃ sarvāṇi bhūtāni madhu yaścāyamāsvapsu tejomayo 'mṛtamayaḥ puruṣo 'yameva sa yo 'yamātmedamamṛtamidaṃ brahmadaṃ sarvaṃ*) (ಬೃ. ಉ. 9-5-2) *[abbreviation and number as printed; the hyphenated line breaks "ಯಶ್ಚಾ-ಯಮಾಸ್ವಪ್ಸು" and "ಯೋಽಯ-ಮಾತ್ಮೇದ" are joined]*

in the Madhuvidyā section of the Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad the identity of the waters with Brahman is stated and the greatness of water is praised. In the Chāndogya Upaniṣad,

आपो वावान्नाद्भूयस्तस्माद्यदा सुवृष्टिर्न भवति व्याधीयंते प्राणा अन्नं (*āpo vāvānnādbhūyastasmādyadā suvṛṣṭirna bhavati vyādhīyaṃte prāṇā annaṃ*)
कनीयो भविष्यतीत्यथ यदा सुवृष्टिर्भवत्यानंदिनः प्राणा भवत्यन्नं बहु (*kanīyo bhaviṣyatītyatha yadā suvṛṣṭirbhavatyānaṃdinaḥ prāṇā bhavatyannaṃ bahu*)
भविष्यतीत्याप एवेमा मूर्तायेयं पृथिवी यदंतरिक्षं यद्यौर्यत्पर्वता यद्देवमनुष्या यत्पशवश्च वयांसि च तृणवनस्पतयः श्वापदान्याकीटपतंगपिपीलकमाप एवेमा मूर्ता अप उपास्वेति ॥ (*bhaviṣyatītyāpa evemā mūrtāyeyaṃ pṛthivī yadaṃtarikṣaṃ yadyauryatparvatā yaddevamanuṣyā yatpaśavaśca vayāṃsi ca tṛṇavanaspatayaḥ śvāpadānyākīṭapataṃgapipīlakamāpa evemā mūrtā apa upāsveti ‖*) (ಫಾ. ಉ. 7-10-1) *[abbreviation as printed, "ಫಾ. ಉ."; the hyphenated line breaks "ಯದ್ಯೌರ್ಯ-ತ್ಪರ್ವತಾ" and "ಶ್ವಾಪದಾ-ನ್ಯಾಕೀಟ" are joined]*

Water is greater than food. If there is no good rain, all beings fear that there will be a shortage of food. If there is good rain, they think that abundant and plentiful food will be obtained *[printed "ಪುಷ್ಪವೂ ಪುಷ್ಕಲವೂ", as supplied]*; the beings *[the supplied text ends here, in the middle of the sentence, with the cut-off word "ಪ್ರಾಣಿಗಳೆ"; the rest is in the next window]*

### Printed p. 122

*Running head: 122 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨ ಸೂ. ೧೬೧] (as supplied).* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 9 continue; the first word ल्लवू (*llavū*) completes the word "ಪ್ರಾಣಿಗಳೆ" cut off at the end of the previous window: "ಪ್ರಾಣಿಗಳೆಲ್ಲವೂ", "all the beings".)

...all the beings will be happy. The condensed water itself has pervaded the form of the earth, the form of the atmosphere and of the sky, the forms of gods, men and the like, of beasts, birds, grass, trees, and of worms, insects, ants and the like. Everything is made of water. Therefore worship water.

स योऽपो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्त आप्नोति सर्वान् कामांस्तृप्तिमान् भवति यावदपां गतं तत्रास्य यथा कामचारो भवति योऽपो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते ॥ (*sa yo 'po brahmetyupāsta āpnoti sarvān kāmāṃstṛptimān bhavati yāvadapāṃ gataṃ tatrāsya yathā kāmacāro bhavati yo 'po brahmetyupāste ‖*) (ಛಾ. ಉ. 7-10-2) *[abbreviation "ಛಾ. ಉ." as printed; the hyphenated line break "ಯಾವ-ದಪಾಂ" is joined]*

One who worships water as Brahman obtains all his desired objects and attains satisfaction; he also obtains unlimited freedom. Since the greatness of water is told in this way and in other ways, what one of the Ṛbhus said, आपो भूयिष्ठाः (*āpo bhūyiṣṭhāḥ*), is a true statement of the principle.

अग्निर्भूयिष्ठः (*agnirbhūyiṣṭhaḥ*)—आंतरं बाह्यं च दाहपाकभुक्तजरणादिव्यापारेणाग्नेरेव जगन्निर्वाहकत्वादग्नेरेव भूयिष्ठत्वमित्येव मन्यते । (*āṃtaraṃ bāhyaṃ ca dāhapākabhuktajaraṇādivyāpāreṇāgnereva jagannirvāhakatvādagnereva bhūyiṣṭhatvamityeva manyate |*) *[the hyphenated line break "ಅಗ್ನೇ-ರೇವ" is joined]* It is explained that since fire alone is the cause of all the activities of the world, such as burning, cooking and digestion, inside and outside, there is no one greater than fire.

In the Chāndogya Upaniṣad, having stated the identity of water and Brahman, when it is said that the Brahman who is in the form of water should be worshipped,

अस्ति भगवोऽद्भो भूय इत्यद्भो वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवन्नवीत्त्विति ॥ (*asti bhagavo 'dbho bhūya ityadbho vāva bhūyo 'stīti tanme bhagavannavīttviti ‖*) (ಭಾ. ಉ 7-10-2) *[abbreviation as printed, "ಭಾ. ಉ"; the hyphenated line break "ಭಗವನ್ನ-ವೀತ್ತ್ವಿತಿ" is joined]*

and Nārada asks Sanatkumāra what there is that is more excellent than water,

तेजो नावाद्भ्यो भूयस्तदा एतद्वायुमागृह्याकाशमभितपति तदाहुर्निशोचति नितपति वर्षिष्यति वा इति तेज एव तत्पूर्वं दर्शयित्वाथापः (*tejo nāvādbhyo bhūyastadā etadvāyumāgṛhyākāśamabhitapati tadāhurniśocati nitapati varṣiṣyati vā iti teja eva tatpūrvaṃ darśayitvāthāpaḥ*)
सृजते तदेतदूर्ध्वाभिश्च तिरश्चिभिश्च विद्युद्भिराह्रादाश्चरंति तस्मादाहुर्विद्योतते स्तनयति वर्षिष्यति वा इति तेज एव तत्पूर्वं दर्शयित्वाथापः (*sṛjate tadetadūrdhvābhiśca tiraścibhiśca vidyudbhirāhrādāścaraṃti tasmādāhurvidyotate stanayati varṣiṣyati vā iti teja eva tatpūrvaṃ darśayitvāthāpaḥ*)
सृजते तेज उपास्वेति। स यस्तेजो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते...यथा कामचारो (*sṛjate teja upāsveti| sa yastejo brahmetyupāste...yathā kāmacāro*)
भवति ! (*bhavati !*) (ಛಾ. ಉ 7-11-1) *[the hyphenated line breaks "ತದಾಹು-ರ್ನಿಶೋಚತಿ" and "ತಸ್ಮಾದಾಹುರ್ವಿ-ದ್ಯೋತತೇ" are joined]*

the radiance (fire) in the form of lightning is more than water. It checks the wind and makes the sky hot. Then the beings understand: "Now there is a scorching heat; it will rain." The radiance first causes this heat and afterwards pours down rain.

### Printed p. 123

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 123 (as supplied).*

When lightning and thunder arise, they hope for rain. Fire in the form of lightning is praised as this radiance which is Brahman. Since the manifestation and the power of this fire are of many kinds, there is praise of this fire in many ways in many places. In the Madhuvidyā section of the Bṛhadāraṇyaka, having stated the identity of water and Brahman, it says immediately afterwards,

अयमग्निः सर्वेषां भूतानां मध्वस्याग्नेः सर्वाणि भूतानि मधु यश्चायमस्मिन्नग्नौ तेजोमयोऽमृतमयः पुरुषोऽयमेव स योऽयमात्मेदममृतमिदं ब्रह्मदं सर्वं । (*ayamagniḥ sarveṣāṃ bhūtānāṃ madhvasyāgneḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni madhu yaścāyamasminnagnau tejomayo 'mṛtamayaḥ puruṣo 'yameva sa yo 'yamātmedamamṛtamidaṃ brahmadaṃ sarvaṃ |*) (ಬೃ. ಉ. 9-53) *[reference as printed; the hyphenated line breaks "ಯಶ್ಚಾಯ-ಮಸ್ಮಿನ್ನಗ್ನೌ" and "ಯೋಽಯ-ಮಾತ್ಮೇದ" are joined]*

For all beings this fire is in the form of honey. The luminous and immortal Puruṣa who dwells within this fire is the Self, Brahman and everything: thus the identity of fire with the Self of the universe is stated. And in the conversation that took place between Pravāhaṇa Jaivali and Gautama the greatness of fire is explained in detail.

असौ वै लोकोऽग्निर्गौतम तस्यादित्य एव समिद्रश्मयो धूमोऽहरर्चिर्दिशो०गारा अवांतरदिशो विस्फुलिंगास्तस्मिन्नेतस्मिन्नग्नौ देवाः श्रद्धां (*asau vai loko 'gnirgautama tasyāditya eva samidraśmayo dhūmo 'hararcirdiśo0gārā avāṃtaradiśo visphuliṃgāstasminnetasminnagnau devāḥ śraddhāṃ*)
जुह्वति तस्या आहुत्यै सोमो राजा संभवति । (*juhvati tasyā āhutyai somo rājā saṃbhavati |*) (ಬೃ. ಉ. 6-2-9) *[the hyphenated line break "ಅಹರರ್ಚಿರ್ದಿ-ಶೋ೦ಗಾರಾ" is joined; "ಶೋ೦ಗಾರಾ" is printed with a zero-like sign]*

For this fire, which stands in the heavenly world, the sun is the fuel; the clouds are the smoke, the horizons the embers, the intermediate directions the sparks. In this fire, the Self of the universe, the gods offer faith. From this offering of faith King Soma arises.

पर्जन्यो वा अग्निर्गौतम तस्य संवत्सर एव समित् अभ्राणि धूमो .... .... ... (*parjanyo vā agnirgautama tasya saṃvatsara eva samit abhrāṇi dhūmo .... .... ...*)
अस्मिन्नग्नौ देवाः सोमं राजानं जुह्वति तस्या आहुत्यै वृष्टिः संभवति । (*asminnagnau devāḥ somaṃ rājānaṃ juhvati tasyā āhutyai vṛṣṭiḥ saṃbhavati |*) (6-2-10) *[the line has printed dots]*

The cloud that produces rain is this sacrificial fire. By offering Soma in this fire, rain is produced.

अयं वै लोकोग्निर्गौतम तस्य पृथिव्येव समिदग्निर्धूमो रात्रिरर्चिश्चंद्रमा अंगारा नक्षत्राणि विस्फुलिंगास्तस्मिन्नेतस्मिन्नग्नौ देवा वृष्टिं (*ayaṃ vai lokognirgautama tasya pṛthivyeva samidagnirdhūmo rātrirarciścaṃdramā aṃgārā nakṣatrāṇi visphuliṃgāstasminnetasminnagnau devā vṛṣṭiṃ*)
जुह्वति तस्या आहुत्या अन्नं संभवति । (*juhvati tasyā āhutyā annaṃ saṃbhavati |*) (6-2-11) *[the hyphenated line break "ರಾತ್ರಿರರ್ಚಿ-ಶ್ಚಂದ್ರಮಾ" is joined]*

### Printed p. 124

*Running head: 124 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ ೧೬೧] (as supplied).*

This whole world is the sacrificial fire. The earth is the fuel, Agni is the smoke, night is the flame, the moon the embers, the stars the sparks. In this world-fire the gods pour down rain. From this food is produced.

पुरुषो ना अग्निर्गौतम .... .... .... .... योषा वा अग्निः : (*puruṣo nā agnirgautama .... .... .... .... yoṣā vā agniḥ :*) (6-2-193) *[reference as printed; the line has printed dots]*

Man, woman and everything are transformations of this fire.

ते य एवमेतद्विदुर्य्ये चामिा अरण्ये श्रद्धां सत्यमुपासते तेऽर्चिरभि (*te ya evametadviduryye cāmiā araṇye śraddhāṃ satyamupāsate te 'rcirabhi*)
संभवंति .... .... .... ........ .... .... .... .... ते तेषु ब्रह्मलोकेषु पराः (*saṃbhavaṃti .... .... .... ........ .... .... .... .... te teṣu brahmalokeṣu parāḥ*)
परावतो वसंति तेषां न पुनरावृत्तिः । (*parāvato vasaṃti teṣāṃ na punarāvṛttiḥ |*) (6-2-15) *[the line "…ತೇಽರ್ಚಿರಭಿ" ends with a word split without a hyphen, the next line begins with "ಸಂಭವಂತಿ", so it is quoted in parts; the lines have printed dots]*

The Kannada explains to Gautama that the knowers who have known the universal form, the universal power and the universal nature of the world-fire attain the world of Brahman and obtain liberation, and that for them there is no re-entry into saṃsāra. In this teaching fire is described as the support of the whole world, the giver of wealth, the nourisher, the protector and the giver of liberation, and as the cause and also the goal धैयवू (*dhaiyavū*) *[so printed]* of all kinds of activities of the terrestrial, atmospheric and heavenly worlds. In the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā also,

वि यो रजांस्यमिमित सुक्रतुर्वैश्वानरो वि दिवो रोचना कविः । (*vi yo rajāṃsyamimita sukraturvaiśvānaro vi divo rocanā kaviḥ |*)
परि यो विश्वा भुवनानि पप्रथेऽदब्बो गोपा अमृतस्य रक्षिता ! (*pari yo viśvā bhuvanāni paprathe 'dabbo gopā amṛtasya rakṣitā !*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-7-7)

this fire measured all the worlds and created all the spheres of radiance.

श्रीणि शता श्री सहस्रां,ग्निं त्रिंशच्च देवा नव चासपर्यन् । (*śrīṇi śatā śrī sahasrāṃ,gniṃ triṃśacca devā nava cāsaparyan |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-9-9)

the gods, three thousand three hundred and thirty-nine in number, also worshipped this same great fire. Thus the greatness of fire is praised. This fire, who is the support of the universe and the giver of life to the whole universe, is praised in the Śatapatha and other texts as everything, in sentences such as

अग्निर्वै भुवोऽग्नेर्हीदग्ं सर्वं भवति । (*agnirvai bhuvo 'gnerhīdagṃ sarvaṃ bhavati |*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 8-1-1-4) *[abbreviation as printed]*

On the basis of these authorities there is truth also in what the second of the Ṛbhus said, अग्निर्भूयिष्ठः (*agnirbhūyiṣṭhaḥ*). The third,

### Printed p. 125

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 125 (as supplied).* (The Special Topics of Ṛk 9 continue: the third Ṛbhu.)

वधर्यंतीं—प्रैकोऽब्रवीत् (*vadharyaṃtīṃ—praiko 'bravīt*)—he describes the earth as the greatest of all. Sāyaṇa has explained the word "vadharyantī" in two ways. First:

वधरिति रेफांतोऽपि वज्रनाम । वधोर्क इति तन्नामसु पाठात् । तदिच्छति वृष्टु ध्य (*vadhariti rephāṃto 'pi vajranāma | vadhorka iti tannāmasu pāṭhāt | tadicchati vṛṣṭu dhya*)
काय इति वधर्यंती मेघपंक्तिरुच्यते । (*kāya iti vadharyaṃtī meghapaṃktirucyate |*) *[the first line ends with the printed "ವೃಷ್ಟು ಧ್ಯ" and the second begins with "ಕಾಯ", a word split over two lines (so printed, with a stray "ಧ್ಯ"); quoted in two parts]*

Since "vadhaḥ", "vajraḥ" and "arkaḥ" are read among the names of the thunderbolt (Ni. 3-11), the word "vadha" means the thunderbolt. Because, in order to shed rain, the cloud awaits a stroke from the thunderbolt, "vadharyantī" means the rain-producing cloud. Understood thus, since the cloud is the cause of water also, Sāyaṇa has explained that the cloud is the most excellent.

अभ्रं भूत्वा मेघो भवति मेघो भूत्वा प्रवर्षति । (*abhraṃ bhūtvā megho bhavati megho bhūtvā pravarṣati |*) (ಛಾ. ಉ. 5-10-6.)

Thus the cloud, being the cause of water too, is the basis of all abundance of food. And:

ताभिः पर्जन्यो वर्षति पर्जन्येनौषधिवनस्पतयः प्रजायंते ॥ (*tābhiḥ parjanyo varṣati parjanyenauṣadhivanaspatayaḥ prajāyaṃte ‖*) (ತೈ. ಉ. 4-23-1)

Since the herbs and trees that provide food to beings are produced by this raining, the purport is that nothing is more useful than the cloud, which is the cause of this rain. *[the abbreviations "ಛಾ. ಉ." and "ತೈ. ಉ." are as printed]*

Second, making the construction "bahubhyaḥ vadharyantīm":

बहूनामर्थाय (*bahūnāmarthāya*)
आत्मनः वधं इच्छंतीं भूमिं । (*ātmanaḥ vadhaṃ icchaṃtīṃ bhūmiṃ |*) *[the sentence is split over two lines]*

it may mean the earth, which for the life of many beings wishes its own "vadha" (beating, cutting) by works such as ploughing. If this meaning is taken, the word "pṛthvī" then does not mean only the conventional earth but means the whole world. Since this word is included both among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5) and among the names of the atmosphere (Ni. 2-10), it may mean the wide world. To understand it as a synonym of the cloud also seems proper. Even if that meaning is right, the depth of the exposition of principle given by the first two is not seen in this third view.

ऋता वदंतः (*ṛtā vadaṃtaḥ*)—here the word "ṛta" is explained as true speech. To show that when the Ṛbhus expound the meaning of principles they declare that it is true, and that precisely because they have such true speech the name "Ṛbhu" became well known, Sāyaṇa has explained, quoting the Nirukta derivation

उरु भांतीति वा ऋतेन भांतीति वा, ऋतेन भवंतीति वा (*uru bhāṃtīti vā ṛtena bhāṃtīti vā, ṛtena bhavaṃtīti vā*) (Ni. 11-15)

the view of the Nirukta authors being that they are famous by the name "Ṛbhus" because they shine by truth or because they are of the nature of truth.

### Printed p. 126

*Running head: 126 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧] (as supplied).*

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses भूयिष्ठाः (*bhūyiṣṭhāḥ*) (the rule Pā. Sū. 6-4-159; the number of the first sūtra is split over two lines), अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*) (root ब्रूज् (*brūj*) *[so printed]*), वधर्यंतीम् (*vadharyaṃtīm*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-8), ऋता (*ṛtā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70), वदंतः (*vadaṃtaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-186) and अपिंशत (*apiṃśata*) (root पिश (*piśa*)), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೯ ॥. *[A stray line ":0:" follows in the supplied text; kept in kn.md as an italic note.]*

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> श्रो॒णामेक॑ उद॒कं गामवा॑जति मां॒समेकः॑ पिंशति सू॒नयाभ्॑यतं ।
> आ नि॒म्रुचः॒ शकृ॒देको॒ अपा॑भर॒त्किं स्वि॑त्पु॒त्रेभ्यः॑ पि॒तरा॒ उपा॑वतुः ॥ १० ॥

> *śroṇāmeka udakaṃ gāmavājati māṃsamekaḥ piṃśati sūnayābhyataṃ |*
> *ā nimrucaḥ śakṛdeko apābharatkiṃ svitputrebhyaḥ pitarā upāvatuḥ ‖ 10 ‖*

*[The hyphenated line breaks in the Saṃhitā block are joined. The Saṃhitā has सूनयाभ्यतं (*sūnayābhyataṃ*) where the Pada has "ಸೂನಯಾ । ಆಭೃತಂ" and the Pratipadārtha has निम्मुचः (*nimmucaḥ*) where the Saṃhitā and the Pada have "ನಿಮ್ರುಚಃ"; kept as supplied.]*

### Printed p. 127

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೫. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 127 (as supplied).* *[The word "ಪಡೆದರು" ("they obtained") stands alone at the head of the page, before the label of the Pada, in the supplied text; kept in kn.md as an italic note. The label of the Pada was missing from the block and is inserted.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> श्रोणां । एकः । उदकं । गां । अव । अ ज ति । मांसं । एकः । पिं शति । सूनया ।
> आभृतं ।
> आ । निम्रुचः । शकृत् । एकः । अप । अभरत् । किं । स्वित् । पुत्रेभ्यः । पितरा । उप ।
> आवतुः ॥ १० ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The block reads "शोणं ii ना छंदसीति (*śoṇaṃ ii nā chaṃdasīti*)" as supplied.]*

> अत्रर्भव एवर्त्विग्रूपेण स्थित्वा सर्वत्र यज्ञं निर्वहंतीत्याह । शोणां
> शोणं ii ना छंदसीति पररूपाभावः ॥ शोणवर्णमुदकं रुधिरमेक ऋभुषु
> मध्य ऋत्विग्गामवाजति । वाह्यभूमिं प्रति गमयति । रक्षोभागत्वेन स्थापयति ।
> तथैको मांसं सूनया छेदनसाधनेन स्वधितिनाभृतं संपादितं पिंशति । हृदयजिह्वावक्ष आद्याकारेणाहृतं संपादितं मांसं पिंशति । अवयवीकरोति । स्वधितिना
> तत्तदवयवान्विभजतीत्यर्थः । अथैको निम्रुचो निःशेषेण च्छिन्नान्मांसात् शकृत्
> कुक्षिस्थं जरत्ृणमूवध्यनामकमपाभरत् । अपाहरन्निरसनाय । एवमृतिग्रूपेण
> स्थितेभ्यः पुत्रेभ्यः पुरुत्रातृभ्यः पुत्रस्थानीयेभ्य ऋभुभ्यः सर्वॆष्टनुष्ठितेषु पितरा
> यज्ञस्य पालयितारौ जायापती किं स्विदुपावतुः । किं नु खलु प्राप्पुतः ॥ अवतेः
> प्राप्त्यर्थाच्छांदसो लिट् ॥ सर्वं तैरेवानुष्ठितमिति तयोर्न किंचित्कृत्यमस्तीत्यर्थः । अथवायमर्थः । एकस्तेषामन्यतमः शोणां पंगुकां गामुदकं प्रत्यवगमयति । अपरः सूनया मांसविक्रयिण्याभृतं संपादितं मांसं पिंशति । अपर आ
> निम्रुचोऽस्तमयादर्वागेव शकृद्योपुरीषं हरति । एवमृभुभिरनुष्ठिते सति किं
> स्सिदेतस्मात्परिचरणादन्यत्पुत्रेभ्यः सकाशादुपावतुः । उपगच्छेतामिति ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): the Ṛbhus themselves, as priests, do all the sacrificial work, bringing blood, cutting flesh, removing dung; what is left for their parents to do?]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

एकः (*ekaḥ*)—one of the Ṛbhus; शोणां उदकं (*śoṇāṃ udakaṃ*)—the red-coloured water, that is, blood; गां (*gāṃ*)—to the sacrificial ground; अवाजति (*avājati*)—brings; एकः (*ekaḥ*)—another; सूनया (*sūnayā*)—with a chopping knife; आभृतं (*ābhṛtaṃ*)—cut; मांसं (*māṃsaṃ*)—the flesh; पिंशति (*piṃśati*)—separates into pieces; निम्मुचः (*nimmucaḥ*)—from the flesh cut completely; शकृत् (*śakṛt*)—the dung and the like; एकः (*ekaḥ*)—another; अपाभरत् (*apābharat*)—took away (to throw outside); पुत्रेभ्यः (*putrebhyaḥ*)—to the Ṛbhus in the position of sons, or to the Ṛbhus in the form of priests who are protectors; पितरौ (*pitarau*) *[so printed in the Pratipadārtha]*—the sacrificer, protector of the sacrifice, and his wife; किंस्वित् (*kiṃsvit*)—what else indeed; उपावतुः (*upāvatuḥ*)—can procure and give (as help)?

### Printed p. 128

*Running head: 128 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).* (Ṛk 10 continues: the Bhāvārtha.)

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

One of the Ṛbhus places the blood on the sacrificial ground. Another separates into pieces the flesh cut with a chopping knife. Another takes the dung and the like, separated from the completely cut flesh, to throw it away. What other help could the sacrificer in the form of father and mother, and his wife, give to the Ṛbhus in the form of priests?

**Printed English translation**

One pours the red water, (the blood), upon the ground ; one cuts the flesh, divided into fragments by the chopper; and a third separates the excrement from the other parts: in what manner may the parents (of the sacrifice) render assistance to their sons ?

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Two kinds of meaning are given for this ṛk. The first meaning has been given in the Pratipadārtha. According to the second explanation, the word श्रोणा (*śroṇā*) *[so printed, "ಶ್ರೋಣಾರಬ್ದಕ್ಕೆ", with "ರಬ್ದ" for "ಶಬ್ದ"]* is explained as "lame" *[printed in Latin letters "(Laine)", kept as supplied]*, the word सूना (*sūnā*) as a shop that sells meat, and नम्रुचः (*namrucaḥ*) *[so printed]* as "before sunset". According to this, एकः शोणां गां उदकं अवाजति (*ekaḥ śoṇāṃ gāṃ udakaṃ avājati*) means that one of the Ṛbhus leads a lame cow to the water; एकः सूनया आभृतं (*ekaḥ sūnayā ābhṛtaṃ*) मांसं पिंशति (*māṃsaṃ piṃśati*) means that another divides the flesh brought from the meat shop; and एकः निम्रुचः शकृत् अवाभरत् (*ekaḥ nimrucaḥ śakṛt avābharat*) means that another brings, before sunset, cow-dung and the like (for purification). The purport is that, since the Ṛbhus in the form of priests, standing in the position of sons to the sacrifice, carried out all the equipment needed for the sacrifice, the sacrificer in the position of the parents and his wife have no toil at all.

Here the Ṛbhus are designated as priests in the position of sons to the sacrifice, and the sacrificer, the protector of the sacrifice, and his wife (printed "ಸತ್ನಿ") as पितरा (*pitarā*).

हनामैना इति त्वष्टा यदब्रवीच्च मसं ये देवपानमनिंदिषुः । (*hanāmainā iti tvaṣṭā yadabravīcca masaṃ ye devapānamaniṃdiṣuḥ |*)
अन्या नामानि कृण्वते सुते सचा अन्यैरेनान् कन्या३नामभिः स्परत् ॥ (*anyā nāmāni kṛṇvate sute sacā anyairenān kanyā3nāmabhiḥ sparat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-161-5)

In the previous ṛk of this same sūkta, when the Ṛbhus made the ladle defiled, Tvaṣṭṛ angrily tried to kill them; when they took other names and hid themselves in the form of priests, the mother honoured them in their priestly form: thus the earlier story was told when the ṛk quoting it was explained.

### Printed p. 129

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩ ವ ೬.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 129 (as supplied).*

Here, indicating that same context, it is said that the Ṛbhus fully performed their duties in the priestly form.

पुत्रेभ्यः—पुरु त्रातृभ्यः पुत्रस्थानीयेभ्यो ऋभुभ्यः । (*putrebhyaḥ—puru trātṛbhyaḥ putrasthānīyebhyo ṛbhubhyaḥ |*) Because they protect the sacrifice completely, the priests are called sons. Here, since the Ṛbhus are priests, they are designated by the word "putra".

पितरा—यज्ञस्य पालयितार् जायापती (*pitarā—yajñasya pālayitār jāyāpatī*) *[so printed, "ಪಾಲಯಿತಾರ್"]* the "jāyāpati" protectors of the sacrifice, the sacrificer and his wife *[printed "ಪತ್ನಿಯರು", plural]*.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses श्रोणाम् (*śroṇām*) (root श्रु (*śru*); with the rule ಉ. ಸೂ. 3-386, the abbreviation as printed), उदकम् (*udakam*) (root उंदी (*uṃdī*); ಉ. ಸೂ. 2-197; a Pā. Sū. number is split over two lines), अजति (*ajati*), पिंशति (*piṃśati*), सूनया (*sūnayā*) (ಉ. ಸೂ. 2-293), अभृतम् (*abhṛtam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-49), अभरत् (*abharat*) and, on p. 130, आवतुः (*āvatuḥ*) (root अव (*ava*); Pā. Sū. 3-4-6 and 3-4-82), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೧೦ ॥. *[A stray signature line "[9] Vol. 12" stands at the foot of p. 129 and a stray ":C:" after the grammar; both kept in kn.md as italic notes.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> पुराणप्रसिद्धान्सुधन्वनः पुत्रान् कर्मणा देवत्वं प्राप्तवत ऋभून् स्तुत्वा
> वेदप्रसिद्ध्या सूर्यरश्मिरूपानृभूनिदानीं सौति । तथा च यास्को निरुक्ते
> पुराणप्रक्रियासिद्ध्या नृभून्बहुधा निरुच्य तदुदाहरणत्वेन विष्टि शमीत्येतामुदाहृत्यादित्यरश्मयोऽप्यभव उच्यंत इत्युक्ता तस्मिन्नर्थ एतामृचमुदाजहार ।
> नि. ११-१६. । हे ऋभव उरु प्रभूतं भासमानाः ऋतेनादित्येन भासमाना वा
> ऋतेनोदकेनोदकार्थमुत्पन्ना वा । रश्मीनामुरुभासमानत्वं प्रसिद्धं । तथादित्यादुत्पत्तिरपि । उदकार्थं भवनमपि रश्मीनां घर्मकाले भूमिगतं सारवादाय
> पुनर्वर्षकाले प्रवर्षंतीति प्रसिद्धं । अदित्याज्ञायते वृष्टिरिति शास्त्रात् । तथा
> विधा नरः प्रकाशावृष्ट्यु दकादेर्नेतारः यूयमुद्वत्तून्नतेषु प्रदेशेषु मेर्वादिषु तृणं व्रीहियवादिरूपमस्यॆ प्राणिजाताय तदुपकारार्थमकृणोतन । कृतवंतः स्थ ॥ उपसर्गाच्छंदसि धात्वर्थ इति वतिः ॥ तथा निवत्तु प्रवणदेशेश्वस्मा
> एवाप उदकान्यकृणोतन । अकुरुत । किं स्वभोगार्थं नेत्याह । स्वपस्यया

> *vedaprasiddhyā sūryaraśmirūpānṛbhūnidānīṃ sauti | tathā ca yāsko nirukte*
> *purāṇaprakriyāsiddhyā nṛbhūnbahudhā nirucya tadudāharaṇatvena viṣṭi śamītyetāmudāhṛtyādityaraśmayo 'pyabhava ucyaṃta ityuktā tasminnartha etāmṛcamudājahāra |*
> *ni. 11-16. | he ṛbhava uru prabhūtaṃ bhāsamānāḥ ṛtenādityena bhāsamānā vā*
> *ṛtenodakenodakārthamutpannā vā | raśmīnāmurubhāsamānatvaṃ prasiddhaṃ | tathādityādutpattirapi | udakārthaṃ bhavanamapi raśmīnāṃ gharmakāle bhūmigataṃ sāravādāya*
> *punarvarṣakāle pravarṣaṃtīti prasiddhaṃ | adityājñāyate vṛṣṭiriti śāstrāt | tathā*
> *vidhā naraḥ prakāśāvṛṣṭyu dakādernetāraḥ yūyamudvattūnnateṣu pradeśeṣu mervādiṣu tṛṇaṃ vrīhiyavādirūpamasyè prāṇijātāya tadupakārārthamakṛṇotana | kṛtavaṃtaḥ stha ‖ upasargācchaṃdasi dhātvartha iti vatiḥ ‖ tathā nivattu pravaṇadeśeśvasmā*
> *evāpa udakānyakṛṇotana | akuruta | kiṃ svabhogārthaṃ netyāha | svapasyayā*

### Printed p. 131

*Running head: [ಅ. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೬.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 131 (as supplied: "೨." is missing after "ಅ.").* (Ṛk 11: the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya continues from the previous window.)

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (continued)**

*[The continuation stood as plain lines in the supplied text and is made a block; the hyphenated line breaks are joined ("ಗೃಹೇ೦-ತರಿಕ್ಷೇ" is printed so).]*

> शोभनकर्मेच्छया । शोभनं कर्मेच्छः कृतमिति यथा यजमानाः स्तुवंति तदिच्छयेत्यर्थः । किंच यूयमगोह्यस्याग्राह्यस्यादित्यस्य गृहे मंडले यद्यावत्कालमसस्तन सुप्तवंतः स्थ रात्रौ निर्वापारास्तिष्ठथ ॥ षस स्वप्ने । अस्माल्लुजि पूर्ववत्तस्य तनबादेशः । अदादित्वाच्छपो लुक् ॥ तत्तावंतं कालमद्याहनीदमिदानीं
> नानु गच्छथ । अनुसृत्य न गच्छथ । यावंतं कालं रात्रौ सूर्यॆ निगूढा वर्तद्वे
> तावंतमेव कालमहन्यपि नानुगच्छथेत्यर्थः । यद्वा । अगोपनीयस्य तस्य गृहे०तरिक्षे यद्यावंतं कालमसस्तन वृष्टिमदत्त्वा । स्वापमकुरुत तत्तावंतं कालमद्येदानीं वर्षसमय इदं सस्याभिवर्धनवृष्टिप्रवाहरूपं कर्म नानु गच्छथ । अनुसृत्य न प्राप्पुथ । यावतं कालं विवर्षथ तावंतमेव कालं प्रवर्षथेत्यर्थः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): the Ṛbhus raise grass on heights, waters in hollows; as they rested in the Sun's abode at night, they must not desist by day.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

नरः (*naraḥ*)—you leaders of all the rains and the like; ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, who are most luminous and are in the form of rays of the sun, you; उदत्सु (*udatsu*) *[so printed, with a stray "!" after it]*—in high places; अस्मै (*asmai*)—for the benefit of the beings of this world; तृणं (*tṛṇaṃ*)—plants such as rice and barley; अकृणोतन (*akṛṇotana*)—you created; स्वपस्यया (*svapasyayā*)—for the assistance of excellent works; निवत्सु (*nivatsu*)—in low-lying places; अपः (*apaḥ*)—(you made) the waters (flow); अगोह्यस्य (*agohyasya*)—of the Sun, who cannot be hidden; गृहे (*gṛhe*)—in the dwelling; यत् (*yat*)—for how long (at night); असस्तन (*asastana*)—you rested; तत् (*tat*)—for that same time; इवं (*ivaṃ*) *[so printed]*—on this day too; नानुगच्छथ (*nānugacchatha*)—(leaving your duty) do not remain just there.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, leaders of all the rains and the like, most luminous and in the form of the rays of the sun, you have created, for the benefit of the beings of this world, plants such as rice and barley. For the assistance of excellent works you have made the waters flow in low-lying places. For as long as you rested at night in the dwelling of the Sun, who cannot be hidden, for that long do not, on this day *[printed "ಹೆಗಲಿನಲ್ಲಿಯೂ", "on the shoulder", presumably for "ಹಗಲಿನಲ್ಲಿಯೂ"; kept as supplied]* too, leave your duty and remain just there.

**Printed English translation**

Ribhus, leaders (of the rains), you have caused the grass to grow upon the high places; you have caused the waters to flow over the low places; for (the promotion of) good works: as you have reposed for a while

### Printed p. 132

*Running head: 132 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).*

in the dwelling of the unapprehensible (Suu) [sic], so desist not to-day from (the discharge of) this (your function).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The glory of the Ṛbhus is to be seen from two points of view. First, after describing that the Ṛbhus, the sons of Sudhanvan, obtained divinity by their good works, it is said that the Ṛbhus who are in the form of the rays of the sun, well known in the Veda, are now praised. In the Purāṇa the Ṛbhu is called a mind-born son of Brahmā, and the Bhāgavata (4-8) says that he is one of these five: Sanaka, Sananda, Sanatkumāra, Sanatsujāta and Ṛbhu. Since Sāyaṇa says "well known in the Purāṇa", it is a matter that deserves examination.

As said in the Nirukta sentence आदित्यरश्मयोऽप्यभव उच्यंते (*ādityaraśmayo 'pyabhava ucyaṃte*) (Ni. 11-16), Sāyaṇa has explained the ṛk by stating here the identity of the rays of the sun with the Ṛbhus.

अगोह्यस्य—आगोह्य आदित्योऽगूहनीयः । (*agohyasya—āgohya ādityo 'gūhanīyaḥ |*) *[so printed, "ಆಗೋಹ್ಯ"]* This epithet, applied to the Sun, who cannot be covered, tells his nature. [In the ṛk 8-98-4 this epithet is, in the same sense, an epithet of the mountain.] *[bracketed as supplied]*

ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—explaining the meaning of this word with the Nirukta derivation, the special character of this name was told earlier.

उद्वत्सु निवत्सु—उन्नतेषु प्रदेशेषु, श्रवणदेशेषु (*udvatsu nivatsu—unnateṣu pradeśeṣu, śravaṇadeśeṣu*) *[so printed, "ಶ್ರವಣದೇಶೇಷು"; the bhāṣya block has "ಪ್ರವಣದೇಶೇ"]* means in high and low regions. The Nirukta authors have used the words प्रवत उद्धतो निवत इति (*pravata uddhato nivata iti*) as synonyms of gods, men and animals (Ni. 10.20, printed with a stop). Durgācārya explains it: उद्धतः- (*uddhataḥ-*) देवाः, प्रवतः-मनुष्याः (*devāḥ, pravataḥ-manuṣyāḥ*) and "nivataḥ" as animals. अवतिर्गतिकर्मा (*avatirgatikarmā*): derived from the root "ava", which indicates movement, "uddhata" is the name of those who move in the world of the gods, "pravata" of men and the like who move on the earth, and "nivata" of worms, insects and the like living very low down.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses उद्वत्सु (*udvatsu*) (Pā. Sū. 5-1-118), अकृणोतन (*akṛṇotana*) and, on p. 133, निवत्सु (*nivatsu*), स्वपस्यया (*svapasyayā*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-8 and 3-3-102), अगोह्यस्य (*agohyasya*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-2), असस्तन (*asastana*) (root षस (*ṣasa*); Pā. Sū. 7-1-45 and 2-4-72) and गच्छथ (*gacchatha*), with the accents; it ends with the mark ॥ ೧೧ ॥.
