*Running head: 134 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).* (Ṛk 12: the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya continues from the previous window.)

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (continued)**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined.]*

> तात्या तात् तायमाने वृष्टुदके । यद्वा । तात्या तासु वर्षासु ॥ छांदसस्यप्रत्ययो दकारस्यात्वं च ॥ अथवा पितृविशेषणं । तात्या तत्कालीनौ पितरा जगतः
> पालकौ सूर्याचंद्रमसौ क्व स्विदासतुः । कुत्र खलु तिष्ठतः ॥ अस्तेर्लिटि व्यत्ययेन
> भूभावाभावः । यद्वा । असतेर्गत्यर्थस्य लिट्येतद्रूपं ॥ अहोरात्रविभागेन
> प्रवर्षयथेत्यर्थः । किंचेदृशान्नो युष्मान् यः कोऽपि वृष्टिप्रतिबंधकारी
> राक्षसादिः करस्कं । बाहुनामैतत् करस्न बाहू इत्युक्तत्वात् अत्र त्वकारांतं ।
> युष्मदीयं हस्तवाददे गृह्णाति प्रतिरोधं करोति तमशपत । नाशयथ । यश्च
> प्राब्रवीत् प्रकर्षेण वाचा निरुंधे । तस्मै प्रो प्रैवाब्रवीतन । प्रकर्षेण भर्तृनादिशब्दं कुरुथ । यद्वा । यो नूनं स्तौति तं प्रकर्षेण स्तुतेत्यर्थः ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): as rays the Ṛbhus cloud the world and rain; where are the parents then? Curse whoever seizes your arms; roar at him who reviles you.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays) यत् (*yat*)—when; भुवना (*bhuvanā*)—the beings; संमाल्य (*saṃmālya*) *[so printed]*—having covered (with clouds); पर्यसर्तत (*paryasartata*) *[so printed]*—you move about or shed rain, (then); तात्या (*tātyā*)—at the time when the rain is falling; वः (*vaḥ*)—your *[the printed text has an unmatched closing bracket after "ಜಗತ್ತಿನ", "your and the world's"]*; पितरा (*pitarā*)—the parents, the sun and the moon; क्वस्वित् (*kvasvit*)—where indeed; आसतुः (*āsatuḥ*)—are; यः (*yaḥ*)—any enemy whatsoever; वः (*vaḥ*)—your; करस्नं (*karasnaṃ*)—the rays in the form of arms; आददे (*ādade*)—seizes and obstructs, (him); अशपत (*aśapata*)—curse and destroy; यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever; प्र अब्रवीत् (*pra abravīt*)—reviles and disparages; तस्मै (*tasmai*)—to him; प्रो अब्रवीतन (*pro abravītana*)—make a sound that causes fear. [Or यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever; प्र अब्रवीत् (*pra abravīt*)—praises you; तस्मै (*tasmai*)—to him; प्रो अब्रवीतन (*pro abravītana*)—make an encouraging sound.]

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays, when you cover the beings with clouds and shed rain, where indeed are, at that time, the sun and the moon, your protectors and the parents of the world? Curse and destroy any enemy who seizes and obstructs your rays in the form of arms. Make a frightening sound at him who disparages you with improper words.

**Printed English translation**

As you glide along enveloping the regions (in clouds); where, then, are the parents (of the world)? curse him who arrests your arm: reply sternly to him who speaks disrespectfully (to you).

### Printed p. 135

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಆ. ೩. ವ. ೬. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 135 (as supplied; "ಆ." printed for "ಅ.").*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

तात्या—तायमाने वृष्टुदके ; यद्वा तासु वर्षासु । (*tātyā—tāyamāne vṛṣṭudake ; yadvā tāsu varṣāsu |*) It is explained as: when the rain-water falls uninterruptedly *[printed "ಅನುಸ್ಕೃತವಾಗಿಯೂ"]* and in streams. Monier Williams, in his Sanskrit-English Dictionary, explains this word as "tātya", relating to the father, gives the meaning "Fatherly", and shows the present ṛk and the ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-37-6 as examples. But since the word "pitarā" occurs again in this ṛk, it is not proper to take "tātyā" as "relating to the father". The meaning given by Sāyaṇa is the correct one.

Sāyaṇa, in the ṛk

वासयसीव वेधसस्त्वं नः कदा न इंद्र वचसो बुबोधः । (*vāsayasīva vedhasastvaṃ naḥ kadā na iṃdra vacaso bubodhaḥ |*)
अस्तं तात्या धिया रयिं सुवीरं पृक्षो नो अर्वा न्युहीत वाजी ॥ (*astaṃ tātyā dhiyā rayiṃ suvīraṃ pṛkṣo no arvā nyuhīta vājī ‖*) (ಋ ಸಂ, 7-37-6) *[reference as printed, with a comma]*

explains the word "tātyā" with तनोतेरिदं रूपं संततया (धिया) । (*tanoteridaṃ rūpaṃ saṃtatayā (dhiyā) |*) as the passive use of the root तनु (विस्तारे) (*tanu (vistāre)*), which gives the meaning "extended" *[printed "ವಿಸ್ತ್ರತವಾಗಿರುವುದು"]*; as it is there taken to mean uninterrupted praise, so in the present ṛk too the meaning is "by rain that falls without a break", that is, "at the time of rain". Or, षितृविशेषणं । तात्या तत्कालीनौ (*ṣitṛviśeṣaṇaṃ | tātyā tatkālīnau*) पितरा । (*pitarā |*) *[so printed, "ಷಿತೃ" for "ಪಿತೃ"; the sentence is split over two lines]* "relating to that time", it becomes an epithet of the word "pitarā": thus Sāyaṇa has given this second alternative meaning also.

करस्नं (*karasnaṃ*)—the word "karasna" is included among the names of the arms (Ni. 3-8). The Nirukta authors, having quoted the ṛk

बृबदुक्कं हवामहे सृप्रकरस्नमूतये । साधु कृण्वंतमवसे ॥ (*bṛbadukkaṃ havāmahe sṛprakarasnamūtaye | sādhu kṛṇvaṃtamavase ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-32-10)

have explained the word "karasna" as कर्मणां प्रस्नातारौ । (*karmaṇāṃ prasnātārau |*) *[the supplied text has "ಕರಸ್ಯ ಶಬ್ದಕ್ಕೆ" and "ಪ್ರಸ್ನಾತಾರೌ"]* and have shown that, since the arms are the promoters of works, this name is well known for them. (6-17, as printed without an abbreviation.)

प्र अब्रवीत् (*pra abravīt*)—for this word both meanings, "reviles" and "praises", are given. If the prefix "pra" is added to the root "brū" the meanings are "pronounce loudly", "make known", "proclaim". This same point the scholar Wilson, speaking of the special points of this ṛk, has explained in the words "pra prefixed to brū, may mean, either to speak harshly or kindly; to censure or to praise" *[the supplied text has the Kannada letters "ಪ್ರ" and "ಬ್ರೂ" inside the English sentence]*. Because both meanings are possible, Sāyaṇa also, when he says तस्मै प्रो अब्रवीतन (*tasmai pro abravītana*), has explained that the meaning is: make a sound so that the one who reviles you is afraid, or say words of favour so that the one who praises you is encouraged.

पितरा-जगतः पालकौ । (*pitarā-jagataḥ pālakau |*) the sun and the moon, the protectors of the world.

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): resting in the Sun's orb, the Ṛbhus ask who impels them to rain; the Sun answers: Vāyu; after the year they light the world again.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋभवः (*ṛbhavaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays; सुषुप्वांसः (*suṣupvāṃsaḥ*)—resting, having taken support in the orb of the Sun; तत् (*tat*)—this question; अपृच्छत (*apṛcchata*)—ask (it is this); अगोह्य (*agohya*)—O Sun, whom no one can hide; इदं (*idaṃ*)—to this work of raining; नः (*naḥ*)—us; कः (*kaḥ*)—who; अबूबुधत् (*abūbudhat*)—has impelled; बस्तः (*bastaḥ*)—the Sun, who covers (everyone); श्वानं (*śvānaṃ*)—Vāyu; बोधयितारं (*bodhayitāraṃ*)—as the impeller; अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*)—said; संवत्सरे (*saṃvatsare*) *[printed with a double hyphen]*—the year

### Printed p. 138

*Running head: 138 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧ ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ. ೧೬೧] (as supplied).* (The Pratipadārtha continues.)

having been completed; अद्य (*adya*)—now; इदं (*idaṃ*)—this world; व्यख्यत (*vyakhyata*)—you made shine in manifold ways (he also said).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, in the form of rays, resting with the orb of the Sun as your support, ask the question: "O Sun, whom no one can hide, who has impelled us to this work of raining?" Then the Sun, who covers everyone, said that Vāyu is the impeller. He also said: "Afterwards, when the year has been completed, you made this world shine in manifold ways."

**Printed English translation**

Ribhus, reposing in the solar orb, you inquire, who awakens us, unapprehensible (Sun), to tbis [sic] office (of sending rain). The sun replies-The awakener is the wind ; and the year (being ended), you again to-day light up this (world).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

It is a well-known matter that the rays of the sun draw up water and perform the work of raining in the form of clouds. On careful examination, that work cannot be completely carried out by the sun's rays alone. To make clear that other help is also necessary, it is explained here in the form of question and answer. It is described here figuratively that the Sun says, in answer to the question of the rays, that Vāyu, who pervades the whole atmosphere and holds lordship of the middle world, is the chief helper in this work of raining.

Among Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, it is clear that Vāyu is the presiding deity of the middle world. For the sun's rays, which travel through the atmosphere, the impulse of this presiding deity of the atmosphere is indispensable for the shedding of rain. Vāyu is the lord of all the activities that take place in this world.

अथ विश्वज्योतिषमुपदधाति । नायुर्वै मध्यमा विश्वज्योतिर्नायुर्हैवांतरिक्षलोके विश्वं ज्योतिर्नायुमेवैतदुपदधाति ॥ (*atha viśvajyotiṣamupadadhāti | nāyurvai madhyamā viśvajyotirnāyurhaivāṃtarikṣaloke viśvaṃ jyotirnāyumevaitadupadadhāti ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 8-3-2-1) *[abbreviation as printed; the hyphenated line break "ನಾಯುರ್ಹೈ-ವಾಂತರಿಕ್ಷ" is joined; "ನಾಯು" is printed for "ವಾಯು"]*

Of the three universal lights, Agni, Vāyu and Āditya, Vāyu is the universal light of the middle world, and he is the one who illumines the whole atmosphere and rules it.

### Printed p. 139

*Running head: [ಅ.೨.ಅ. ೩ ವ. ೬.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 139 (as supplied).*

वेत्थ चत्वारि महांति वेत्थ चत्वारि महतां महांतीत्यग्निर्महांस्तस्य महतो (*vettha catvāri mahāṃti vettha catvāri mahatāṃ mahāṃtītyagnirmahāṃstasya mahato*)
महदोषधयश्च वनस्पतयश्च तद्ध्यस्यान्नं वायुर्महांस्तस्य महतो (*mahadoṣadhayaśca vanaspatayaśca taddhyasyānnaṃ vāyurmahāṃstasya mahato*)
महदापस्तद्ध्यस्यान्नमादित्यो महान् .... .... .... ॥ (*mahadāpastaddhyasyānnamādityo mahān .... .... .... ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 10-3-4-4) *[the last line has printed dots]*

The great things of the world are four: Agni, Vāyu, Āditya and Puruṣa. Among these four great things, for Vāyu, who stands in the atmosphere, the great water is food.

अयं वै वर्षस्येष्टे योऽयं पवते सोऽयमेक इवैव पवते सोऽयं (*ayaṃ vai varṣasyeṣṭe yo 'yaṃ pavate so 'yameka ivaiva pavate so 'yaṃ*)
पुरुषेंतः प्रविष्टः प्राज्ञ प्रत्यज्ज तानिमौ प्राणोदानौ प्राणोदानौ (*puruṣeṃtaḥ praviṣṭaḥ prājña pratyajja tānimau prāṇodānau prāṇodānau*)
वै मित्रावरुणौ तद्य एव वर्षस्येष्टे स त्वा वृष्ट्यावत्त्येवृतदाह तमेतेनैवाददीत यदा ह्येव कदा च वृष्टिः समिव समनक्त्याहुतिमेवैतत्करोत्याहुतिर्भूत्वा देवलोकं गच्छादिति ॥ (*vai mitrāvaruṇau tadya eva varṣasyeṣṭe sa tvā vṛṣṭyāvattyevṛtadāha tametenaivādadīta yadā hyeva kadā ca vṛṣṭiḥ samiva samanaktyāhutimevaitatkarotyāhutirbhūtvā devalokaṃ gacchāditi ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 1-8-3-12) *[the hyphenated line breaks "ತಮೇತೇ-ನೈವಾ" and "ಕರೋ-ತ್ಯಾಹುತಿ" are joined]*

Vāyu, who has entered within the Puruṣa and moves in two parts as prāṇa and udāna, pervades the atmosphere as one alone and is the sole lord of all rain. By the offering of an oblation useful to him he is helped to shed rain. By that rain he protects everyone.

तद्वायव्यं भवति । अयं वै वायुर्योऽयं पवत एष ना इदग्ं सर्वं (*tadvāyavyaṃ bhavati | ayaṃ vai vāyuryo 'yaṃ pavata eṣa nā idagṃ sarvaṃ*)
प्रप्याययति यदिदं किंच वर्षति वृष्टादोषधयो जायंते.... .... .... (*prapyāyayati yadidaṃ kiṃca varṣati vṛṣṭādoṣadhayo jāyaṃte.... .... ....*)
तस्माद्वायव्यं भवति ॥ (*tasmādvāyavyaṃ bhavati ‖*) (ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. 2-6.-3-7) *[reference as printed, with a stop; the middle line has printed dots]*

The falling of rain is by the impulse of Vāyu. It is by the help of Vāyu, who is the cause and the increaser of rain, that plants and the like grow. (Therefore the Śākamedha oblation is to be offered to Vāyu.)

Thus all the śruti sentences say that Vāyu, who is in the atmosphere, is the impeller of the falling of rain; this is made clear here too, and it is shown that the sun's rays are the instrumental causes.

श्वानं—अंतरिक्षे श्वसंतं वायुं । (*śvānaṃ—aṃtarikṣe śvasaṃtaṃ vāyuṃ |*) Vāyu has this name because he has the chief breath in the atmosphere (Ni. 7-29).

संवत्सरे अद्य इदं व्यख्यत (*saṃvatsare adya idaṃ vyakhyata*)—it means that after the rainy season has passed, in the hot season the sun's rays again light up the world free of clouds.

#### Ṛk 14

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> दि॒वा यां॑ति म॒रुतो॒ भूम्या॒ग्निर॒यं वातो॑ अं॒तरि॑क्षेण
> याति ।
> अ॒द्भिर्या॑ति॒ वरु॑णः समु॒द्रैर्यु॒ष्मा इ॒च्छंतः॑ शवसो
> नपातः ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[Variants between Saṃhitā and Pada kept as supplied: the Pada has अत्भिः (*atbhiḥ*), समुद्रॆः (*samudrèḥ*) and शव सः (*śava saḥ*) (with a space) where the Saṃhitā has "ಅದ್ಭಿಃ", "ಸಮುದ್ರೈಃ" and "ಶವಸೋ".]*

> दिवा । यांति । मरुतः। भूम्या । अग्निः । अयं । वातः । अंतरिक्षेण । याति ।
> अत्भिः । याति । वरुणः । समुद्रॆः । युष्मान् । इच्छंतः । शव सः । नपातः ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*[Hyphenated line breaks in the block are joined. The supplied text has "ಆಗ." with a stop for the hyphen at one line end ("ಆಗ.-ಚ್ಛಂತಿ"), kept as supplied, and a stray "॥ ॥ ೬ ॥" at the end of the bhāṣya, kept as supplied. The Pratipadārtha stood inside this block after the garbled label "ಪ್ರಸಿಪದಾರ್ಥ".]*

> हे शवसो वृष्टिकार्यस्य बलस्य नपातो नपातयितार ऋभवः युष्मानिच्छंतो वृष्ट्यनुकूलचिकीर्षया युष्मानेवेच्छंतः सर्वॆ देवा आयांति ।
> कस्मादिति तदुच्यते । मरुतो वृष्ट्यनुकूला देवा दिवा द्युलोकाद्यांति । आग.
> च्छंति ॥ सावेकाच इति तृतीयानिभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥ भूम्या भूमेः सकाशादग्निर्याति । आगच्छति । अयं वातः सर्वदा संचारी वायुरंतरिक्षेण याति । अंतरिक्षादायाति । तथा वरुण आवरको जलाभिमानी देवः समुद्रॆः समुंदनस्वभावैरद्भिः ताभ्य आयाति । यद्वा । पूर्व० वृष्टिसाहाय्यायागता एते देवाः कृतकार्याः संतः पुनर्युष्मान्कामयमानाः स्वस्वदेशं प्रति यांतीत्यर्थः ॥ ॥ ६ ॥

*[Gist of the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (my own short summary): Ṛbhus, sons of strength: desiring you, the Maruts come from the sky, Agni from the earth, Vāyu from the atmosphere, Varuṇa with waters; or they go home.]*

*[The label of the Pratipadārtha is garbled in the supplied text ("ಪ್ರಸಿಪದಾರ್ಥ"); the standard label is inserted, the garbled word kept in kn.md as an italic note, and the text moved out of the bhāṣya block.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

शवसः नपातः (*śavasaḥ napātaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, who urge on the power that takes the form of rain (or, O Ṛbhus, sons of strength); युष्मान् इच्छंतः (*yuṣmān icchaṃtaḥ*)—desiring you; मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts; दिवा (*divā*)—from the heavenly world; यांति (*yāṃti*)—come; अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni; भूम्या (*bhūmyā*)—from the earth (comes); अयं वातः (*ayaṃ vātaḥ*)—this Vāyu; अंतरिक्षेण (*aṃtarikṣeṇa*)—from the atmosphere; याति (*yāti*)—comes; वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—the god Varuṇa; समुद्रॆः (*samudrèḥ*) *[so printed]*—with the waters (of the atmosphere) that have a flowing nature; अद्भिः (*adbhiḥ*)—with waters; याति (*yāti*)—comes.

### Printed p. 142

*Running head: 142 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೨. ಸೂ- ೧೬೧.] (as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ṛbhus, sons of strength, desiring you, the Maruts come from the heavenly world, Agni from the earth, Vāyu from the atmosphere, and Varuṇa also, with the waters that are in the atmosphere.

**Printed English translation**

Sons of Strength, the Maruts, desirous of your coming, advance from the sky : Agni comes (to meet you) from the earth : the wind traverses the firmanent [sic] ; and Varuna comes with undulating waters.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

For the words "yāti" and "yānti" Sāyaṇa has given one meaning by adding the prefix "ā" and another without it. According to the first meaning, the gods named in the ṛk come, to help the shedding of rain, from various directions, desiring the company of the ray. According to the second explanation, since, by the sentence संवत्सर इदमद्या व्यख्यत (*saṃvatsara idamadyā vyakhyata*) of the previous ṛk, the rainy season has ended, all the gods set out for their own appointed places, so as to help the shining of the ray.

श्वानं बस्तो बोधयितारमब्रवीत् । (*śvānaṃ basto bodhayitāramabravīt |*) In the previous ṛk the chief help of Mātariśvan was told. In the present ṛk, for the same work of raining, the collective help of the gods Maruts, Agni, Vāta and Varuṇa is indicated. To know the relation between the fall of rain and these gods, one must know the individual nature of these gods and their mutual relation.

Mātariśvan, Vāyu and Maruts: in the conventional sense now current, these names are well known as mere synonyms of Vāyu. But in the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā these names have different significations. Those significations are examined here only from the point of view of the fall of rain described in the present ṛk.

The Ṛbhus (the rays of the sun) are the means of the fall of rain. Mātariśvan is their helper. Both these divine functions are transformations of Agni in the form of lightning. Since the Ṛbhus are plainly said to be the rays of the sun, there is no reason for doubt there. But as to what the relation is between Mātariśvan, a transformation of Vāyu, and Agni, in some ṛks the identity of the two deities is stated.

### Printed p. 143

*Running head: [ಅ. ೨. ಅ. ೩. ವ. ೬. ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 143 (as supplied).*

तनूनपादुच्यते गर्भ आसुरो नराशंसो भवति यद्विजायते । (*tanūnapāducyate garbha āsuro narāśaṃso bhavati yadvijāyate |*)
मातरिश्वा यदमिमीत मातरि वातस्य सर्गो अभवत्सरीमणि ॥ (*mātariśvā yadamimīta mātari vātasya sargo abhavatsarīmaṇi ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-29-11)

When Agni is in the form of an embryo he is called "tanūnapāt". When he forms and makes manifest his radiance in the atmosphere he is called "Mātariśvan": thus Mātariśvan is said to be a transformation of the Agni that is in the atmosphere.

स मातरिश्वा पुरुवारपुष्टिर्विदद्गातुं तनयाय स्वर्वित् । (*sa mātariśvā puruvārapuṣṭirvidadgātuṃ tanayāya svarvit |*)
विशां गोपा जनिता रोदस्योर्देवा अग्निं धारयन्द्रविणोदां ॥ (*viśāṃ gopā janitā rodasyordevā agniṃ dhārayandraviṇodāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-96-4)

In this, Sāyaṇa explains मातरि सर्वस्य जगतः निर्मातरि अंतरिक्षे श्वसन् वर्तमानः । (*mātari sarvasya jagataḥ nirmātari aṃtarikṣe śvasan vartamānaḥ |*) as the Agni in the atmosphere, who is the maker of the whole world; and Skandasvāmin has explained मातरिश्वसंबंधादग्निरेव मातरिश्वेत्युच्यते (*mātariśvasaṃbaṃdhādagnireva mātariśvetyucyate*) *[the hyphenated line break "ಮಾತರಿ-ಶ್ವೇತ್ಯುಚ್ಯತೇ" is joined]* as: because of connection with Mātariśvan (Vāyu), that is, because Mātariśvan is the cause of the manifestation of Agni, Agni too has the name Mātariśvan.

It was explained earlier that the god Vāyu, who is in the atmosphere and, as explained in the Special Topics of the previous ṛk, is the presiding deity of the atmosphere, is also only a transformation of Agni. The changed state of the Agni in the form of lightning that causes rain is called by names such as Mātariśvan and Vāyu.

वरुणः समुद्रॆरद्भिर्याति (*varuṇaḥ samudrèradbhiryāti*)—in saying that Varuṇa comes with the waters that are in the atmosphere, the mutual relation of water and Varuṇa is also stated. "Samudra" here means the atmosphere. (The explanation of this has been given in great detail earlier.) The relation of Varuṇa to rain will be told later.

In the present ṛk it is explained that the gods beginning with Mātariśvan, joining with Agni in the form of the sun's rays, provide rain at the time of rain and, when that has ended and the year is complete, the shining sun's ray for the world.

**Grammar (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā)**

*Note (the Sanskrit grammar is only transcribed, not explained):* the Kannada analyses दिना (*dinā*) *[so printed, for "ದಿವಾ"]* (Pā. Sū. 6-1-168) and यांति (*yāṃti*) (root या (*yā*)); the analysis of Ṛk 14 is cut off in the supplied text at the end of this window and continues in the next window.
